# Closed Paths: Master Lookup (545+ Approaches)

**SEARCH THIS FILE before proposing any approach.** Use grep/ctrl-F.

Last updated: 2026-04-26 (Sessions 1-68 + S48-fresh + critique-46 + critique-56 + S57 + S60-fresh-crit, 198+ sub-agents)

## Failure Modes
- **C** = Circularity (needs primes to compute primes)
- **E** = Equivalence (reduces to zeta zero sum / explicit formula)
- **I** = Information Loss (smooth approximation loses critical bits)

| Stein's-method finite-x Wasserstein test of D(x)=(π(x)-Li(x))log(x)/√x (ATTACK_VECTORS §C5) | FAIL | E | Plateau detected: W_1(D̂_K, N(μ̂, σ̂²)) ≈ 0.0083 at K=10000 over x∈[10^6,10^7]; does NOT shrink as 1/√K (Z=15.34, inflation 4.5× vs sample-fitted Gaussian control). Pointwise structural origin (S110): D_emp ≈ explicit-formula sum (corr 0.98 at n=1000 zeros) — i.e., E1.5 reasserted. Across 10 sub-windows of [10^6,10^7] (width 0.5 in log10), corr(W_1 emp, W_1 D_th(50 zeros)) = 0.906. Empirical kurt_excess=-0.41 (CI [-0.46,-0.36]) — sub-Gaussian, sourced from arcsine modes cos(γ_k log x − arctan(2γ_k)). FIRST quantitative finite-x Wasserstein bound for π(x)-Li(x); orthogonal to asymptotic Selberg/Hejhal CLT and pointwise discrepancy bounds (Pintz, Korevaar). New EDGE **E1.7** (EVS L; demoted from M by S112 — see below). Reduces structurally to E1.5 explicit formula → joins E7.1/E7.6/E7.11 closure family. **S112 PARTIAL refutation:** the W_1 *magnitude* `c≈0.008` is NOT Riemann-specific. At K=5000, n_modes=50, n_trials=60 per family, D_emp's W_1=0.00863 is indistinguishable from random-phase Riemann (z=-0.93), random-phase non-Riemann uniform[10,145] (z=-1.26), and random-phase non-Riemann equispaced (z=-1.55). The "indistinguishable from random-phase variant" test originally cited (S108 z=-1.06) is a generic oscillatory-sum property, not a Riemann signature. §C5 verbatim criterion clause "ties to a specific zeta-zero contribution" fails; A→B demotion. Riemann-rand-phase ENSEMBLE vs non-Riemann ENSEMBLE is KS-distinguishable at p<10⁻⁴, but a single empirical observation lacks resolving power. **S113 further PARTIAL:** the plateau is universal across non-Gaussian distributions, not even oscillatory-sum-specific. 9-distribution test (uniform, single arcsine, 50-arcsine sums w/ unit & 1/√k weights, two-Gaussian mixture, t df=10, Laplace, analytic low-zero sum, Gaussian control) at K∈{200..10000}, n_trials=30: every distribution with non-zero kurtosis plateaus (K=200/K=10000 ratio < 2 vs Stein-CLT prediction 7.07); pure Gaussian decays as 1/√K (ratio 6.42 ≈ √50). Plateau magnitude W_1/σ tracks |kurtosis| monotonically across the 9 families. Linear interp at kurt=-0.41 (D_emp's kurtosis) predicts W_1/σ ≈ 0.042; observed W_1/σ = 0.038 — within 10%. Conclusion: the W_1 plateau is a generic kurtosis-driven W_1(P, N(μ_P, σ_P²)) measurement, fully predicted by D's kurtosis under log-uniform x. The cross-domain Stein technique import survives but is not load-bearing for the conclusion. See `experiments/analytic/stein_wasserstein_pi/stein_wasserstein_pi_results.md`, `experiments/analytic/stein_wasserstein_pi/test_plateau_universality_results.md`, `novel/finite_x_wasserstein_plateau.md`, `archive/sessions/session112_verify_c5_stein.md`, `archive/sessions/session113_verify_c5_stein.md`. **S114 verify-6 PARTIAL:** S108's W_1=0.00829 reproduces to 0.3% under three independent W_1 implementations (mid-rank quantile, scipy MC Gaussian reference, CDF-integral); S108 number is solid. SEPARATELY, S113's "kurtosis fully predicts W_1/σ within 10%" claim REFUTED on Beta(α,α) at analytically-tuned α=5.817 to give kurt=-0.41 (matching D_emp): Beta gives W_1/σ = 0.0328 vs S113-predicted 0.0426 vs D_emp 0.0376 (gaps -23% / -13%). S113 universality is qualitative (any non-Gaussian distribution plateaus) but NOT quantitatively kurtosis-only. S108 grade stays B. **S115 verify-7 CONFIRM:** the sub-window correlation r=0.906 (S108 structural-matching argument, originally cited as evidence of low-zero structural origin) IS Riemann-φ-specific. 200-trial random-phase null preserving the same Riemann γ_k gives mean r = -0.04 ± 0.39 (matches n=10 noise floor 1/√n ≈ 0.316), with 0/200 trials reaching r ≥ 0.906 (one-sided p < 0.005, z=+2.44 above null mean). Random-phase + non-Riemann γ ∈ [10,145] and pure-noise controls give statistically identical distributions (means within 0.02, stds within 0.01). The actual zero phases are necessary AND sufficient to reproduce V_emp = (sub-window W_1 vector) pointwise — exactly E1.5 (Riemann's explicit formula). Structural-matching headline survives, but its mathematical content reduces to E1.5 already in project. S108 grade stays B. Verify chain saturated after 7 sessions; parser fix applied to S108 self-grade line so run.sh rotates back to production. | Pfaffian / α-determinantal point process structure of ζ zeros at order n=4 (ATTACK_VECTORS §C6) | FAIL | E | First project measurement of GOE/GSE Pfaffian and Vere-Jones α-DPP point-process models on ζ-zero R_4 at order n=4 (8000 unfolded zeros, 96 random non-equally-spaced 4-tuples in [0.5, 6.0], window-counting at tol=0.20). **B-grade structural negative on Pfaffian:** χ²/dof discrimination (dof=96, per-tuple SE=0.135 from GUE-MC batches) gives ζ vs sine-kernel det theory **0.96** (perfect fit), ζ vs GUE-MC pool **1.24**, ζ vs GOE-MC pool **1.99** (rejects GOE), ζ vs GSE-MC pool **3.11** (strongly rejects GSE). Decisive structural rejection: GOE Pfaffian and GSE Pfaffian models do NOT fit ζ-zero R_4 at order 4, while sine-kernel determinantal model fits within noise. **B+ partial-positive on α-DPP shift:** α-determinant scan over α ∈ [−1.5, −0.5] at Δα=0.025 finds α\*(zeta) = **−1.10** with L2\* = 0.114 vs L2(α=−1) = 0.118 (ΔL2 = 0.0045); tuple-bootstrap (200 resamples) 95% CI [−1.150, −1.049] formally excludes α=−1. **Critical bias control:** 16 independent matched-size 8000-eigenvalue GUE-MC trials with same 96 tuples + same α-DPP scan: α\* mean = **−1.013**, std = 0.035, 0/16 trials at α\* ≤ −1.10. ζ at α\* = −1.10 is **z ≈ −2.5σ** below GUE-MC distribution (parametric one-sided p = 0.007; non-parametric Laplace p = 0.059). The α-DPP shift is suggestive but does NOT meet the 5σ A-grade bar. | §D.D34 ATTACK_VECTORS — De Branges Hilbert space `H(E_xi)` finite-rank reproducing-kernel projection of `chi_[0,x]` onto `H_N` (cross-domain: de Branges Hilbert spaces of entire functions / Hermite-Biehler model spaces; FIRST de Branges import in project, CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES §10 "Riemann xi-function model spaces" promoted PROPOSED → USED E) | FAIL | E | **WILD-SWING SESSION attempting A-grade frontier target.** D34 asks whether the de Branges projection error of the indicator decays at polylog rate `O((log N)^{-c})`, which would deliver a polylog Lagarias-Odlyzko substitute via de Branges geometry — one of the four major attempted approaches to RH. **Modelling reduction (Lagarias 2007 §3):** the de Branges projection of `d psi(x)` onto `H_N` agrees, up to smooth correction, with the N-truncation of the Riemann explicit formula `psi_N(x) = x - 2 Re sum_{n=1}^N x^{rho_n}/rho_n - log 2pi - (1/2) log(1 - x^{-2})`; the projection-error rate question reduces to the explicit-formula truncation rate. **Method:** N up to 8000 zeros (project's existing `data/zeta_zeros_8000.txt`); `psi(x)` exact via segmented Eratosthenes sieve; `psi_N(x)` evaluated at 30 log-uniform x samples per octave anchored at x ∈ {10^4, 10^5, 10^6, 10^7}; RMS error across the 30-x grid; GUE control via synthetic ordinates with sine-kernel (Wigner-surmise β=2) spacings + Riemann-von Mangoldt mean density. **Headline result (B-grade structural):** prime-zero RMS projection error decays as `rms_N(x) ≈ 1.2 sqrt(x) · N^{-0.35±0.04}` with R² = 0.96 across all four x-anchors — squarely inside the classical explicit-formula truncation regime; **GUE control gives ZERO decay**, projection error stable at `~0.30 sqrt(x)` independent of N (R² ≈ 0.6–0.9 against no-decay null). **Falsifiers (4 pre-stated):** F1 (no-decay null) REFUTED for primes / CONFIRMED for GUE; F2 (`N^{-1/2}` classical) CONSISTENT (slope -0.35 with log correction at N ≤ 8000 collinearity); F3 (polylog A-grade) NOT REFUTED but NOT CONFIRMED (polylog and power-law fits indistinguishable on N ∈ [30, 8000]; disambiguation requires N ≥ 10^6 zeros from Bober-Hiary 2018 LMFDB — predicts factor 2.3 in rms ratio, decidable with current pipeline given larger zero list); F4 (prime ≈ GUE) REFUTED (15× ratio at N=8000). **Closure mode E:** subsumes into E1.5 (explicit formula); de Branges Hermite-Biehler reframing adds no acceleration over classical truncation rate. **Joins family of major-RH-approach polylog-frontier closures:** Connes-Bost (S203, S141, S140, S202), Galway frontier (S195+S196), de Branges (THIS SESSION). Three of the four major RH approaches now have explicit-formula-rate-equivalent closures of their polylog frontiers; only the pair-correlation programme retains an open frontier (extensively explored in commit threads 1, 3, 5, all closed). **Partial-positive observation (NOT promoted to new edge):** the 15× prime-vs-GUE projection-error ratio at fixed N=8000 (constant 0.058 ± 0.005 across x ∈ [10^6, 10^7]) is a NEW quantitative discrimination, but structurally absorbs into E1.5 + E7.1 — it restates "the explicit formula's log-density-bias content survives the de Branges projection" in geometric language; honest-failure clause prevents inflating it to a new edge. **Cross-domain refs:** de Branges 1968 *Hilbert Spaces of Entire Functions* (Prentice-Hall); Lagarias 2007 *The Riemann hypothesis: arithmetic and geometry* = arXiv:math/0511099 §3; Davenport 2000 *Multiplicative Number Theory* (3rd ed.) ch. 18 (classical truncation bound); Selberg 1942 *Arch. Math. Naturvid.* (`O(sqrt(x log x / N))` original); Burnol 2002 *C. R. Acad. Sci. Paris* 333 (de Branges-Krein for ζ). **Cites** E1.5, E5.7, E7.1, E7.18 (S133 FHK convergence-rate edge). | Möbius-side parity bisection of π(x) (paradigm-shift mode, no external technique) | PARTIAL | C | **C-grade refinement of E2.2 (DEMOTED from self-graded B by S428 critique — agent's own self-grade-defense paragraph offered C-demotion as defensible: "textbook-level identity rearrangement", "below the published-paper threshold", bridge identity has one-line Dirichlet-convolution proof, 4-cell decomposition is near-tautological algebraic rearrangement; per CLAUDE.md "Self-grade DOWN, not up, when in doubt" rule).** Composes E2.2 (Liouville bisection π=(x−L)/2−C_3) with the standard Möbius rearrangement Q−M = 2·S_o, S_o = #{n≤x sqfree, ω odd}. Defines C_3*(x) := #{n≤x sqfree, composite, ω odd, ω≥3}. Identity verified integer-exact ∀x∈[1,10⁶]: **π(x)=(Q(x)−M(x))/2−C_3*(x)** (F1) and **NS_o(x)=(x−Q−L+M)/2** with NS_o:=#{not sqfree, Ω odd} (F2, bridge identity); one-line proof Σμ²λ=Σμ=M. Consequence: **C_3(x)−C_3*(x)=NS_o(x)** (F3). 4-cell decomposition (squarefree × Ω-parity) closed-form: S_e=(Q+M)/2, S_o=(Q−M)/2, NS_e=((x−Q)+(L−M))/2, NS_o=(x−Q−L+M)/2. Empirical Möbius-side parity bisection inherits E1.6's near-independence: I(B(x) mod 2 ; C_3*(x) mod 2) ≈ 1.15·10⁻⁵ bits at N=10⁶, same order as I(A;C_3) ≈ 2·10⁻⁵. Falsifiers F1–F6 ALL PASS (zero failures dict at N=10⁶). **Why C**: (Q−M)/2 ~ 0.304·x dominated by C_3* ~ 0.304·x with π~x/log x as needle; computing C_3*(x) to π(x)-precision reduces to counting squarefree k-almost-primes which itself reduces to π in arithmetic progressions (E3.4 obstruction). No polylog opening. Algorithmic: same x^{2/3} via M(x). **What's new**: the Möbius bisection itself, the bridge identity (one-line proof), the 4-cell decomposition, and the empirical Möbius-side parity-independence — none of these were recorded in EDGES.md or CLOSED_PATHS.md before. NO external technique imported (paradigm-shift discipline). Composes: E2.2, E1.6, E2.10, folklore Möbius `Q−M=2·S_o`. See `experiments/constructions/mobius_bisection_of_pi/`, `archive/sessions/session427_mobius_bisection.md`. | 427 |

| AHK matroid Hodge log-concavity of arithmetic prime-divisibility transversal matroid (ATTACK_VECTORS §D31) | FAIL | I | B-grade mode I closure. Built the **arithmetic transversal matroid** `M_P^N` (ground = `[2, N]`, blocks `B_p = {n in [2, N] : p | n}` for primes `p ≤ N`, rank = max bipartite matching to blocks); computed characteristic polynomial via Whitney expansion `χ_M(t) = Σ_{S ⊆ E} (−1)^{|S|} t^{r(M)−r(S)}` over all `2^{|E|}` subsets at `N ∈ {8, 12, 16, 20}`. AHK 2018 *Annals* 188 = arXiv:1511.02888 log-concavity inequality `|w_k|² ≥ |w_{k−1}|·|w_{k+1}|` verified empirically across ~200 matroids (zero violations, sanity check). Compared the AHK slack `δ_k := |w_k|² − |w_{k−1}|·|w_{k+1}|` of the prime matroid to **degree-preserving configuration-model controls** (matching bipartite degree sequence on both sides via stub-pairing). At `N = 20` (50 controls), prime `|w_k|` values exceed config-model means by 3-4×, with z-scores `+2.55σ to +3.44σ` on individual coefficients and `+2.85σ to +5.61σ` on slack. **W-trick attenuation**: `W = 2` (odd integers, odd primes only) reduces deviation to a uniform `+3.17σ` across all coefficients (variance is 1-dimensional in control ensemble); `W = 6` collapses the matroid to free `U_{6,6}` for `N ≤ 20` (need `N ≥ p²` for `p ≥ 5` for non-trivial test). **Direct-sum decomposition**: `M_P^N = M_conn^N ⊕ U_{1,1}^{ν(N)}` where `ν(N) = π(N) − π(N/2)` (Bertrand prime count) and the `U_{1,1}` factors are isolated edges from primes `p ∈ (N/2, N]` (each appears only as itself in `[2, N]`). The `(t-1)^{ν(N)}` factor inflates `|w_k|`; config-model destroys these isolated edges by stub-pairing single-stub right-nodes. **D31.a sub-experiment** (CLOSED in S149): re-ran on connected part `M_conn^N` only (50 controls); deviation reduces by `0.5–1.0σ` but **does not erase**: conn-only `δ_1` z = `+3.91σ` at `N = 20`, individual `|w_k|` z = `+2.41σ to +2.88σ`. Bertrand factor accounts for ~50% of full-matroid deviation; ~50% is residual structure of `M_conn^N` not yet identified analytically (plausibly tied to multiplicative-coincidence 4-cycle structure `Σ_{p<q ≤ √N} (N/(pq))²`). Connected-part char polys (rank 4): `χ_{M_conn^{16}} = 20 − 42t + 30t² − 9t³ + t⁴`, `χ_{M_conn^{20}} = 36 − 70t + 44t² − 11t³ + t⁴`. Pre-stated falsifiers: (E) `±2σ` after W-trick — **NOT achieved**; (I) `≥3σ` deviation, structurally identified — **achieved (+5.6σ at k=1 full, +3.9σ at k=1 conn)**, half-traced to Bertrand; (A) closed-form HL-product `χ_{M_conn^N}` — not pursued (D31.b open). New shape edge **E2.24**: 7th orthogonal pseudorandomness-detection category after Gowers / Anderson / DPP / PH / subword / Mahler / Newman; structurally distinct from E7.17 graph-Laplacian Hodge L_1 (S126, D22) — Chow ring `A^*(M)` and Hodge-Riemann form on graded pieces vs. Hodge Laplacian eigenvalue spectrum. Cross-domain technique: **AHK matroid Hodge theory / Heron-Rota-Welsh log-concavity** (Adiprasito-Huh-Katz 2018 arXiv:1511.02888; Heron 1972 / Rota 1971 / Welsh 1976; Brändén-Huh 2020 *Annals* 192 Lorentzian polynomials = arXiv:1902.03719; Huh 2012 *J. AMS* 25; Edmonds-Fulkerson 1965 transversal matroids). PROPOSED → USED I, edge E2.24. Channelled mathematician: Huh / Adiprasito (combinatorial Hodge). Two open follow-ups (D31.b/c): (b) `χ_{M_conn^N}` HL-product fingerprint at `N ∈ {32, 48, 64}` via deletion-contraction + matroid representation matrix, fit `|w_k|^? = ∏_{p ≤ N^{α_k}} f(p)`; (c) AHK Hodge applied to the χ_P-driven flag matroid of the coprimality complex (S126 / E7.17 closure), structurally distinct matroid class (Björner-Wachs 2007 *Adv. Math.* 207 shellability + Stanley-Reisner ring). See `experiments/algebraic/d31_ahk_matroid_hodge/`, `archive/sessions/session149_d31_ahk_matroid_hodge.md`. | 149 |
| Cohn-Elkies / Delsarte LP bound on chi_P (ATTACK_VECTORS §D29) | FAIL | E | B-grade negative-shape closure (mode E). Wild-swing single-session attempt at the discrete-Z Cohn-Elkies / Delsarte-LP density bound (Cohn-Elkies 2003 *Annals* 157 = arXiv:math/0110009; Viazovska 2017 *Annals* 185 = arXiv:1603.04246; framework re-cast as Delsarte 1973 / Schrijver 2005 SDP arXiv:math/0405348). LP: maximize f_hat(0)=f(0)+2 Σ f(t) subject to f_hat(xi)>=0 (Bochner, M=4096 sample points), f(0)=1, Σ_{t≥1} g(t) f(t) ≤ 0 with g=R_P(t)/π(N) (autocorrelation). Bound: π(N)/N ≤ 1/f_hat^*(0). At N∈{10^4,10^5,10^6}, T_max=500: 1/f_hat^*(g_obs)∈{0.147, 0.158, 0.158} while ρ=π(N)/N∈{0.123, 0.096, 0.078} — saturation S_N=ρ·f_hat^*(0) decays as 0.835→0.608→0.498 (S_N → 0 like 1/log N, vacuous in the limit). T_max sweep at N=10^6, T∈{50,100,200,400,800,1500} gives clean log-linear fit f_hat^*(0; g_obs) ≈ 1.154 + 0.848·log T (residuals all |.|<0.1, R²>0.999). Implication: matching ρ~1/log N at N=10^6 requires log T~13.66, T~e^13.66~8.6·10^5 ≈ N — i.e. **the LP becomes tight only at T_max ≈ N, super-polylog**. For any T_max=polylog(N) the bound is loose by factor log N. Optimal f^* is NOT a Viazovska modular form: residue-mod-4 breakdown gives sum f^*(t)|_{t≡0} ≈ +20.6, sum f^*(t)|_{t≡2} ≈ -20.2, sum f^*(t)|_{t≡1,3} ≈ +0.27 — a period-4 sinusoid f^*(t) ≈ A·cos(πt/2)·1_{t even} reflecting the parity barrier (E2.1). f_hat^* concentrates 38× at xi=1/4 over xi=0. Strictly inside sieve barrier (E6.7). Two A-grade criteria (LP saturation + modular f^*) both ruled out cleanly. New negative-shape edge **E2.23**: "Cohn-Elkies/Delsarte LP optimum scales as f_hat^*(0)=O(log T) for prime autocorrelation; loose by factor log N for any T=polylog(N)." Cross-domain technique: linear-programming bounds for codes / sphere packing (Delsarte 1973; Cohn-Elkies 2003 arXiv:math/0110009; Viazovska 2017 arXiv:1603.04246; Bachoc-Vallentin 2008 arXiv:math/0608426; Schrijver 2005 arXiv:math/0405348) — UNUSED before S145, promoted PROPOSED → USED mode E. See `experiments/analytic/cohn_elkies_chi_p/`, `archive/sessions/session145_d29_cohn_elkies_chi_p.md`. | 145 |

Greedy random elementary collapse run on H_N for N ∈ {64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192} with 20 seeds each (200 seeds at N ∈ {64, 256, 1024} for determinism test). Findings: **(F1)** m_0(H_N) = Θ(N) — explicitly m_0(H_N)/N ∈ {0.844, 0.859, 0.895, 0.906, 0.920, 0.930, 0.935} at the listed N (monotone increasing toward 1), no polylog compression. Falsifier 1 (linear scaling) and Falsifier 2 (matches ER baseline within 2%) BOTH triggered. **(F2)** Greedy random Morse output is *deterministic* across all 200 seeds at every tested N — distinct (m_0, m_1) outputs = 1. By contrast, ER(N, |E_div|) baseline produces 5–30 m_0-variance per N. The divisibility lattice exhibits a structural Morse-rigidity not present in matched-density random graphs (consistent with Bjorner EL-shellability of (Z, |), but a sharper empirical observation: greedy elementary collapse on the 1-skeleton converges to a unique critical-cell count regardless of order). **(F3)** Sharp closed-form identity, verified to N=8192: collapses(N) = (π(N) − π(N/2)) + Π_pow(N) + ε(N), where π(N) − π(N/2) = #(primes in (N/2, N]), Π_pow(N) := #{p^k : p prime, k ≥ 2, N/2 < p^k ≤ N} = Σ_{k≥2} (π(N^{1/k}) − π((N/2)^{1/k})) ~ Θ(√N/log N), and ε(N) ≡ 1 across all measured N. **Why E**: the discrete-Morse approach REDUCES π(N) to a primes-in-interval count (π(N) − π(N/2)), no easier than the original problem — circular structural reduction. The greedy upper bound is `m_0 ≈ N − N/(2 ln N) − Θ(√N/ln N) + O(1)`. The Morse inequality m_0 ≥ b_0 = 1 leaves room for non-greedy improvement, but ω(n) ≥ 2 cycles in H_N prevent simple peeling, and any optimal-matching encoding of χ_P would require evaluating π(N) in the dual cohomology basis (circular by F3). Closes companion path to D14 (cellular sheaves on same poset, S103) — both order-based topological probes give no compression. CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md row "Discrete Morse theory": PROPOSED → USED-E (S232). New negative-shape edge candidate (E2.X TBD): discrete-Morse 1-skeleton complexity of (Z, |) ∩ [1, N] reduces to π(N) − π(N/2). See `experiments/topological/d17_discrete_morse_divisibility/d17_discrete_morse_divisibility_results.md`, `archive/sessions/session232_d17_discrete_morse_divisibility.md`. | 232 |
| **C11 / D17.b — Discrete-Morse 1-skeleton complexity of the SQUAREFREE-only divisibility Hasse diagram H_N^sqfree (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES.md §1 C11)** | FAIL | E | **B-grade refinement of D17 / S232.** Composes E2.28 (Baker-Norine on the same poset) + E1.6 / E2.10 (parity-bisection wheel limit) + D17 (full-lattice identity). Vertices V_sqfree(N) = {n ≤ N : μ(n) ≠ 0}, |V_sqfree(N)| = (6/π²)N + O(√N); edges = (m, mp) with m, mp squarefree, p prime, p ∤ m, mp ≤ N. Greedy random Morse collapse run at N ∈ {64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192} with 20 seeds + 20 ER baselines each, 200 seeds determinism test at N ∈ {64, 256, 1024, 4096}. **(F1) Polylog A-grade FAILS** — m_0(H_N^sqfree)/|V| ∈ {0.821, 0.833, 0.854, 0.863, 0.880, 0.890, 0.898, 0.907} monotone increasing toward 1. **(F3) Sharp two-term identity HOLDS exactly** with no residual: collapses(H_N^sqfree) = π(N) − π(N/2), ε(N) ≡ 0 across every measured N (vs D17's ε(N) ≡ 1). The squarefree restriction REMOVES BOTH the Π_pow(N) prime-power term AND the constant chained-collapse +1, leaving exactly π(N) − π(N/2). **Two-line analytical proof** (vs D17's empirical-only): vertex-degree case-analysis shows initial leaves are exactly primes p ∈ (N/2, N] (count = π(N) − π(N/2)); each leaf's only edge is to vertex 1; after peeling, deg(1) drops from π(N) to π(N/2) ≥ 2 for N ≥ 6; no other vertex's degree changes; cascade halts in one wave. Order-independence is automatic (leaves mutually non-adjacent), so Morse rigidity holds analytically (sharper than D17's empirical claim). **(F2) ER baseline match FAILS** at amplified gap — divisibility-Hasse is ≈ 4 % more collapsible than matched-density ER (vs D17's 0.5–2 %). The squarefree restriction *amplifies* the structural distinguishability of divisibility-poset Morse complexity from random graphs of the same density. **(F4) Morse rigidity HOLDS** — single (m_0, m_1) output across 200 seeds at every N. **Why E**: same circular reduction as D17 — m_0 reduces exactly to |V_sqfree(N)| − (π(N) − π(N/2)), where |V_sqfree(N)| is polylog-evaluable but π(N) − π(N/2) is no easier than π(N). The Boolean-lattice shellability hope for the truncated cube graph is empty: the 1-skeleton has no degree-1 vertices except at the top truncation, so shellability lives one dimension up. Successors: D17.b.i full order complex (chain-level m_0, A-grade still on the table), D17.b.ii multiplicative-indicator generalisation (k-free, k-almost-primes), D17.b.iii Lean formalisation. | Boolean Fourier low-degree structure | FAIL | - | 30% excess low-deg weight = parity/mod-4 only; noise sensitivity near-random; no junta structure | 17 |
| Communication matrix rank shortcut | FAIL | - | rank(pi_N) = 2^{N/2-1}+2 exactly; converges to 50% of max; no efficient 2-party protocol | 17 |
| Partition generating function sum | FAIL | C | Sum [f(n)=0] has no GF shortcut; indicator of zeros doesn't factor through GFs | 17 |
| MacMahonesque generalizations | FAIL | C | Higher M_a also circular + more expensive; polynomials in n with periodic corrections | 17 |
| Additive number theory encoding | FAIL | - | Ono shows additive characterization exists but counting is equally hard from both viewpoints | 17 |

## Session 18: Streaming / Space Complexity

| Approach | Verdict | Mode | Key Finding | Session |
|----------|---------|------|-------------|---------|
| Streaming exact pi(x) | FAIL | E | Sieve-based streaming needs Omega(sqrt(x)/log(x)) space (must encode primes up to sqrt(x)); one-way CC from rank gives same bound; no non-sieve streaming algorithm known | 18 |
| Online p(n) with small state | PARTIAL | - | O(log n) state suffices (just store last prime, test next candidates with AKS); but time = O(n log n), catastrophic. Space is NOT the bottleneck | 18 |
| Space-time tradeoff for pi(x) (general) | OPEN | - | Communication complexity gives T*S >= Omega(log^2 x) only -- too weak to rule out polylog(x). Proving T >= x^{Omega(1)} requires circuit lower bounds (Natural Proofs barrier). OBDD model: Omega(sqrt(x)) proven. M-L DAG pebbling: T*S >= Omega(x^{5/6}/ln x) algorithm-specific. Nechiporuk: Omega(log x) trivial. See experiments/circuit_complexity/space_time_tradeoff_results.md | 20 |
| Space-time tradeoff via comm complexity | CLOSED | E | D(pi) = Theta(N/2) bits, giving T*S >= Omega(N^2) = Omega(log^2 x). This is polylog(x), so communication complexity CANNOT rule out polylog-time algorithms. The input has only N=log(x) bits, bounding D(f) <= N, so comm-complexity-based tradeoffs are inherently poly(N). | 20 |
| Space-time tradeoff via Nechiporuk | CLOSED | E | Nechiporuk formula bound uses rank(M_pi, k-bit) = 2^{k-1}+2 but optimal block size s*=3 gives only L(pi) >= Omega(N) = Omega(log x). Method inherently limited to O(N^2) for ANY function. | 20 |
| Space-time via OBDD/BDD size | PARTIAL | E | OBDD size ~ 2^{0.79*N} empirically (N=4..12); matches random functions. OBDD width >= 2^{N/2-1} proven from comm rank. But general BPs can be exponentially smaller than OBDDs. | 20 |
| Space-time via M-L DAG pebbling | CLOSED | E | Lucy DP DAG: depth=pi(sqrt(x)), width=O(sqrt(x)). Pebbling gives T*S >= Omega(x^{5/6}/ln x) but ONLY for Meissel-Lehmer; a non-sieve algorithm could bypass this DAG. | 20 |
| Branching program width for pi(x) | FAIL | E | Nechiporuk bound gives BP size >= 2^{N/2} = sqrt(x) from rank computation; confirms no NC^1 shortcut via BPs | 18 |

| Approach | Verdict | Mode | Key Finding | Session |
|----------|---------|------|-------------|---------|
| Unbalanced communication complexity | FAIL | - | rank = 2^{min(k,N-k)-1}+2 for ALL bit partitions k=1..N-1 (verified N=4..20). No polynomial-rank partition exists. Barrier is intrinsic. | 19 |
| 3-party NOF communication complexity | PARTIAL | - | Balanced (N/3,N/3,N/3): max cut rank = 2^{N/3}, NOF = N/3. Best (1,1,N-2): rank = 4 (trivial). Consistent with TC^0 but insufficient to prove/disprove. | 19 |
| k-party NOF (k=2..8) mode-unfolding rank | CLOSED | - | For k>=3: ALL mode-unfoldings have FULL RANK = 2^{sizes[i]}. Max rank = 2^{ceil(N/k)} exactly. Verified N=6..18, k=2..8 (35+ cases). For k=2: rank = 2^{N/2-1}+2 (sub-full). GF(p) rank identical for p=2,3,5,7. SVD: rank-1 dominates (99.9% variance). Residual (pi-smooth) also full rank. Mode-unfolding rank is too coarse to resolve ACC^0 question -- need tensor rank, discrepancy, or polynomial method. | 20 |
| SVD spectral decay of oscillatory part | FAIL | I | Power-law S~i^{-1} (not geometric). 90% osc variance in ~20 SVs but 99% needs ~30% of total. Max osc SV scales as x^{0.66}. Insufficient for exact computation. | 19 |
| SVD ↔ zeta zero correspondence | CONFIRMED | E | Top osc SVs match zeta zeros (corr 0.95 at N=20 for gamma_1). Zeta basis explains 0.12% of variance at N=20 (need more zeros for larger N). SVD IS explicit formula decomposition. | 19 |
| PSLQ/LLL identity search for f(x)=pi(x)-R(x) | FAIL | I | All 6 relation types tested (linear, polynomial deg 2-4, recurrence, modular, functional, discrete derivative) x=2..10000. All single-point PSLQ relations spurious (cross-validation residuals ~10^4). Fourier confirms zeta-zero dominance. | 19 |
| NFS-type L[1/3] for pi(x) (4 sub-approaches) | FAIL | E+C | Norm sieve = residue class counting (E); Chebotarev = circular + O(x^{1/2}) error (C+I); class groups = Euler products (E); Artin L-fns = explicit formula (E). NFS exploits multiplicative structure; pi(x) is additive-global. | 19 |
| Gap-based predictability for pi(x) | FAIL | I | AR(1..50) gives NO improvement over baseline. MI(g_n;g_{n+1})=0.38 bits (10.3%). Gaps 9% more compressible than i.i.d. Near Cramér random model. | 19 |
| Kt complexity of delta(n) empirical | FAIL | I | |delta|~n^{0.57}; bit ratio 0.52; AR(1) R²=0.996 but RMSE=10.5 (innovations random); uniform mod m; 18% compressible; sign run length 38.5 (zeta oscillation); no exploitable structure beyond smoothness | 19 |
| Fast-forwardable dynamical system on gaps | FAIL | I | AR(1..50) R^2<0 (worse than mean). Residue-conditional (g_n, p_n mod m) R^2<0.05. HMM(2-8 states) = baseline. MI(g_n;g_{n+1})=0.35 bits (8.9%). LZ complexity 71% of random. Correlation dim grows with embedding (no attractor). No substitution/morphism structure. p(n) mod m equidistributed, no autocorrelation. | 20 |

## Summary Statistics

- **Total approaches tested:** 641+
- **FAIL:** ~452 (confirmed impossible or impractical)
- **PARTIAL:** ~19 (works partially but doesn't meet target)
- **WORKS:** ~8 (correct but O(x^{2/3}) or worse, or TC^0 but conditional)
- **EXISTS:** ~3 (formulas exist but computationally useless)
- **OPEN:** 5 (circuit complexity TC^0/NC, BPSW correctness, QFT deterministic, #TC^0⊆NC?, S*T tradeoff)

### By Failure Mode
- **Circularity (C):** ~82 approaches
- **Equivalence (E):** ~222 approaches
- **Information Loss (I):** ~180 approaches

Promise version (coprime to 2,3,5) reduces by only 1-2 degrees. Quantum lower bound: Q(chi_P) >= N/4 queries. SOS degree = adeg. PARITY-like, not MAJORITY-like. See experiments/circuit_complexity/approx_degree_prime_results.md | 28 |

| CRT Prime Locator (progression counting) | FAIL | C | π(x;q,a) as hard as π(x). L-function zeros same barrier. Only 4-5 CRT moduli needed but each requires full prime counting | 24 |

| Prime gap linear recurrence | FAIL | I | R² negative for all orders 1-20 (worse than mean predictor). Next gap ≈ 10±8 regardless of history | 24 |
| k-automatic prime indicator | FAIL | I | 2-kernel has 38 growing subsequences (vs 6 for Thue-Morse). NOT k-automatic for any k | 24 |
| LFSR encoding over finite fields | FAIL | I | L/N = 0.5000 over ALL GF(p) tested (p=2..23). Maximally random-like in every field | 24 |

| Multiplicative Fourier / characters | FAIL | E | L(1,χ) polylog via digamma but gives density not count. Summing L-function zeros same barrier | 24 |

| Tensor network (MPS) Legendre sieve contraction | FAIL | I | Bond dim ~ 2^(0.33-0.43 * a), exponential in #primes. Sieve 5-20x more structured than random but still exponential. Dominant SV is rank-1 (floor(x)), corrections full-rank. O(sqrt(x)) unique floors don't help: non-local assignment to bipartitions. Confirms S10/S20 | 26 |
| Explicit BDD circuit synthesis for pi(x) | PARTIAL | E | ROBDD with multi-ordering: LSB BDD ~ 2^(0.73*N). Better than OBDD 2^(0.79*N) (S20) but worse than sqrt = 2^(0.5*N). Per-bit: MSB~N+1 (trivial), LSB~2^(0.73*N). Influence(LSB)~N/2. BDDs are restricted (branching programs); general circuits could be exponentially smaller. Does NOT prove circuit lower bound. | 28 |
| Ramanujan Library PSLQ on delta(n)=p(n)-R^{-1}(n) | FAIL | I | PSLQ on n=1..2000, validated n=2001..2500. Linear recurrence orders 2-20: all relations unique (spurious). Polynomial deg 2-3 in consecutive deltas: all unique. Mixed delta(n) vs log(n)/sqrt(n): all unique. Modular: brute-force mod 2 order-4 gives 70% but trivial predictor gives 92% (autocorrelation artifact). Berlekamp-Massey: LFSR length L=N/2 for all mod 2,3,5,7,11 (maximally complex). Differenced delta same. Entropy 6.26 bits. Confirms algebraic independence of delta sequence | 26 |
| Group-theoretic cross-modulus prime race shortcut | FAIL | E+C | E(x;q) correlations between moduli are trend artifacts (QR/NQR imbalance); oscillatory parts independent (roughness ratio 0.997). Galois lifts go wrong direction (fine->coarse free, coarse->fine needs new zeros). Linear prediction of p(n) mod q from E(x;q_i): R^2 < 0.03, MI < 3% of max. Character orthogonality makes cross-modulus shortcuts mathematically impossible. CRT via prime races costs 96*O(sqrt(x)) for q<=23, worse than direct pi(x) | 26 |
| Wheel decomposition circuit complexity | FAIL | I | Per-class pi_r(x) circuits 3-4x MORE complex (normalized transitions) than full pi(x). Classes near-independent (I/H < 0.01), preventing shortcuts. Total entropy grows as phi(M). Entropy reduction from mixed-radix conditioning is finite-size effect (shrinks as N grows). Decomposition destroys sequential regularity that makes pi(x) partially structured | 28 |
| Multiplicative-additive circuit structure (Legendre I-E cancellation) | FAIL | I | Signed matrix rank = #distinct floors (full rank, no reduction). 90% of floor values have nonzero net I-E contribution. Effective terms O(sqrt(x)) but no way to evaluate sum sublinearly | 28 |
| Carry propagation structure in pi(x) sum | FAIL | I | Carry chain distribution IDENTICAL to random Bernoulli(1/ln x). Max carry = O(log pi(x)) = O(N). No exploitable additive structure | 28 |
| Monochromatic rectangle partition of pi(x) | FAIL | I | Partition number ~ 2^(0.76*N), exceeding rank ~ 2^(0.41*N). Nondeterministic communication complexity exponential. partition/rank ratio grows as 2^(0.35*N) | 28 |
| Per-bit complexity gradient analysis | DIAGNOSTIC | I | LSB-half influence 2x MSB-half, ratio growing with N. Bit 0 influence ~ N/2, max sensitivity = N. MSB influence O(1). Crossover at bit N/2. All CONSISTENT with polylog circuits | 28 |
| Approximate degree of chi_P at rounding threshold | DIAGNOSTIC | -- | adeg(chi_P, 0.49) = ceil(N/2) for N=4..10. Same for pi(x) mod 2. Counting adds no difficulty. Quantum lower bound Omega(N/4), still polylog | 28 |
| Rounding boundary / frac(R(x)) analysis | FAIL | I | frac(R(x)) perfectly uniform; precision follows geometric distribution P(k bits)=2^{-(k-1)}; no easy subset. R(x) accuracy drops to 13% at N=16 | 28 |
| Legendre I-E cancellation structure | FAIL | E | Signed matrix rank = distinct floors (full rank). 90% nonzero net. Max collision grows but doesn't cancel | 28 |
| Linear dynamical systems over Z/MZ | FAIL | I | p(n) mod M has no linear recurrence dim<=4 for M=3..30. No affine recurrence. Random matrix orbits match at chance level. Only trivial mod 2 (all odd) | 29 |
| GF(2) sieve matrix product fast-forward | FAIL | E | Product of sieve matrices is DIAGONAL (commuting). Encodes Mobius parity = Legendre sieve in disguise. Rank = #primes+1s. No matrix structure shortcut | 29 |
| Polynomial recurrence mod m (deg<=3) | FAIL | I | No degree-1/2/3 polynomial recurrence p(n)=f(p(n-1),p(n-2)) mod M for M=3,5,7. Only trivial mod 2. Extends S24 LFSR to nonlinear | 29 |
| CF / Stern-Brocot structure of p(n)/n | FAIL | I | CF partial quotients follow Gauss-Kuzmin (random). SB path lengths O(log p(n)). p(n)/n behaves as generic real | 29 |
| CRT from modular periods of p(n) | FAIL | I | p(n) mod m NOT periodic for m>=5 (within 500 terms). Only mod 2/3/4/6/12 trivially periodic (all primes odd, Dirichlet). Cannot fast-forward residues | 29 |
| Cipolla residual autoregression | FAIL | I | AR(1) 91.4% reduction but irreducible error grows as O(log n) = O(gap std). AR(20) gives no improvement over AR(1). Cannot achieve O(1) for exact | 29 |
| GUE CLT for zero sum | FAIL | I | CLT gives O(sqrt(log x)) typical size but actual correction is O(sqrt(x)/log(x)). CLT applies to random ensembles, not specific zero config | 29 |
| Zero grouping/clustering | FAIL | I | Group size 2→error 9.65, group 4→0.51 (lucky), group 8→2.65 at x=10K. Rapid oscillation of exp(i*gamma*log(x)) makes each zero position essential | 29 |
| Zero clustering truncation (Proposal 21) | FAIL | I | GUE-based clustering with sinc coherence factor. 10 zeros perform as well as 200 (error saturates at O(1)). Tail prediction via GUE completely fails — tail/partial ratios swing -3.4 to +3.3. Each zero carries independent information. | 37 |
| GUE surrogate replacement | FAIL | I | Random matrix surrogates give mean=-0.81±1.18 vs actual -0.25. Right order of magnitude, wrong value. Specific zero config matters | 29 |
| Interpolation of pi correction | FAIL | I | 100 Chebyshev nodes on [2,10000]: max error=7. Fine structure from zeta zeros prevents polynomial interpolation with few points | 29 |
| Polynomial empirical correction to R(x) | FAIL | I | Polynomial in 1/log(x) reduces error 60-80% but cross-validation confirms overfitting. Oscillatory part genuinely non-polynomial | 29 |
| Compressed sensing on prime indicator | FAIL | C | pi(x)-x/log(x) has 99% energy in 1.25% of Fourier components, but those ARE zeta zero frequencies. Circular | 29 |
| Transfer matrix / stat mech for sieve | FAIL | E | State space 2^{pi(sqrt(x))}—exponential. Wheel sieve already exploits small-prime periodicity. No stat-mech shortcut | 29 |
| p-adic interpolation of pi(x) | FAIL | I | pi(x) NOT p-adically continuous: pi(x) mod p^k varies across all residue classes of x mod p^k. Local info doesn't determine global count | 29 |
| Wilson's theorem bulk primality | FAIL | E | Running factorial requires O(x!) precision. Computing (k-1)! mod k for single k is O(k). No bulk shortcut | 29 |
| Goldbach representation extraction | FAIL | C | Computing r_2(n) requires knowing primes. Hardy-Littlewood formula error O(sqrt(n/log(n))) too noisy | 29 |
| Finite field F_q[x] analogy lift | FAIL | I | F_q[x] works because its zeta has ONE zero. Z has infinitely many. No deformation from F_q to Z preserves the counting formula | 29 |
| PSLQ identity search | FAIL | I | No integer relation found among pi(x), li, R, sqrt, log, zeta values with coefficients ≤1000. Known asymptotics are the only relations | 29 |
| Primorial decomposition optimization | FAIL | E | Optimal c=3 in x^{1/c} split → O(x^{2/3}). This IS Meissel-Lehmer. No c gives better tradeoff | 29 |
| Short-interval error structure | FAIL | C | Short-interval Fourier structure from zeta zeros. CS recovery requires zero frequencies a priori | 29 |
| Number-theoretic hash p(n) mod m | FAIL | I | p(n) mod m for m≥3 shows no periodicity, no autocorrelation, pseudorandom. No polylog-computable formula | 29 |
| DE search for f(x)=pi(x)-R(x) | FAIL | I | Tested linear ODEs order<=3 poly deg<=3, Euler-type, nonlinear poly ODEs, Volterra integrals. Best linear ODE residual matches random noise (spurious from smoothing). Euler/nonlinear residuals ~1.0. f encodes infinitely many zeta-zero oscillations incompatible with finite-order DE | 29 |
| Extended PSLQ identity search x=2..100000 | FAIL | I | 14-element basis (log,sqrt,roots,li-variants,zeta zero oscillations). 18 PSLQ tests, 15 relations with nonzero f-coeff -- ALL fail cross-validation (residuals 13-53000). Functional relations f(ax) vs f(x) for a=2,3,4: fail. Shift recurrences f(x)..f(x+10): fail. Extends S19 to 10x range | 29 |
| Wilf-Zeilberger definite sum for f(x) | FAIL | I | Delta_f bimodal (prime indicator). Higher-order differences RMS GROW (ratio->2.0=white noise). Hypergeometric recurrence R^2=0.997 is spurious (trivial autocorrelation). Summation kernel full Hankel rank 250/250 (incompressible). No WZ certificate | 29 |
| f(x) vs Bernoulli numbers | FAIL | I | Bernoulli corrections < 10^{-7} at x=1000, zero correlation with f(x) (r=-0.006). Trivial zeros contribute negligibly | 29 |
| f(x) vs zeta values zeta(2..7) PSLQ | FAIL | I | PSLQ finds x-dependent relations with large coefficients (~10^6). Different coefficients at each x. Perfect-square x gives trivial sqrt relations. No universal algebraic relation | 29 |
| f(x) vs Dirichlet L-values L(1,chi) | FAIL | I | Same as zeta values -- x-dependent large-coefficient relations only. L(1,chi_3)=0.605, L(1,chi_4)=pi/4. No universal relation | 29 |
| f(x) vs Ramanujan tau function | FAIL | I | Pearson r=+0.010, p=0.93. Spearman r=+0.054. Zero correlation. Different mathematical objects (weight-12 modular form vs zeta zero oscillations) | 29 |
| Chebyshev psi(x)/log(x) as f(x) shortcut | FAIL | E | g(x)/log(x) captures 91% of f(x) variance (r=0.996) via partial summation identity. BUT psi(x) requires O(x) computation -- worse than O(x^{2/3}) sieve. Known identity, not shortcut | 29 |
| LLL minimal polynomials for f(x) | FAIL | I | "Candidate" polynomials at every x but DIFFERENT polynomials at each x (x=100: [156396,287371,96731], x=1000: [119569,141263,-381961]). Multi-point relations at float64 precision limit. f(x) values effectively algebraically independent transcendentals | 29 |
| Polynomial-in-log(x) fit for f(x) | FAIL | I | f(x) ~ sum a_j*log(x)^j: best validation RMSE=1.976 (~47% of std). With sqrt(x) factor: no improvement. Degree>4 overfits. f(x) not a polynomial in log(x) | 29 |
| Incremental delta(n) via autocorrelation | FAIL | I | r(1)=0.975 but smooth-trend artifact (after MA-10 detrending: r(1)=0.41). AR(1) RMSE=7.31, AR(5) RMSE=7.27, both >> 0.5 threshold. Only 5.3% of predictions within rounding range. H(delta(n+1)\|delta(n))=4.93 bits/step. RMSE grows with n (4.8->8.1). See experiments/proposals/critique_incremental_delta.py | 30 |
| Verification-prediction separation | FAIL | C | Primality testing is O(polylog) but ordinality (is g the n-th prime?) requires pi(g) = O(x^{2/3}). Claim conflates primality with ordinality. Local sieving gives interval count but not global rank. At x=10^100: primality ~10^9 ops, ordinality ~10^68 ops. See experiments/proposals/critique_verification_separation.py | 30 |
| NOF discrepancy for chi_P (3-party) | FAIL | - | Discrepancy HIGH (~bias), dominated by density imbalance. Trivial cylinder (A=B=C=all) achieves max. No communication lower bound. Degree-1 Walsh correlation 3-1513x random (parity), degree ≥ 2 matches random. Consistent with TC^0. See experiments/circuit_complexity/nof_discrepancy_chi_P.py | 31 |
| Gamma-2 (factorization) norm / sign-rank | FAIL | - | γ₂ ~ 2^{0.186N}, 85-93% of random matrices. Sign-rank = rank for all N=4..10. No hidden low-rank sign structure. SM complexity bound O(log γ₂) = O(N) trivially weak. F_2 rank = rank_R - 1. See experiments/circuit_complexity/gamma2_norm_chi_P.py | 31 |
| True tensor rank of chi_P (3-way) | DIAGNOSTIC | - | Tensor rank ~ d^{1.5} = 2^{N/2} = sqrt(x). N=6: rank=5 (random=7), N=9: rank≤19 (random=29), N=12: rank≈67 (random>100). chi_P 25-35% BELOW random but still EXPONENTIAL. Confirms N/2 universality. Close to generic d²/3 bound. See experiments/circuit_complexity/tensor_rank_robust.py | 31 |
| BDD size / sensitivity / decision tree | FAIL | - | BDD grows as 1.661^N (R²=0.997). chi_P ~30% simpler than random (ratio 0.69-0.71). Sensitivity = block sensitivity = certificate complexity = N for N≥7. Decision tree depth = N. All N variables essential. See experiments/circuit_complexity/min_circuit_size.py | 31 |
| F_2 correlation profile (degree-d polynomials) | DIAGNOSTIC | - | Degree-0,1 dominate: W(0)+W(1) = 47%(N=6) to 68%(N=16). W(1) z-score grows from 3 to 1513 (parity). W(d≥2) BELOW random (z-scores -2 to -30). After bias+parity removal, chi_P more pseudorandom than random. No exploitable low-degree F_2 structure. See experiments/circuit_complexity/f2_correlation_profile.py | 31 |
| Recursive prime counting (FMM-inspired) | FAIL | E | Aggressive recursion achieves O(log log x) depth but work catastrophically concentrated at depth 0: Theta(x^{2/3}/ln x). phi recursion tree has O(x^{2/3}/ln x) leaves. FMM analogy fails: no smooth kernel. Hierarchical sieve: large primes make O(x^{2/3}) irregular removals. Reduces to Meissel-Lehmer. See experiments/wildcard/recursive_prime_counting.py | 32 |
| Tropical/min-plus fast-forward via gap structure | FAIL | I | Hankel rank 418/500 (ratio to random: 0.98). Corr dim grows with embedding (2.1→5.8, no attractor). Linear R²<0.001, quadratic R²<0.002. MI(g_n;g_{n+1})=0.013 bits (0.4% of entropy). Min-plus accuracy 7-11%. Algebraic obstruction: p(n)=sum(gaps) needs (+,×) not (min,+). Gaps indistinguishable from IID. See experiments/wildcard/tropical_prime_gaps.py | 32 |
| Trace formula / moment method for zero sums | FAIL | I | Moment expansion S(x)=Σ(lnx)^k/k!·M_{k-1} DIVERGES: |M_k|~γ_max^k>>k!, radius of convergence x<1.0007. GUE trace analogy breaks: bounded eigenvalues required. Weil geometric side circular (needs all primes to x). Effective rank ~N (no compression). Gaussian damping unfavorable trade-off. 4 independent failures. See experiments/wildcard/trace_formula_approach.py | 33 |
| Contour integral evaluation of zero sum | FAIL | E | Contour ∮(ζ'/ζ)(s)·x^s/s ds equivalent to zero summation by residue theorem. Nyquist: quadrature points ≥ enclosed zeros. Smoothing trades accuracy for fewer zeros (uncertainty principle). SVD of zero-contribution matrix: 99% energy needs 133/500 components. No sparse representation in any basis. See experiments/wildcard/contour_integral_zerosum.py | 32 |
| Hybrid analytic + local sieve | FAIL | E | R⁻¹(n) error O(√p(n)) with ratio 0.05-0.25. Truncated zero sum often WORSE than none (K=0 error 12 vs K=500 error 208 at n=50000). Optimal hybrid complexity O(x^{1/2+ε}) matches Lagarias-Odlyzko. Iterative refinement oscillates. Cannot beat O(√x) zeros required. See experiments/wildcard/hybrid_analytic_sieve.py | 32 |
| Hilbert-Pólya trace / GUE model / functional equation | FAIL | E,I | GUE: correct statistics, wrong values (std≈20-60). Moments: M_k~γ_max^k diverges. Selberg trace: circular (spectral↔geometric duality). Functional equation: doesn't change kernel. δ(x) incompressible: poly deg-50 RMSE=0.21≈deg-2. 90% Fourier power needs 138/500 modes. See experiments/wildcard/hilbert_polya_trace.py | 32 |
| Convergence acceleration of zeta zero sum | FAIL | I | Richardson, Euler-Maclaurin, Padé, Cesàro, Aitken Δ², Shanks tested. Errors GROW as ~N^{0.8-1.0} (random walk). Best: Shanks(3) gives ~10x constant improvement. Root cause: each zero carries independent info (GUE-random phases). No structured error terms to exploit. See experiments/wildcard/zero_sum_acceleration.py | 32 |
| GRH batch Miller testing for pi(x) | FAIL | E | GRH Miller (witnesses ≤2ln²n) correct to 10^5. Batch testing: 1.02x speedup (negligible). Cost O(n·polylog n) — worse than sieve O(n·loglog n) and much worse than Meissel-Lehmer O(x^{2/3}). Each number requires independent modular exponentiation. See experiments/analytic/conditional/grh_miller_batch.py | 33 |
| GRH explicit formula optimal T | FAIL | E | Under GRH, need T=O(√x·log²x) zeros for error<0.5. With 1000 zeros, error≥18 for x≥10^4. Individual zeros at γ~200-400 still contribute >0.5 to pi(10^6). No way to use fewer than O(√x) zeros. Confirms Lagarias-Odlyzko at O(√x·polylog x). See experiments/analytic/conditional/grh_miller_batch.py | 33 |
| Elliott-Halberstam for pi(x) counting | FAIL | E | EH controls distribution in residue classes, not total count. Summing li(x)/φ(q) over coprime residues gives li(x) (same error as direct). Residue-class method 10^3-10^6x SLOWER than direct primepi. EH is about equidistribution, not enumeration. See experiments/analytic/conditional/elliott_halberstam_gaps.py | 33 |
| Gap structure (Cramér) for p(n) | FAIL | I | Cramér model fits well (max gaps 40-60% of ln²p). R⁻¹(n) search interval has O(ln x) primes. BUT identifying which is p(n) requires pi(x) at boundary — the counting bottleneck. At x=10^100: counting 10^57x more expensive than searching. Cramér solves wrong problem. See experiments/analytic/conditional/elliott_halberstam_gaps.py | 33 |
| Schoenfeld interval sieve under RH | FAIL | E | |pi(x)-li(x)|<√x·ln(x)/(8π). Sieve cost O(√x·ln x·ln ln x)=O(x^{1/2+ε}), better than ML O(x^{2/3}) asymptotically but still exponential in input bits. Iterative refinement with 1000 zeros: only 21% error reduction at x=10^6. Need O(√x·log²x) zeros for exactness. See experiments/analytic/conditional/schoenfeld_cramer.py | 33 |
| Cramér search + counting bottleneck | FAIL | E | Walk phase O(ln⁴x) trivial (0-114 steps verified). Counting phase pi(x₀) grows to dominate: 25% at n=5M, heading to 100%. At x=10^100: count=10^66.7 ops vs walk=10^9.5 ops. No conjecture reduces counting below O(x^{1/2+ε}). See experiments/analytic/conditional/schoenfeld_cramer.py | 33 |
| Best conditional algorithm (all conjectures) | FAIL | E | Under RH+Odlyzko-Schönhage: O(x^{1/2+ε}). RH alone with Turing zeros: O(x^{2/3+ε}), WORSE than unconditional. GRH, EH, Cramér all fail to improve beyond O(x^{1/2+ε}). For p(10^100): ~10^51 ops minimum. The √x barrier is fundamental — no standard conjecture breaks it. See experiments/analytic/conditional/best_conditional_algorithm.py | 33 |
| Spectral truncation / adaptive zero selection | FAIL | E | Select "resonant" zeros (γ·log(x) near kπ). For n<100, 1-2 zeros suffice (small-number artifact). For n≥500, 1000 zeros insufficient. Adaptive selection 41% better than sequential but cannot reduce O(√x) requirement. = reordering explicit formula sum. See experiments/proposals/proposal16_spectral_truncation_bound.py | 33 |
| p-adic lifting via CRT for pi(x) | FAIL | C+E | CRT works (3-6 primes suffice) but pi(x) mod p costs O(x^{2/3}) per prime. floor(x/d) mod q ≠ floor((x mod q)/(d mod q)) — floor division not a ring homomorphism. 12th CRT variant tested. See experiments/proposals/proposal17_padic_lifting.py | 33 |
| Dequantized Grover counting for pi(x) | FAIL | I | Tang 2024/Chia 2025 dequantization requires low-rank input. Prime indicator has adeg=N/2, comm rank=2^{N/2-1}+2 — full-rank. Truncated Möbius sums diverge. Sampling variance O(x²/S). Hyperbola=known O(√x). See experiments/proposals/proposal18_dequantized_grover_count.py | 33 |
| PSLQ/LLL on delta(n) (Ramanujan Library) | FAIL | I | delta(n) range [-130,102], std~33, lag-1 autocorr 0.95. PSLQ finds DIFFERENT relations per n (no universal formula). No recurrence order 1-6. No modular patterns. Best basis RMSE=31. 7th PSLQ/identity variant tested. See experiments/proposals/proposal19_ramanujan_library_delta.py | 33 |
| Étale cohomology point-counting for pi(x) | FAIL | C+E | Grothendieck-Lefschetz is polylog for FIXED variety, but encoding pi(x) needs unbounded dimension. Frobenius eigenvalues = zeta zeros. Character sums cost O(x). Hasse-Weil gives enough bits but each costs O(x^{2/3}). EC trace correlation 0.39 — too weak. 3rd algebraic geometry variant tested. See experiments/proposals/proposal20_etale_cohomology_count.py | 33 |
| MKtP / meta-complexity framework | FAIL | E | Kt framework reformulates "Is pi(x) in NC?" as "Is Kt(pi(x)mod2\|x) = O(polylog)?" — equivalent, not easier. Brandt's MKtP↔circuit lower bounds is GENERIC (any function in E, not pi(x) specifically). Kt(T_N) = O(2^N·N) by sieve regardless of circuit size. No new technique beyond circuit complexity theory. See experiments/circuit_complexity/meta_complexity_analysis.py | 35 |
| Approximate circuit complexity phase transition | FAIL | I | NO phase transition in rank-k approximation accuracy. Accuracy degrades GRADUALLY (rank-2: 63%, rank-10: 83%, rank-20: 95% at N=14). Errors spatially uniform (CV=0.22-0.25). No "easy core" to exploit for approx-then-correct strategy. See experiments/circuit_complexity/approx_circuit_complexity.py | 35 |
| Truth table compressibility for pi(x) | FAIL | I | 2.6x more compressible than random at N=20 (gzip 0.385 vs 1.0) but only LOCAL structure (prime sparsity creates runs). Block entropy → max at all scales. Shannon entropy → 1.0 bit/entry. Doesn't imply small circuits. See experiments/circuit_complexity/meta_complexity_analysis.py | 35 |
| GF(2) SLP / common subexpression analysis | FAIL | I | ANF sparsity EXACTLY 0.50 (random). CSE savings 50-66% = SAME as random functions. SLP length Θ(2^N). No GF(2) algebraic compression. Variable frequencies uniform. Monomial patterns at each degree → 0.50. pi(x) mod 2 indistinguishable from random Boolean function in ANF structure. See experiments/circuit_complexity/gf2_slp_structure.py | 35 |
| Smooth approximation for pi(x) parity | FAIL | I | R(x) accuracy for pi(x) mod 2 → 0.50 (random) as N grows. Smooth part provides ZERO parity information. Top-2 SVs capture only 12% of variance at N=14 (decreasing as 1/N). Parity entirely determined by oscillatory (zeta zero) contributions. See experiments/circuit_complexity/approx_circuit_complexity.py | 35 |
| Unbalanced communication matrix rank | DIAGNOSTIC | - | For k_LSB > N/2, actual rank EXCEEDS formula 2^{min(k,N-k)-1}+2 — often full row rank. Formula only tight for k ≤ N/2. SVD spectrum flat (power-law decay, no gap). Each additional SV contributes small equal increment. Polynomial rank for k=2logN but component functions g_i(MSBs) are as hard as pi(x). See experiments/circuit_complexity/approx_circuit_complexity.py | 35 |
| Depth-2 threshold circuits for pi(x) LSB | FAIL | I | PTF degree = N/2 exactly (LP-verified N=4-12, ratio 0.50 stable from N=6). Requires C(N,N/2) ~ 2^N/sqrt(N) monomials = EXPONENTIAL. Single LTF accuracy → 0.50 (random). Depth-2 heuristic with k=64 gates fails for N≥8. Matches random function PTF degree (Gotsman 1994). Does NOT rule out poly-depth TC^0. See experiments/circuit_complexity/threshold_circuit_construction.py | 35 |
| SAT-based minimum circuit size for pi(x) mod 2 | INCONCLUSIVE | - | N=4: exact 6 gates (same as random). N=5-8 upper bounds: 12, 39, 81, 153 (grow ~2x/bit). N=9-10 unreliable (Shannon timeout, DNF fallback gives loose 2015/4829). pi(x)mod2 behaves like random function — no structural advantage. Need ABC or dedicated tools for tight bounds at N>=9. See experiments/circuit_complexity/sat_circuit_minimization.py | 35,36b |
| Adelic local-global prime counting (CRT mod q) | FAIL | E | 6th CRT variant. pi(x) mod q via AP decomposition 100-1000x SLOWER than pi(x) direct. R(x) error grows as O(sqrt(x)), mod-q correctness degrades to 0/6 at x=10^6. Liouville/Mertens parity ~50% (random). floor division not a ring homomorphism kills all modular decomposition. 3-6 moduli suffice for CRT but each costs O(x^{2/3}). See experiments/wildcard/adelic_prime_count.py | 35 |
| MPS / TT bond dimension of chi_P | FAIL | E | Bond dim of prime indicator at every cut j EXACTLY equals min(2^j, 2^{d-j-1}+1). Proof: rows i>=1 of unfolding have all odd columns zero (primes>2 are odd), so rank ≤ 2^{d-j-1}+1. Saturated empirically k=10..20 (k=20 half-cut: 513/1024). Random control hits full 2^{d/2}. Constant factor 2 = wheel-2 in MPS language. Residual delta(n) bond dim ~ N^{0.42} at eps=0.01, returns to full at exact-integer precision. No polylog tensor-network representation. See experiments/wildcard/mps_bond_dimension.py | 42 |
| J-fraction of pi(n)/n generating function (D-finiteness test) | FAIL | E | Hankel-determinant J-fraction coefficients (a_k, b_k) for k=0..11 from first 80 moments are erratic: b_3=-3, b_4=-8.28, b_12=-407.4. log\|H_k\| decays linearly in k but that is a trivial consequence of moments in [0,1] — NOT evidence of D-finiteness. pi(n) is not P-recursive at any low order. See experiments/proposals/cf_jfraction_pi.py | 43 |
| Persistent homology of detrended primes (TCDP) | FAIL | I | 0-dim long-lived bar count is sub-(N/log N) but Cramér-model baseline is too — sublinearity is a generic property of bounded-detrended trajectories, not specific to primes. PH gives a measurement, not an inverse-map: recovering p_n from a barcode requires bar endpoints AND original 1D ordering of n, which IS pi(x). Re-confirms TDA closure (line 199). See experiments/proposals/persistent_homology_primes.py | 43 |
| Free probability R-transform of prime indicator (FCP) | FAIL | C | A_N = diag(1_P) is idempotent, so trace(A_N^j)/N = pi(N)/N for all j. ESD = (1-pi(N)/N)*delta_0 + (pi(N)/N)*delta_1 — Bernoulli measure parameterized by pi(N) alone. R-transform is closed-form q/(1-qz) with q = pi(N)/N. Free convolution preserves this triviality. Encodes only pi(N), the very quantity we want. | 43 |
| Reservoir computing on delta(n) (Dynamical Compressibility) | FAIL | I | ESN is a strict subset of recurrent dynamical systems; transformer (line 149, 1.1% accuracy), FNO, GP, PSLQ symbolic regression on delta(n) all already failed. delta(n) Kt entropy 5.58 bits/value (line 555). Polylog reservoir state cannot encode irreducible info. Symbolic-regression readout duplicates line 146. | 43 |
| Stein's-method sub-Gaussian explicit-formula error (SGEFE) | FAIL | I | Tested f_T(x) = R(x) - sum_{|gamma|<T} 2 Re R_log(rho*log x) for x in {100..10000}, T in {10..1000}, all 1000 zeros. log10\|err\| vs T^2/log(x): slope ~-3e-6, R^2 = 0.2-0.4 — NO sub-Gaussian decay. Error saturates at O(0.5) for x=10000 even at T=1000. Tail L^2 norm sqrt(x*logT/T) is THE rate, no concentration gap. Stein-method has no martingale structure to exploit on GUE-random phases. Duplicates lines 24, 617, 662. See experiments/proposals/stein_explicit_formula_decay.py | 43 |
| Fixed-point iteration p_{k+1} = R^{-1}(n + sum_rho R(p_k^rho)) | FAIL | I+E | Iteration is non-contracting and non-monotone. n=10: k=2 hits err=-0.14 then k=3 drifts back to -1.89. n=1000: k=1 hits err=-0.16, k=2/k=3 drift to -0.6. n=100, n=10000: no iterate within 0.5 in 3 steps (200 zeros). Effect of #zeros at n=1000: 200 zeros converges k=1, but 500 zeros does NOT in same budget — non-monotone. Map f(x)=R^{-1}(n+S(x)) has \|f'\| oscillating ~ \|S'(x)\|, GUE-random; no contraction. Sporadic correctness ~50% of tested n. No improvement over direct explicit-formula evaluation: still O(sqrt(x)) zeros per step. See experiments/sieve/inversion_search.py | 44 |
| Liouville-parity triangulation (identity pi(x)=(x-L(x))/2-C_3(x)) | FAIL | E | Identity verified exactly for all x in [2,10000] (corrected: original proposal had wrong term Q(x)-1; should be C_3(x), the count of integers <=x with Omega(n) odd and >=3). Bottleneck just shifts to C_3(x), which grows ~3*pi(x) and is parity-random vs simple proxies (50.0-50.7% match for pi(x^{1/3}), pi(x^{1/2}), x/8, L(x)). Truncated L_K (cap on Omega) fails parity even at K=10 (59% match). Re-confirms pi(x) mod 2 closures (lines 233, 672). See experiments/proposals/proposal25_liouville_parity_check.py | 46 |

Independent confirmation: SoS lower bounds on random-like predicates (Grigoriev '01, Schoenebeck '08, KMOS '17) give degree Omega(N) given pseudorandomness of pi(x) mod 2 in 25+ measures (novel/pseudorandomness_of_pi.md). Full zero sum (T->infty) gives density polylog/sqrt(x)->0 by GUE statistics; truncated tail Sum_{|gamma|>T} y^rho/rho contributes Omega(sqrt(y/(log x log T))) which dominates at large y. | Borel-Pade x Cesaro-Fejer hybrid for pi(x) (stacked convergence-acceleration) | FAIL | E | Apply Fejer window K_T(gamma)=(sin(gamma/2T)/(gamma/2T))^2 first, then Borel-Pade resum the windowed-increment partial sums. Sweep x in {1000..100000}, T in {50,100,300,1000}, 2000 zeros, mp.dps=30. Borel-Fejer recovery flat at 3/8 (37.5%) regardless of T, vs Fejer-alone 6/8 (75%) at T=1000. Diagnosis: Borel-Pade locks into a T-independent asymptote — at x=10000 the hybrid returns S in {1229.61, 1229.70, 1229.70, 1229.70} across T={50,100,300,1000}, IDENTICAL to 4 decimals. Pade fits the leading envelope of increments; the Borel integral integral e^{-z}P/Q dz extracts a tail-completion that depends only on low-order coefficients. New zeros added by larger T are exponentially suppressed by 1/k! in the Borel transform, so they cannot move Pade's leading-order fit. Strict regression vs Fejer alone at every T (regressions 3-4, wins 0-1). Closes the natural S45+S46 stacking; convergence-acceleration interventions cannot be combined to break sqrt(x). See experiments/proposals/borel_fejer_hybrid.py | 51 |
| Connes-Consani-Moscovici spectral-triple operator (arXiv:2511.22755) as polylog architecture for pi(x) (Chain A step 4 / FOCUS-1 / E3.1) | FAIL | E | Numerical proxy: rank-one self-adjoint perturbation V = c\|v><v\| of discretized scaling operator D = -i d/du on L^2([-L,L]), L = log(lambda), x_cutoff = 10^4, N=1200 Fourier modes; v built from von-Mangoldt-weighted prime-power delta-comb in log space (best variant). Sweep B in {1..9} primes ({2}, {2,3}, ..., {2..23}); coupling tuned at B=6. Median matched-error[:50] flat at 0.13-0.20 across all B (no monotonic decrease in B); K_accurate(<0.5) saturates at 50/50 by comb-density artefact (also true for B=0 control); under monotone test (mu_K vs gamma_K), K_accurate=0 for all B. Proxy does NOT reproduce CCM's published 10^{-55} accuracy on err[1] (off by ~53 orders), but scaling-law conclusion is independent of kernel choice. Three independent arguments close polylog claim: (i) rank-one perturbation has B parameters; Cauchy interlacing limits eigenvalue placement to ~B substantial shifts; (ii) diagonalization of N x N truncation costs O(N^3) = O(K^3) for K eigenvalues — worse than O(x^{1/2+eps}) summation; (iii) CCM's published B=6 -> K=50 with err range 10^{-55..-3} implies geometric per-zero error growth ~11.3x, yielding K_accurate(B=6) ~ 53 even at face value, with NO multi-B data point in the literature establishing super-linear scaling. Even granting most-generous linear K_accurate(B)=B, reaching K=10^50 zeros for p(10^100) requires B ~ 10^50 primes, identical to existing O(sqrt(x)) zero-summation barrier. Closes E3.1's elevated edge value to baseline analytic equivalence. See experiments/analytic/connes_operator/. | 53 |
| BBP-style isolated-digit spigot for psi(x) via explicit-formula partial sums | FAIL | I | Empirical zero-vs-precision scan: K in {10,50,200,1000,2000} zeros, x in {1e4..1e7}. Anomaly hunt (4001 consecutive x near 1e6, K=2000): residuals are i.i.d. Gaussian with std ~25 (= 0.025*sqrt(x)); smallest |res|=0.012 at x=1001799 matches Gaussian-tail expectation (1.5 expected, 1 observed) — no structural cancellation. BBP works because summands decay GEOMETRICALLY (1/16^k); zero contributions decay only as 1/k via `sqrt(x)/|rho_k|`, so zeros-per-digit grows ~10^d (exponential in digits) not O(d). Reason: 1/|rho_k| weighting concentrates magnitude on low-frequency zeros, so random subsamples miss the bulk of the signal. Falsifies "compressed sensing applies to zero basis" — signal is DENSE in zero basis with smoothly-decaying amplitudes, not K-sparse. C_3(x) mod 2 same with AC = 0.148 (density bias only). | Character-twisted Liouville sums L_chi(x) for chi mod q in {3,5,7,11,13} (FOCUS-2 q>=3 / Chain B) | FAIL | I | Generalizes the S55 q=2 closure to all 34 non-trivial Dirichlet characters across q in {3,5,7,11,13}. Right-hand side computable in O(polylog) via floor counts, no prime info. Verified by EXACT integer arithmetic in Z[zeta_d] (cyclotomic-polynomial reduction; rank-deficient FP-projection appears to fail for d in {5,10,12} but exact check passes 2000/2000 sampled x for ALL 34 chars). Next-bit pseudorandomness: A_chi(x) mod 2 = #{n<=x: lambda(n)*Re(chi(n))=+1} mod 2 has LFSR/N in [0.4995, 0.5002] (full pseudorandom rank), block-entropy up to 7.97/8, FFT z up to 8.5 (within 1.5sigma of order-statistic baseline ~4.8), AC values up to 0.6 are pure coset-density bias 1/phi(d). MAX MI(A_chi mod 2 ; pi(x;q,a) mod 2) = 9.55e-6 bits across all q, all chi, all a in (Z/q)* — at noise floor for N=1e6 samples. Closes the natural extension of S55 to q>=3; the character-twisted route to chain B's missing primitive collapses by the same dual mechanism (free identity + pseudorandom next bit). Adds measures #27..#31 to novel/pseudorandomness_of_pi.md. See experiments/sieve/pi_mod_q_fixed/character_twisted_liouville.py | 56 |

| Healy-Viola Frobenius-mod-q transplant of AKS (FOCUS-1 sub-attack 3) | FAIL | E+I | Tests whether reducing AKS congruence (a+x)^n ≡ a^n + x^n mod (n, x^r-1) modulo a prime q\|n-1 yields a TC^0-amenable test via the q-power Frobenius F: F_q[x]/(x^r-1)→itself, F(a+x)=a+x^q (ring hom). Frobenius gives a binomial decomposition (a+x)^n = prod_i (a+x^{q^i mod r})^{c_i} where c_i are base-q digits of n; verified matches naive repeated squaring (6/6 cases). HEADLINE NEGATIVE: primes do NOT satisfy mod-q AKS for non-trivial a — 0/399 passes across 19 primes in [101..2207] for every q\|n-1, q<r, every a in {1..q-1}. Trivial case a≡0 mod q always passes (both sides reduce to x^n) but carries zero primality info. WHY: residual (LHS-RHS) mod (q,x^r-1) has 8-102 of r non-zero coefficients (Lucas's theorem mod q gives binom(n,k) non-zero whenever k is a base-q sub-string of n; explicit n=101=0b1100101: 16 odd binomials in {0,1,4,5,32,33,36,37,...}). DEPTH: even granted the test worked, Frobenius decomposition saves only constant factor log(q)/log(2) bit-ops vs naive (~2x for q=2, ~3.3x for q=7); both reduce to O(log n) sequential r-dim multiplications = NC^1, not TC^0. (E) The mod-q computation is length-O(log n) growing-dim r×r MPOW over F_q — same primitive E5.3 unchanged by q-twist. (I) AKS Frobenius identity is mod-n specific; reducing mod q≠n loses it (no Hensel lifting). With FOCUS-1 sub-attacks 2 (S61, line 714) and 3 (this) closed, only sub-attack 1 (Bernstein 2003 strengthened gcd) remains un-built. See experiments/circuit_complexity/aks_alternative/frobenius_transplant/. | 64 |

| Brandt 2024 (TCC) MKtP diagonalisation → π(x) mod 2 lower bound (FOCUS-3) | FAIL | E | Brandt 2024 "Lower Bounds for Levin-Kolmogorov Complexity" (TCC, IACR ePrint 2024/687) proves MKtP ∉ DTIME[O(n)] unconditionally (Thm 1) plus three sharper conditional results (Thm 2 one-sided heuristic; Cor 1 quadratic for RAM; Cor 2 slightly super-linear) via a length-monotonic depth-first traversal TRAVERSE_{c_Ω,c_Kt} that uses 1-Kt-randomness of Chaitin's Ω prefixes to bypass the black-box barrier. The technique relativizes and is **not subject to algebrization or Natural Proofs (T4)**, satisfying FOCUS-3's stated promise. **HOWEVER it does not extend to π(x) mod 2** for four orthogonal reasons: (O1) the hard string z that TRAVERSE produces is an oracle-dependent Kt-random prefix, NOT a fixed natural function — π_N is fixed per N and Brandt has no mechanism to use a "fast π-decider" to construct z; (O2) the contradiction is `Kt(z) ≥ |z|` ∧ `Kt(z) ≤ |M| + log₂ t` — self-referential Kt on both sides — and there is no analog of `f-hardness(z) ≥ |z|` for a fixed Boolean function f = π mod 2; (O3) Lemma 2's 1-Kt-randomness of Ω prefixes is the *only* black-box-barrier-bypass ingredient, and there is no density of "π-hard inputs" (π is total and in DTIME[x^{2/3}]); (O4) Brandt produces UNIFORM-TIME lower bounds, not circuit lower bounds — Chain E (E5.3) needs a circuit lower bound and Brandt explicitly avoids the Williams/Hirahara algorithmic-method route precisely because that's the route subject to Natural Proofs on stronger classes. Empirical pseudorandomness of π(x) mod 2 (E1.9, 33 measures, novel/pseudorandomness_of_pi.md) is consistent with π_N being Kt-random but does not establish it asymptotically and would itself face T4. Bounded-Kt simulator (3-bit-op stack VM, L_MAX=12, T_MAX=4096) confirms TRAVERSE behaves as in the paper (descends on Kt-random, right-steps on compressible) and that π_N is incompressible by short bounded-VM programs at all tested N ∈ {3..10}. First bracket is polylog-smooth. Second bracket is sum of two *independent* chaotic signals because δ(n) and δ(n/2) sample different ranges of zeta zeros (heights ~√n vs ~√(n/2)); independent chaotic signals add in RMS, not cancel: Var[δ(n) − 2δ(n/2)] = Var[δ(n)] + 4·Var[δ(n/2)] ~ const·n. Strong family-level corollary: any polylog-many evaluations of p at inputs n_1,…,n_k ≪ n cannot reconstruct p(n), because they don't carry zero-phase data at height √n — only low-frequency phase. | g_q paired bisection (A(x) mod q, C_3(x) mod q) for q ∈ {2,3,5,7,11,13} — composition C1 of E1.6 + E1.5 | FAIL | I | Construction succeeded as composition (paper-grade) but **does not open a polylog route to π(x)**. Three predictions tested at N = 2·10⁶ across q ∈ {2,3,5,7,11,13} and X ∈ {10⁴, 10⁵, 10⁶, 2·10⁶}: **(PR1)** per-component closed form `H(F mod q | F(x−1) mod q) = h_2(F(X)/X) + O(1/F(X))` for F ∈ {π, A, C_3} — PASS at 5e-3 threshold (worst |diff| 1.6e-3 at small X, ≤ 5e-6 at X = 2·10⁶). Extends S69's E1.5 closed form to all monotone Ω-stratified summatories. **(PR2)** joint closed form `H(g_q(x) | g_q(x−1)) = H_3(1−ρ_A, ρ_π, ρ_C3)` — PASS at 5e-3 in regime q²·100 ≤ π(X), with the same finite-state-coverage failure mode at q² ~ π(X) that S69 documented. **(PR3)** q-stable marginal independence I(A mod q ; C_3 mod q) < 0.01 bits — PASS, worst I = 1.17e-4 bits at q = 13, X = 2·10⁶ (two orders below threshold). The composition reveals a quantitative *destructive-interference* picture: each bisection component carries asymptotically 1 bit/step (ρ_A, ρ_C3 → 1/2) while their integer difference π carries 0 bits/step (ρ_π → 0). The (delta_A, delta_C_3) increments live on 3 of the 4 corners of {0,1}² (incompatibility (0,1) → impossible since C_3-events ⊆ A-events) — the constraint that produces the destructive cancellation. **No polylog handle**: g_q is at least as expensive to compute as π (Ω-stratified counting), and the components are pseudorandom at full per-step rate, so peeling off A or C_3 separately gives no advantage. Strict generalisation of E1.6 (q=2 only → q-stable up to q=13) and of E1.5 (π only → π, A, C_3 + joint). Cites E1.5, E1.6, E2.10, S69. See experiments/constructions/g_q_bisection_invariant/. | 70 |
| N/2 universality battery on {f_pi, f_sqfree, f_mu_pos, f_lam_pos, f_sqfree3, PRF} — composition C5 of E1.4 + E2.5 | REFINEMENT | I | Construction succeeded as a *refinement* of E1.4 plus a structural unification of E2.7 + E2.8; does not open polylog. Ran the cheap subset of E1.4's six measures (M1 balanced communication-matrix rank, M2 GF(2) Berlekamp-Massey linear complexity, M3 approximate degree at eps=0.49, M4 PTF degree) on six Boolean functions on {0,...,2^N-1} for N up to 14. Three rank regimes emerge with **closed-form identities**: rank(M_chi_P) = 2^{N/2-1} + 1 exactly (verified N=6,8,10,12,14, the indicator analogue of E2.7's "+2" for the counting function); rank(M_sqfree) = rank(M_mu_pos) = 3·2^{N/2-1} exactly (3/4 of full rank); rank(M_lam_pos) = 2^{N/2} (FULL); rank(M_prf) → 2^{N/2}. Structural cause: rank(M_f) ≤ (1 − rho_f)·2^{N/2} where rho_f is the density of lower-half indices b for which f(a·2^{N/2}+b) = 0 for every row a — for chi_P even-b columns are zero (x even ⇒ x non-prime for x>2), giving rho=1/2; for sqfree, mu_pos the columns with b ≡ 0 mod 4 are zero (4 | x ⇒ not squarefree), giving rho=1/4; for lam_pos no automatic column zeros, giving rho=0. M3 (approximate degree): hits ceil(N/2) exactly for {chi_P, mu_pos, lam_pos} at every tested N, but **deviates** for sqfree (BELOW: 4 vs 5 at N=10), sqfree3 (ABOVE: 4 vs 3 at N=6, 6 vs 5 at N=10), and density-matched PRF (BELOW). M4 PTF mirrors M3. M2 BM/2^N ≈ 0.50 universally across all six functions. **Net new content**: (i) refinement of E1.4 — N/2 universality is a property of the parity-of-Omega family {chi_P, mu_pos, lam_pos}, not of all natural NT Boolean functions; (ii) unification of E2.7 + E2.8 — both rank-deficiency anomalies trace to a common column-zeroing density rho_f; (iii) three exact rank closed forms for non-pi functions. Cites E1.4, E2.5, E2.7, E2.8. See experiments/constructions/n_over_2_universality_class/. | 71 |

| §A.A3 ATTACK_VECTORS — spectral primality test via fixed-generator (Z/nZ)* Cayley graph (cross-domain: spectral graph theory, Bourgain) | FAIL | E | Frontier attack on whether spec(Cay((Z/nZ)*, {2,3,5,2⁻¹,3⁻¹,5⁻¹})) decides primality of n. Empirical: 533 values n ∈ [7,2000] coprime to 30 (300 primes, 13 prime powers, 214 semiprimes, 6 triple-prime composites); 24 spectral features tested (gap, mults, integer-eigenvalue count, trace moments, max multiplicity, entropy). **No feature is disjoint primes-vs-composites.** Mann-Whitney AUC for primes-vs-prime-powers (the hard sub-case): 0.509 with {2,3,5}, 0.566 with {2,3,7}, 0.673 with {2,3,5,7}∪inv — essentially noise. **Structural reason** (proved from CRT character theory + cross-checked on 342 n × 3 generator sets, 0 violations): for n odd squarefree-or-not coprime to S, n_int_eigs(Cay) ≥ **2^ω(n)** (each (Z/p_i^{a_i}Z)* contributes 2-torsion of order 2; product over distinct prime factors). Lower bound is sharp in 84/533 cases. **Spectrum probes ω(n), not primality**: prime n and prime power n=p^k both have ω=1, so 2^ω = 2, and (Z/p^kZ)* is cyclic for odd p (Gauss), structurally identical to (Z/qZ)* for prime q with appropriately aligned discrete logarithms. (E) Spectral feature reduces to ω(n) extraction; deciding primality from ω(n) requires also a perfect-power test, which inherits AKS-class growing-dim MPOW depth (E5.3, E7.10). **Distinct from prior Cayley graph closures** (CLOSED_PATHS lines 354, 383, 384, 385) which used primes-as-generators (circular); A3 fixed generators and varied n. **Net new content**: (a) explicit lower bound n_int_eigs ≥ 2^ω(n) with character-theoretic proof; (b) negative-shape edge candidate: any fixed-generator abelian Cayley graph spectrum is blind to prime-vs-prime-power; (c) generator-robustness empirically verified across {2,3,5}, {2,3,7}, {2,3,5,7}. Cites E5.3, E5.8, E7.10. See experiments/circuit_complexity/cayley_spectral_primality/. | 79 |
| Tensor-network compression family (MPS / HT / TR / PEPS / CP / Tucker / MERA) — N1 negative-shape closure of E2.1 | REFINEMENT | I | Family-level lift of E2.1 from MPS-only to a uniform half-cut bond-dim lower bound across **five classical polynomial-spatial-locality tensor ansätze**. Cites E2.1 (annotated S77), E1.9, E6.3. See experiments/constructions/tensor_compression_family_closure/. | 77 |
| §D.D4 ATTACK_VECTORS — Szegedy quantum walks on number-theoretic graphs (cross-domain: Szegedy 2004 discriminant matrix theorem) | FAIL | E | Frontier attack on whether Szegedy walks on a number-theoretic graph yield polylog primality extraction. Tested **three families** with the discriminant matrix `D(x,y) = sqrt(P(x,y)·P(y,x))` and the eigenvalue-lifting theorem `cos(θ_k) → e^{±2iθ_k}` (mixing time `O(1/sqrt(δ))`). **(A) Cayley `Cay((Z/NZ)*, {2,3,5,inv})`** for primes N∈{31,…,1009} (14 sizes): empirical `t_classical(N) ~ N^{0.789}`, `t_szegedy(N) ~ N^{0.414}` — quadratic Szegedy speedup empirically realised but neither is polylog. Direct **quantum extension of E7.12 (S79)**: classical Cayley spectrum probes ω(n) not primality, AND Szegedy quantisation inherits the same poly-mixing barrier. **(B) Coprime `C_x = ([1..x], gcd=1)`** for x∈{30,…,1000}: spectral gap is `Ω(1)` (asymptotically `δ → 0.4166...`); stationary prime mass / uniform prime mass `→ ζ(2) = π²/6 ≈ 1.6449` (Mertens; verified at x=1000 with deviation -0.022). Both classical and Szegedy mix in `O(1)`, but bias is constant — `O(log x)` samples to hit a prime + AKS verification = no polylog extractor of π(x). **(C) Divisor `D_x = ([1..x], m|n)`**: gap `~Ω(1)` (slowly decaying 0.21 → 0.166 across x=30..1000). High-prime-mass eigenvectors localise on **prime-centered divisor clusters** `{p, 2p, 3p, ..., 1}` for *individual* primes near x/3-x/2 (e.g., k=14 eigenvector at x=250 is concentrated 100% on {43, 47} ∪ multiples ∪ {1}). Each eigenvector identifies 1-2 specific primes — same structural mechanism as E7.12 (degree class probing), not global primality. **Cross-domain ingredient performed real work**: empirical confirmation `t_Q ≈ sqrt(t_C)` for the Szegedy-Cayley case validates the discriminant theorem at the project's scale; the π²/6 stationary identity is a clean Mertens-law derivation specific to the coprime-graph chain. **Falsification check**: no graph yields mixing time `O(polylog(x))` AND a primality-localised eigenvector with poly-log gap; both Cayley (poly mixing) and coprime/divisor (Ω(1) mixing but no global primality eigenvector) miss the joint condition. Closes ATTACK_VECTORS §D.D4. **Suggested EDGE** (E7.13 candidate): "Szegedy-walk quantisation of any reversible chain on `[1..x]` with adjacency defined by simple arithmetic relation does not yield polylog primality extraction — either spectral gap shrinks polynomially (Cayley class) or eigenvectors capture primality only via degree-class clustering (coprime / divisor class). The two conditions are exhaustive on the families tested." Cites E5.3, E5.8, E7.10, E7.12. Cross-domain reference: Szegedy 2004 (arxiv quant-ph/0401053). See experiments/quantum/szegedy_walk_prime_graphs/. | 80 |
| C6 (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — three-pillars × HKM time-space tradeoff classification | REFINEMENT | I | Composition of E7.7 (three-pillars informational closure: prime positions / zeta zeros / floor values) and E6.7 (HKM at (T = N^{8/15}, S = N^{1/3})).
