All four pre-stated falsification criteria (F1: HKM dominated; F2: HKM cross-pillar dominated; F3: identical pillar frontiers; F4: floor-pillar T*S < 5/6) PASS. **Net new content**: (a) explicit pillar-by-pillar Pareto map of the project's catalog; (b) HKM uniqueness lemma (HKM's (T, S) point is achievable on the floor pillar ONLY); (c) saturation verdict (floor pillar asymptotically tight). No polylog opening — refinement of E7.7 and E6.7, not a new edge. Cites E5.1, E6.5, E6.6, E6.7, E6.8, E6.9, E7.6, E7.7, E7.9. See experiments/constructions/pillar_tradeoff_diagram/. | 81 |
| §A.A1 ATTACK_VECTORS — SAT/ILP-based search for small TC^0 (depth-2 sign-threshold W=1) circuits computing PRIMES at N≤8 (cross-domain: SAT-based circuit synthesis, Niemetz et al.) | FAIL | I | Wild-swing on §A1: enumerate small TC^0 circuits matching PRIMES at N=8 via SAT/ILP. Built three solvers (Z3 If-then-else, direct ILP via PuLP+CBC, pre-enumerated column-selection ILP); the pre-enumeration approach is 5-10x faster. **Exact PTF degrees** (LP feasibility on monomial basis, real coefficients): PRIMES at N=4..8 → 2, 3, 3, 3, **4**; pi(x) mod 2 → 3, 3, 3, 4, 4; matched random median over 5 seeds → 2, 3, 3, 3, 4. PRIMES PTF degree statistically indistinguishable from random — confirms E1.10/E3.13 at the Boolean-function level. **Exact depth-2 sign-threshold (W=1 both layers) sizes**: N=4 → M=3, N=6 → M=6 (full kmax candidate set, 1458 W=1 thresholds). N=7 inconclusive (M=5, 6 unsat; M=7, 8 timed out at 180s). N=8: M ≥ 17 with kmax≤5 candidate set (M=8, 10, 12, 14 unsat with kmax=4; M=16 unsat with kmax=5; M=20 timed out). **Statistically significant PRIMES-vs-random gap at N=6**: PRIMES needs M=6 bottom gates; ALL 10 random matched controls (weight 18/64) need M ∈ {7, 8} (4 seeds → M=7, 6 seeds → M=8). Binomial null p < 0.001. **First instance in the project of a circuit-complexity measure where PRIMES empirically deviates from a matched-density random function** (35+ prior pseudorandomness measures show no deviation). **Mechanism**: PRIMES has an exceptionally strong single-bit predictor (bit_0 = "is x odd") with 70.3% accuracy at N=8, vs random's best 1-bit at 57.0% (max over 30 seeds). The depth-2 circuit gets the 1-bit predictor "for free" as the first bottom gate; subsequent gates correct fewer remaining errors → smaller M. The PRIMES-vs-random depth-2 gap REDUCES TO the elementary "most primes are odd" fact (1-bit advantage of 0.13 absolute at N=8). At N=4 the gap is absent (random can match) because the small domain admits accidental 1-bit predictors; at N≥6 the gap is robust because no random function of similar weight has such a strong 1-bit bias. **Bottom-layer gates of found solutions are empirical fits**, not number-theoretic primality witnesses (no Miller-Rabin / BPSW / AKS interpretation). **Closure mode I** (information loss): the depth-2 W=1 sign-threshold complexity at N=8 is super-linear in N (M_PRIMES ≥ 17 vs N=8), strongly suggesting exponential or super-polynomial scaling not polylog. **Cross-domain ingredient performed real work**: SAT/ILP search across 35K candidate functions enabled exact bounds infeasible by hand. Refines CLOSED_PATHS line "Nonlinear sieve: bitwise/TC^0" (S14) with quantitative complexity numbers. ATTACK_VECTORS §A1 is **NOT closed by this session** (depth-5 size-2000 enumeration intractable with current tools); the W=1 sign-threshold sub-family is closed at N=8 with the ≥17-gate lower bound. Cites E1.10, E3.13, E5.3, E7.10, S20, S28, novel/pseudorandomness_of_pi.md (now requires footnote: 36th measure DEVIATES with elementary mechanism). See experiments/circuit_complexity/sat_tc0_primes_n8/. | 84 |
| S148 commit-thread step 1 — S82 follow-on |L(1, chi)|^2 eigenvalue conjecture REFUTED, replaced by Gallagher-variance scaling sigma^2 ~ N/(p log^2 N) | REFUTATION+DISCOVERY | C | Commit-thread session 1/5 on the S82 invariant-subspace theorem. **S82 conjectured**: spike eigenvectors of `M^T M` are Dirichlet character vectors `chi_lift(n) = chi(n mod q)` with eigenvalue `sigma^2 ~ |L(1, chi)|^2`. **Two-prong empirical test against S82's saved JSON dumps at d ∈ {14, 18, 20}, W=2.** **PR1 (S82's hypothesis)**: ratio `(sum sigma^2 in q-block) / (sum |L(1, chi)|^2 over phi(p)-1 non-principal chars mod p)` constant across `p ∈ {3, 5, 7, 11}`. Computed |L(1, chi)|^2 via closed-form `L(1, chi) = -(1/p) sum_a chi(a) psi(a/p)` (digamma; Davenport MNT Ch.6) for all chars mod p ∈ {3, 5, 7, 11, 13}. **PR1 FAILS at d=20**: ratios = (18246, 2093, 813, 539) across p=(3,5,7,11), CV = 1.37 (>>0.2 threshold). **PR2 (alternative)**: rescaled `K(d, p) := sum sigma^2 · p · log^2(N) / N` constant across saturated (d, p). **PR2 PASSES**: K = (4.48, 3.83, 3.79, 4.08, 3.67, 3.38, 3.78) across (d,p) ∈ {(14,3), (18,3), (18,5), (18,7), (20,3), (20,5), (20,7)}, mean 3.86, std 0.32, CV 0.082. **Within-block per-character resolution test**: at d=20, mod-7 block sigma^2 spread max/min = 1.10× while corresponding |L(1, chi)|^2 spread 4.70× — within-block sigma values are nearly equal across the phi(p) spikes, NOT correlated with individual character L-values. **Net new content**: (a) **S82's eigenvalue conjecture sigma^2 ~ |L(1,chi)|^2 is decisively refuted** at the per-block AND per-character level; the residue-class character IDENTIFICATION stands, but the eigenvalue formula does not. (b) **Empirical replacement formula**: `sum_block sigma^2 ≈ 3.86 · N / (p log^2 N) ≈ 3.86 · pi(N)/(p log N) ≈ 3.86 · pi(N; p, *)` — a Gallagher-Montgomery-Vaughan PNT-in-AP variance scaling, NOT an L-function-value formula. (c) **Sharper C-circular interpretation**: the spike eigenvalues are determined by the second-moment of pi(N; q, a) over residue classes (Gallagher 1970, Montgomery-Vaughan MNT I Ch.17), so the C2 spectral barrier reduces to the Gallagher 2nd-moment — a quantity itself controlled by Bombieri-Vinogradov/PNT-in-AP, with no polylog method. (d) Closes the "look-up L(1, chi) to compute pi(N) via spectral compression" route hoped for by S82. **Closure mode C** (circular): to compute `sum_block sigma^2` one would need pi(N; p, *) for small p, which is itself the PNT-in-AP problem. Cites E2.1, E1.5, S74, S82. Cross-domain refs: Gallagher 1970 *Mathematika 23*; Montgomery-Vaughan 2007 *MNT I* Ch.17; Davenport 2000 *MNT* Ch.6 (closed-form L(1, chi) via digamma). See experiments/constructions/spike_eigenvalue_l_squared/. | 148 |

| §D6 ATTACK_VECTORS — Gowers U^k uniformity norms of the bare prime indicator chi_P (cross-domain: additive combinatorics, Green-Tao-Ziegler U^{s+1} inverse theorem) | FAIL | E | Frontier attack on whether chi_P deviates from random pseudorandomly under any Gowers norm at k <= 3 — the only family of "k-degree polynomial structure" tests untouched by the project's 35+ pseudorandomness measures. U^2 via FFT identity `‖f‖_{U^2}^4 = (1/N^4) Σ_k |fhat(k)|^4` (O(N log N)) at N ∈ {2^10, 2^12, 2^14, 2^16, 2^18}; U^3 via recursion `‖f‖_{U^3}^8 = (1/N) Σ_h ‖Δ_h f‖_{U^2}^4` with `Δ_h f(x) = f(x)f(x+h)` (O(N^2 log N)) at N ∈ {2^10, 2^11, 2^12, 2^13}. **Hardy-Littlewood prediction**: `Q^k(chi_P) → S_k = Π_p α_p(k) / (1 - 1/p)^{2^k}` (singular series for {0,1}^k-cube prime k-tuple), where `α_p(k) = #{(x, h) ∈ (Z/p)^{k+1} : x + ε·h ≢ 0 mod p ∀ε ∈ {0,1}^k} / p^{k+1}`. Numerical truncated values: **S_2 = 2.300938** (P_max=100, 1e-5 truncation error), **S_3 = 54.116088** (P_max=47, 0.2% error). **Empirical Q^2(chi_P) trend**: 2.103 (N=2^10) → 2.132 (N=2^12) → 2.146 (N=2^14) → 2.149 (N=2^16) → 2.153 (N=2^18); monotonic increase toward S_2; gap S_2 - Q^2(chi_P) decays as O(1/log N) (from 0.198 at N=2^10 to 0.148 at N=2^18). **Q^2(Bernoulli) ~ 1 + O(1/N)** (1.049 → 1.008 across same range). **Q^3(chi_P) ≈ 35.5** stable across N ∈ [2^10, 2^13], well above Bernoulli's Q^3 → 1 but below S_3 = 54.12 due to slow finite-N convergence at U^3 (compounded log-factors). **Liouville**: Q^2(L) = 1.000 to 4 digits at N=2^10..2^12 — Gowers-uniform at U^2, consistent with Green-Tao Mobius/nilsequence orthogonality (the prime-parity indicator is structurally OPPOSITE to chi_P at U^k). **W-trick**: predicted `S^(W)_k = Π_{p∤W} α_p(k)/(1-1/p)^{2^k}`. | C4 (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — Aggarwal × Dusart × BPSW unified p_n library | REFINEMENT | E | Composition of E6.6 (Aggarwal binary search optimality) + E6.8 (Dusart bracket of width n) + E5.1 (BPSW as TC^0 primality oracle). Built three modes: `agg` (Aggarwal binary search on `pi_lucy(x)`, no BPSW), `bpsw` (BPSW-walk from 2, no Dusart), `hybrid` (Aggarwal narrow-to-K then BPSW-walk in `[L, R]`). All three agree with `sympy.prime(n)` at n ∈ {1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 10, 25, 50, 100, 168, 169, 500, 1000, 10⁴, 10⁵, 10⁶, 10⁷}. Wall-clock benchmark @ pure-Python `pi_lucy`: at n = 10⁶ agg = 379 ms, bpsw = 15.27 s, hybrid (K=64) = 291 ms. **F1 (agreement) HOLDS**; **F3 (hybrid ≥ 10× bpsw at n ≥ 10⁴) HOLDS** at 21×/34×/53× across n ∈ {10⁴, 10⁵, 10⁶}; **F2 (hybrid ≥ 1.5× agg at n ≥ 10⁵) PARTIALLY FAILS** in pure-Python regime (only 1.30× at n = 10⁶) but HOLDS at 1.64× when `pi_lucy` is C-accelerated; **F4 (U-shape in K) FAILS** in pure-Python regime — the K-sweep at n = 10⁶ is monotone-decreasing in time, optimum is K = bracket-width (composition collapses to E6.8 + E5.1 with no Aggarwal narrowing); **F4 HOLDS** under C-accelerated `pi_lucy`: K-sweep at n = 10⁷ shows U-shape with K* ≈ 16384 ≈ √(bracket width), inflating to 0.49 s at K = bracket width. **Net new content**: (a) **the optimal K depends on the `pi(x) / BPSW` cost ratio**, a knob invisible at asymptotic-order analysis (Aggarwal 2025) — Python `pi_lucy` regime: K* = width; C-Lucy: K* ≈ √width; HKM/primecount projection: K* ≈ small constant ⇒ Aggarwal-pure dominates, BPSW adds nothing; (b) **BPSW conditionality propagates 1-to-1 through Aggarwal's wrapper**, not amplified — a single BPSW pseudoprime in the final `[L, R]` shifts the answer by exactly one prime; (c) the **Dusart bracket alone is worth ~50× over naive BPSW-from-2**, matching predicted `2 p_n / n ~ 2 log p_n` at n = 10⁴-10⁷. **Closure mode E** (equivalence): hybrid is a constant-factor re-arrangement of Aggarwal's `O(sqrt(n) log⁴ n)` bound, not an asymptotic improvement; the BPSW conditional is the only non-trivial composition content. No polylog opening — the bottleneck remains `pi(x)` evaluation at HKM/Lucy cost. Cites E5.1, E6.6, E6.8 (annotated). Cross-domain ingredient: none (all three components were already in the project's catalogue). See `algorithms/aggarwal_dusart_bpsw/`. | 120 |
| C7 (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — calibrated 1-bit-bias random control for the S84 PRIMES-vs-random depth-2 sign-threshold gap | REFINEMENT | C | Composition of E1.10 / E3.13 (35-measure pseudorandomness battery, `novel/pseudorandomness_of_pi.md`) + S84 result (PRIMES min M=6 vs unbiased matched-density random min M ∈ {7,8} at N=6, binomial p < 0.001). S84 conjectured the gap was driven by PRIMES's 1-bit-predictor advantage (bit_0 = "is x odd" gives 75.0% accuracy on PRIMES at N=6, vs ~66% for unbiased random of weight 18/64). C7 builds the matched-class-conditional random control `f_cal` on {0..63}: P(f_cal=1 \| x odd) = 17/32 (exactly matching PRIMES's 17 odd primes); P(f_cal=1 \| x even) = 1/32 (exactly matching PRIMES's 1 even prime, namely 2). Two modes: STRATIFIED (exact 17 odd + 1 even, weight always 18, bit_0_acc always 0.75) and BERNOULLI (independent draws; variable weight, variable bit_0_acc 0.625-0.81). 20 samples each through S84's `enum_d2_smart` ILP harness (K=1458 W=1 candidates, CBC, 120s/cell). **Pre-stated falsifiers (4 outcomes)**: F1 (≥1 stratified > 6 → residual structure), F2 (all stratified ≤ 6 → gap is oddness), F3 (≥1 stratified < 6 → calibration overshoots), F4 (stratified mean < unbiased mean → bit_0 primary driver). **Result: F2 + F3 + F4 HOLD; F1 FAILS.** Stratified histogram = {5: 4, 6: 16}, mean 5.80, max 6 — 0/20 above PRIMES; 4/20 *strictly easier* than PRIMES at M=5. Bernoulli histogram = {5: 7, 6: 11, 7: 2} — both M=7 cases have bit_0_acc < 0.75 (PRIMES's value: 0.7344 and 0.7188), empirically confirming the proposed monotonic relationship `bit_0_acc ↑ ⇒ min_M ↓` at fixed weight. PRIMES sits at +0.5σ of the calibrated distribution; under the calibrated null `P(M ≤ 6) = 1.0` (vs unbiased null `P(M ≤ 6) = 0/10 → 0.5^10 ≈ 0.001`). **The S84 PRIMES-vs-random gap REDUCES TO elementary parity** ("π(x) ≈ 1 iff x odd, for x > 2"); no PRIMES-specific structure beyond oddness. **Sub-finding** (Bernoulli stratified by bit_0_acc bins): bit_0_acc ≥ 0.78 → mean min_M = 5.50; [0.75, 0.78) → 5.67; [0.72, 0.75) → 6.40 (the M=7 region); < 0.72 → 5.00 (low-weight compensation). The (bit_0_acc, weight, min_M) surface is the structural explanation. **Closure mode C** (circular): the depth-2 gap is fully captured by an elementary fact already in the project's frame (bit_0 "free first gate" mechanism, S84 §"Mechanism: single-bit predictor advantage"); calibration formally verifies the explanation. Recommends footnote on `novel/pseudorandomness_of_pi.md`: 36th measure (depth-2 sign-threshold) DEVIATES from unbiased random but the deviation is fully captured by the trivial 1-bit oddness fact; pseudorandomness thesis stands "modulo the obvious mod-2 bias." **Net new content**: (a) verified S84 mechanism (bit_0 → depth-2 size) under direct calibrated control; (b) new mechanism prediction empirically confirmed (bit_0_acc-vs-min_M monotonic at fixed weight); (c) PRIMES-vs-calibrated indistinguishability removes the only known deviation in the 35+ measure battery from "structural" to "elementary parity" status. Spawned successor C7.a (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — N=8 calibrated extension. Cites E1.10, E3.13, E1.6, E1.4, S84. See experiments/circuit_complexity/sat_tc0_primes_n8_calibrated/. | 89 |
| §C4 ATTACK_VECTORS — Anderson localisation Lyapunov exponent gamma(E) of chi_P-driven discrete 1D Schrödinger operator (cross-domain: Aizenman-Warzel random operators, Furstenberg-Kifer theorem) | FAIL | E | Frontier attack on whether chi_P deviates from random pseudorandomness on a SPECTRAL-GLOBAL statistic (Lyapunov exponent) — orthogonal to all 36 prior pseudorandomness measures (which are local correlation / entropy / Fourier / tensor rank / Gowers). Setup: H psi(n) = -psi(n+1) - psi(n-1) + V(n)psi(n), transfer matrix T_n = [[V(n)-E, -1],[1,0]] in SL(2,R), gamma(E) = lim (1/N) log ||T_N..T_1||. Pastur-Figotin perturbation for sparse V in {0,1}: gamma(E) ~ rho(1-rho)/(8 sin^2 k), E = -2 cos k in (-2, 2). Vectorised numerical Lyapunov estimator with periodic L^2 renormalisation, O(N) per energy. **Naive Bernoulli baseline at N=10^5, 50 seeds, 51 energies**: gamma_prime(E) deviates from gamma_bern_mean(E) by max |z| = 88.5 sigma at E = 0.108 (k = pi/2 parity resonance). Z-score grows as sqrt(N): 12.3 at N=10^4 → 88.5 at N=10^5. **Confounder: chi_P concentrated on odd indices.** Parity-matched controls C1 (random subset of odd indices size pi(N)-1 plus V(2)=1) and C3 (chi_P shuffled within odd indices) reduce deviation but still leave 33 sigma at E=1.088 ~ -2 cos(2pi/3) = +1, the **mod-3 resonance** (free transfer matrix rotates 2pi/3 per step, maximally couples to mod-3 periodic potentials; chi_P has mod-3 structure since primes >3 are +-1 mod 3). **W-trick cascade** (random potential supported on {n : gcd(n, W) = 1}, plus delta at small primes p|W): max |z| = 33 (W=2) → 12 (W=6) → 6.3 (W=30) → 6.1 (W=210) → 4.0 (W=2310, N=2*10^5). Cascade ends at residual ~ 4 sigma, borderline noise (Bonferroni 0.05/31 → z_thresh ≈ 3.16). **Same structural reason as E2.13/S85** (Gowers U^k of chi_P → Hardy-Littlewood singular series, recovered by W-trick): chi_P deviation is fully captured by small-modulus residue-class structure. Adds new EDGE E2.14: gamma(E) of Schrödinger operator with V=chi_P deviates from random Lyapunov by a quantity that W-tricks down to noise. **Closure mode E**: deviation IS the spectral signature of Hardy-Littlewood / Bombieri-Vinogradov equidistribution mod q; no additional spectral structure beyond what equidistribution already gives; algorithmic gamma extraction costs Theta(N), not polylog. **Cross-domain ingredient performed real work**: transfer-matrix product / Lyapunov machinery (Aizenman-Warzel 2015 *Random Operators*, Furstenberg-Kifer 1983) had never been applied to chi_P; the spectral signature of the W-trick at the Anderson-Lyapunov level is novel. **PROVIDES SECOND INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION** (after E2.13/S85) that chi_P structure = HL equidistribution mod q, in a category orthogonal to additive combinatorics. Cites E2.13, E1.10, E3.13, E7.1. Cross-domain refs: Aizenman-Warzel 2015 (AMS GSM 168); Furstenberg-Kifer 1983 Israel J. Math. 46; Pastur-Figotin 1992; Green-Tao arXiv:math/0606088 (W-trick). See experiments/dynamical/anderson_localisation_chi_p/anderson_localisation_chi_p_results.md. | 88 |
| §B1 ATTACK_VECTORS — slice rank / Croot-Lev-Pach polynomial method on chi_P, via algebraic immunity AI(chi_P) over F_2 (cross-domain: cap-set polynomial method, Tao slice rank, Courtois-Meier algebraic immunity) | FAIL | E | Frontier attack on whether the cap-set polynomial-method machinery yields a polylog primality witness via low-degree annihilators of chi_P. Three structurally distinct invariants: (1) algebraic immunity AI(chi_P) over F_2 (smallest deg g with g·chi_P==0 or g·(1+chi_P)==0); (2) F_p multilinear ANF degree on (F_p)^k via base-p expansion; (3) slice rank brackets via Tao 2016 flattening lower bound + greedy upper bound. **Main empirical finding**: AI(chi_P, N) = 2 for ALL N in [4, 13] while AI(matched-density Bernoulli) grows as Theta(log_2(1/rho)) — reaching 4 at N=11/12 with zero std. Liouville+ AI grows like random; Mobius!=0 also has constant AI=2. **Mechanism (extracted via nullspace recovery)**: the unique smallest annihilator is the SAME polynomial g(x) = (1 + x_0)(1 + x_1) for every N >= 5, which equals 1 iff n ≡ 0 mod 4. The annihilation works because no prime > 2 is divisible by 4 (and chi_P(2) gets killed by bit_1=1). AI(chi_P)=2 is *exactly* the polynomial-method encoding of the trivial mod-4 sieve fact; same g annihilates Mobius!=0 because squarefree numbers exclude multiples of 4. Liouville+ does NOT inherit this since lambda(4)=+1. **W-trick correction CONFIRMS deviation is mod-q structure**: at W=6, b∈{1,5} and W=30, b∈{1,7,11}, AI(chi_P_{W,b}) tracks AI(random) within ±1 across N=8..11; many cells exact match with zero std (e.g. W=6 b=1 N=11: chi_P=5, random=5.00 std 0). **F_p multilinear ANF degree**: chi_P near-saturates max possible degree (p-1)·k at all tested (p,k) other than (3,3) where deg drops 1 below random max, and (5,3) similar single-degree drop. **Slice rank brackets**: at p=2 non-informative (2-row flattenings cap rank at 2 trivially, both chi_P and random); at p=3 chi_P matches random for k>=3. **Same structural reason as E2.13 (Gowers, S85), E2.14 (Anderson, S88)**: chi_P deviation is fully captured by small-modulus residue-class structure; the polynomial-method invariant gives no additional bit beyond mod-q. **Adds EDGE E2.15**: AI_F_2(chi_P) = 2 for all N >= 4 with explicit annihilator (1+x_0)(1+x_1); W-trick at W>=6 removes deviation. THIRD INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION (after E2.13/S85 Gowers and E2.14/S88 Anderson) that chi_P structure = HL equidistribution mod q, now in a third orthogonal mathematical category (Boolean polynomial method / algebraic cryptanalysis). **Closure mode E**: AI deviation IS the polynomial-method encoding of the mod-4 sieve fact; no additional polynomial-method content beyond mod-q. AI extraction itself is exponential time in N (LP over 2^N x sum_i C(N,i) F_2 monomial-evaluation matrix), not polylog. **Cross-domain ingredient performed real work**: Croot-Lev-Pach / Tao slice rank / Courtois-Meier algebraic immunity machinery had never been applied to chi_P; the polynomial-method signature of the W-trick at AI level is novel. Cites E2.13, E2.14, E1.10, E3.13. Cross-domain refs: Croot-Lev-Pach arXiv:1605.01506; Tao 2016 blog (slice rank); Ellenberg-Gijswijt 2017 Annals 185, 339; Courtois-Meier 2003 Eurocrypt LNCS 2656; Green-Tao arXiv:math/0606088 (W-trick). See experiments/algebraic/algebraic_immunity_chi_p/algebraic_immunity_chi_p_results.md. | 92 |
| §D7 ATTACK_VECTORS — Determinantal point process (DPP) / permanantal counterpart fit to integer prime sequence (cross-domain: DPP theory, Hough-Krishnapur-Peres-Virag 2009, Soshnikov 2000, Vere-Jones 1997 alpha-DPP) | FAIL | I | Frontier attack on whether chi_P admits a translation-invariant kernel representation R_2(t) = rho^2 ± K(t)^2 (DPP -, PPP +). Tested four progressively-flexible kernel hypotheses at N = 10^7. **F1 (DPP pair-level)**: K^2_DPP(t) = rho^2 - R_2(t) < 0 at ALL 15 admissible even t in [2, 30]; HL gives S(0, t) > 1 ⇒ R_2 > rho^2. Pair-level DPP infeasible. **F2 (PPP pair-level)**: K^2_PPP(t) = R_2(t) - rho^2 < 0 at ALL 14 odd t > 1 (R_2 ≈ 0). Pair-level PPP infeasible. Sign of (R_2 - rho^2) flips between odd/even — no real translation-invariant K. **F3 (PPP 3-point)** restricting to all-even sub-lattice where K^2_PPP > 0: permanent prediction R_3^PPP overshoots HL prediction R_3^HL = rho^3 S(0, t_1, t_2) by 7.5–79.2% across 19 admissible triples (max gap on 3-AP triples (0,6,12), (0,12,18), (0,18,24)). **F4 (real-signed K)**: with magnitudes |K(t)| = rho sqrt(S(0,t)-1) and signs s : t → ±1, required cross-term σ_req ∈ (-0.541, +0.769), NEVER ±1 (closest 0.769) at any of 19 admissible triples. Real signed K of right magnitudes fails universally. **F5 (complex Hermitian K)**: phases free; least-squares fit of φ over 13 distinct offsets (200 random starts, LM + trust-region) yields best max residual 0.0746 ≫ 0.01 noise floor. No globally consistent phase assignment. **Inadmissibility blindness**: PPP predicts non-zero R_3 for inadmissible triples like (0, 4, 14) (where ν_3 = 3 saturates) because pair correlations alone cannot detect multi-body admissibility. **Mechanism**: HL singular series factorises over PRIMES (∏_p α_p(0, t_1, ..., t_k) with α_p depending on ν_p = #distinct residues mod p); DPP/PPP correlations factorise over PAIRS. Pairwise admissibility ≠ triple admissibility. **Adds EDGE E2.16**: primes are NOT a translation-invariant DPP / PPP / signed-real-K / complex-Hermitian-K point process. FOURTH INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION (after E2.13/S85 Gowers, E2.14/S88 Anderson, E2.15/S92 algebraic immunity) that chi_P structure = HL equidistribution mod q, now in a FOURTH orthogonal mathematical category (point-process theory) AND at the 3-point level (prior three were 2-point). **Closure mode I**: HL prime-by-prime factorisation cannot reduce to pair-level kernel. Cross-domain ingredient (DPP) performed real work; first project use. Cites E2.13, E2.14, E2.15, E1.10, E3.13, E7.1. Cross-domain refs: Hough-Krishnapur-Peres-Virag 2009 *Zeros of GAFs and DPPs* (AMS ULect 51); Soshnikov 2000 (arXiv:math/0002099); Vere-Jones 1997 alpha-DPP. See experiments/constructions/primes_dpp_ppp_fit/primes_dpp_ppp_fit_results.md. | 95 |

| §D2 ATTACK_VECTORS — Persistent homology of Takens-embedded normalised prime gap sequence (cross-domain: persistent homology / Vietoris-Rips filtration, Carlsson 2009 BAMS, Edelsbrunner-Harer 2010, Bauer 2021 ripser arXiv:1908.02518) | FAIL | I | Frontier attack on whether the prime gap sequence carries persistent topological features distinguishable from a matched-density Poisson process. Protocol: M=2000 consecutive normalised gaps x_n = (p_{n+1}-p_n)/log(p_n) starting near p ≈ 10^6 (sieve to N_max=10^7); Takens-embed at delay 1 in d ∈ {2, 3, 4}; ripser persistent H_0 / H_1 with thresh ∈ {4.0, 5.0}; baselines K=20 each: B1 IID Exp(1), B2 random permutation of empirical window (preserves marginal, kills serial correlation). Pre-registered F3 (PRIMES > 3σ from BOTH B1 AND B2 on at least one summary) HOLDS: at d=3, M=2000, x≈10^6: T0 (total H_0 persistence) z(B1) = -10.31, z(B2) = -8.70 (rank 0/20 in both); T1 (total H_1 persistence) z(B1) = -4.20, z(B2) = -11.99 (rank 0/20). Effect robust at second window x ≈ 5·10^6 (T0 z(B2) = -7.58, T1 z(B2) = -8.69) and across d ∈ {2, 3, 4} (T0 z(B2) ∈ [-8.7, -5.1]). Scaling sweep M ∈ {500, 1000, 2000, 4000} shows z-scores grow super-linearly (M=4000 gives T0 z(B1) = -17.8) — signal at least linear in window size. **Mechanism:** primes are MORE clustered (smaller T0) and have FEWER persistent loops (smaller T1) than Poisson-gap and gap-permuted controls. The B2 control isolates serial correlation; T0/T1 deficit relative to B2 is the HL k-tuple admissibility component (consecutive gaps repeat residue patterns more often than random, creating geometric self-similarity in delay-embedding cloud). **Adds EDGE E2.17**: PH of prime gap delay embedding deviates ≥ 5σ from IID Exp(1) AND from gap-marginal-permuted control across multiple windows, dimensions, and sample sizes. FIFTH INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION (after E2.13 Gowers, E2.14 Anderson, E2.15 algebraic immunity, E2.16 DPP failure) of HL equidistribution structure in chi_P, now in a FIFTH orthogonal mathematical category (algebraic-topological / metric-space geometry). **Closure mode I**: PH is a measurement instrument, not an algorithm — no closed-form polylog evaluator suggested; VR-PH costs O(M^3) worst-case. Cross-domain ingredient (PH on Takens embedding via ripser) performed real work; first project quantitative measurement (S10 mention was qualitative, gtda-based, no baseline). Cites E2.13, E2.14, E2.15, E2.16, E7.7. Cross-domain refs: Carlsson 2009 BAMS 46(2); Edelsbrunner-Harer 2010 *Computational Topology*; Bauer 2021 J. Appl. Comput. Topol. 5 (arXiv:1908.02518); Cohen-Steiner-Edelsbrunner-Harer 2007 (PH stability). See experiments/topological/persistent_homology_chi_p/persistent_homology_chi_p_results.md. | 96 |

J. Math.* 60) | FAIL | E | Frontier attack on whether the prime indicator binary word w = chi_P(2)chi_P(3)... has subword complexity p_w(n) := #{distinct length-n factors of w} that deviates from matched-density Bernoulli — the canonical combinatorial-on-words test (no published value for chi_P; Wikipedia "Complexity function"). Promotes the informal CLOSED_PATHS line 181 "Symbolic dynamics — near-random block complexity (S4)" to a precise quantitative measurement with finite-N value, K=20 baselines, and full W-trick cascade. Protocol: sieve to N = 5·10^6 (348,513 primes); five chi_P-derived streams — RAW (chi_P(2..N), L=4,999,999, density 0.0697); ODD (chi_P(2k+1), L=2,499,999, density 0.139); W6 (chi_P(6n+1), L=833,334, density 0.209); W30 (chi_P(30n+1), L=166,667, density 0.261); W210 (chi_P(210n+1), L=23,810, density 0.305). Per stream, n ∈ [1, 22] vectorised rolling-encoding factor count (numpy uint64; cost O(nL), memory O(L)); K=20 random permutations of the stream (B2: preserves density and 1-marginal exactly, kills all >1-gram structure) and K=20 iid Bernoulli matched-density samples (B1). Pre-registered F3 (PRIMES > 3σ from BOTH B1 AND B2 at every n in [n_lo, n_hi] with n_hi ≥ 18, on at least one of {ODD, W6, W30}) HOLDS at z >> 3σ for ODD (max |z_perm| = 277 at n=7), W6 (|z_perm| = 121 at n=8), and W30 (|z_perm| = 25 at n=17). Cascade |z_perm| at peak across streams: RAW 132.7 → ODD 277.1 → W6 120.5 → W30 24.8 → W210 8.4 — clean monotone reduction by ~1.5 orders of magnitude across W ∈ {1, 6, 30, 210}. Ratio p_chi(22) / p_perm_mean(22): RAW 0.018 (98% deficit, parity-driven), ODD 0.216 (78% deficit, mod-3 in odd-stream), W6 0.806 (19% deficit), W30 0.994, W210 1.011 (≈ noise; sign flips above n=17 indicating saturation). Same Hardy-Littlewood k-tuple admissibility engine that drives E2.13 Gowers, E2.14 Anderson, E2.16 DPP failure, E2.17 PH. Residual |z_perm| = 8.4 at W=210, n=12 is consistent with `O(1/log N)` HL singular-series tail. **Adds EDGE E2.19**: subword complexity of chi_P deviates ≥ 100σ from Bernoulli matched-density on raw/W6 streams; W=210 W-trick reduces to ≤ 8.4σ at finite L = 2.4·10^4. **38th pseudorandomness measure**, **SIXTH orthogonal HL-detection family** (after E2.13 Gowers, E2.14 Anderson, E2.15 algebraic immunity, E2.16 DPP, E2.17 PH), in a NEW mathematical category (symbolic-dynamics / factor-complexity / topological-entropy). **Closure mode E**: subword complexity at given window length is O(L log L) — no polylog opening; W-trick fingerprint encodes only HL information already known. **Cross-domain ingredient (factor complexity / topological entropy of binary words) performed real work**: first project quantitative use; status in CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md §5 upgraded PROPOSED → USED E with edge E2.19. | §G1 ATTACK_VECTORS — Liouville Anderson Lyapunov: spectral signature of Möbius pseudorandomness (cross-domain: Anderson localisation [reused S88] + Möbius/nilsequence orthogonality, Green-Tao 2012 *Annals* 175 = arXiv:0807.1736; Sarnak 2010 IAS lectures; Tao 2016 logarithmic-Chowla = arXiv:1509.05422) | FAIL | E | First wild-swing attack into the multiplicative regime beyond the W-trick wall. Pastur-Figotin prediction in-band collapses to gamma_PF(E) = 1/(8 sin^2 k), exactly matching i.i.d. Rademacher ±1 baseline. Question: any sustained |z| > 5 deviation gamma_lambda(E) - gamma_Rademacher(E) at any energy, NOT W-tricked away — would be A-grade Möbius-side spectral non-orthogonality. **Empirical at 51 energies in E ∈ [-2.95, 2.95], three sample sizes: max |z| = 1.78 (N=10^5, 50 seeds, argmax E=-0.236), 2.16 (N=3·10^5, 50 seeds, argmax +0.118), 2.04 (N=10^6, 100 seeds, argmax -2.006).** All below 51-energy Bonferroni threshold z = 3.16. Argmax energy WANDERS between runs (statistical, not arithmetic). chi^2 / K = 0.49-0.69 (Rademacher prediction = 1; lambda is sub-Rademacher). L^2 distance from Rademacher mean: lambda lies at empirical p ≈ {0.58, 0.86, 0.59} of the seed-to-seed Rademacher distribution. Pastur-Figotin agreement: gamma_lambda / gamma_PF = 0.9317 (std 0.32) inside band, identical to gamma_Rademacher_mean / gamma_PF = 0.9309 to 4 decimals. **Independent off-spectral check (two-point Chowla aggregate at N=10^6, h=1..16): Σ z_h^2 = 4.77 vs Rademacher chi^2_16 mean 16, std sqrt(32) ≈ 5.7** — empirical p ≈ 0.997, lambda is *more Rademacher-like than Rademacher* on Chowla. All 16 individual lag z's |z_h| ≤ 1.11. **Stark contrast with E2.14 (chi_P, S88) at same N=10^5 grid: chi_P max |z| = 88.5σ at locked E=0.108 (parity resonance), required W=2310 sieve to reduce to ~4σ; lambda needs no W-trick at all and |z| flat across two orders of N magnitude.** **Mechanism (structural reason for closure mode E):** Möbius/nilsequence orthogonality (Green-Tao 2012, arXiv:0807.1736) predicts lambda is asymptotically orthogonal to all nilsequences including periodic ones; Lyapunov exponent of det-1 transfer-matrix Markov chain on PSL(2,R) is determined by Furstenberg-Kifer measure, a non-abelian analogue of GT nilsequence framework. lambda's centered multiplicativity destroys the mod-q resonance that drives chi_P's 88σ deviation. Sub-Rademacher Chowla aggregate (Σ z² = 4.77 ≪ 16) is consistent with Tao 2016 logarithmic-Chowla theorem (arXiv:1509.05422). **First non-W-tricked spectral measurement** in the project's 38-measure pseudorandomness battery to land at noise floor without sieving. **Pairs with E2.14 to ISOLATE the source of chi_P's Anderson-Lyapunov deviation as exclusively HL singular-series mod-q resonance:** lambda is the canonical chi_P-twin (density 1/2, centered, multiplicative) without mod-q resonance, so it has no spectral signal. **Closes the intended polylog opening:** if lambda-Lyapunov had detectable structure, the explicit-formula μ-side Σ Li(x^ρ) μ_ρ could plausibly be partial-summed in polylog. lambda is featureless ⇒ explicit-formula Möbius side is irreducible at this measurement scale. **Adds EDGE E2.18**: gamma_lambda(E) ≈ gamma_Rademacher(E) within seed noise across [-3, 3], flat in N up to 10^6, no W-trick needed. **Cross-domain ingredient (Möbius/nilsequence orthogonality) performed real work**: first project use; status in CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md upgraded UNUSED → USED E. Cites E2.14, E2.13, E1.10, E3.13. Cross-domain refs: Aizenman-Warzel 2015 *Random Operators* AMS GSM 168; Furstenberg-Kifer 1983; Pastur-Figotin 1992; Green-Tao arXiv:0807.1736 (Möbius nilsequence orthogonality, primary); Sarnak 2010 IAS lectures (Möbius randomness conjecture); Tao 2016 Forum Math Pi 4 e8 = arXiv:1509.05422 (logarithmic-Chowla); Tao-Teräväinen 2017 arXiv:1708.02610. See experiments/dynamical/liouville_anderson_lyapunov/liouville_anderson_lyapunov_results.md. | 100 |
| C3 (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES §1) — Brandt obstructions × per-bit difficulty: per-bit family `{π_J : J = 0..N-1}` under bounded-Kt | REFINEMENT | E | Composition of E5.8 (Brandt-class barrier on π(x) mod 2 — `MKtP not in DTIME[O(n)]` from ePrint 2024/687, S51 closure) + E1.3 (per-bit difficulty profile of π(x), S28 — Fourier weight / influence / R-correlation cleanly stratify bits at boundary J ≈ 0.5N). Defined π_J(x) := bit J of π(x), s_J^(N) := (π_J(0), ..., π_J(2^N - 1)) ∈ {0,1}^(2^N), and the per-bit family F_N = {s_J^(N) : J = 0..N-1}. Imported brandt_mktp.py's bounded-Kt simulator (3-bit-per-op stack VM, ops {PUSH 0, PUSH 1, DUP, FLIP, RPT2, RPT4, ALT, HALT}, L_MAX = 12, T_MAX = 4096, INF = 61). Computed Kt_b(s_J^(N)) for N ∈ {3..7}, J ∈ [0, N-1]. **Pre-stated falsifiers (3 outcomes)**: F1 (uniform saturation → DUPLICATE-PLUS), F2 (J-stratified at E1.3's 0.5N cut), F3 (anomalous). **Result: F2 holds in the meaningful saturation regime N ≥ 4** (N=3 truth-tables are 8 bits long, below VM's saturation threshold and consistent but uninformative). Crucial sharpening: the bounded-Kt cut location is at **J*(N) := ⌈log₂(π(2^N - 1) + 1)⌉ ≈ N − log₂ N**, NOT at E1.3's 0.5N. For J ≥ J*(N), π_J ≡ 0 on [0, 2^N) and Kt_b ∈ {5..8} (program: RPT4 → 4-zeros-per-step). For J < J*(N) — including J's in E1.3's "easy zone" J ∈ [⌈0.5N⌉, J*) — Kt_b = INF = 61 (saturated). Concrete examples: N=5 J=3 (13/32 ones, R-correlated) saturates; N=6 J=3 (34/64 ones, R-correlated) saturates; N=7 J=4 (75/128 ones) saturates. Numerical cuts: N=4: J*=3 vs 0.5N=2 (gap +1); N=5: J*=4 vs 2.5 (gap +1.5); N=6: J*=5 vs 3 (gap +2); N=7: J*=5 vs 3.5 (gap +1.5). The bounded VM is **blind to E1.3's smooth/oscillatory transition** at the Fourier cut — it only resolves the *trivially-zero* boundary; bits in E1.3's "easy zone" but below J* saturate exactly like hard-zone bits. **Structural extension argument** (independent of empirical data): the four Brandt obstructions O1-O4 still apply to every fixed π_J in the per-bit family. (O1) Each π_J is a fixed total Boolean function; per-bit decomposition is parameter-controlled but each π_J is not oracle-dependent. (O2) π_J answers "is bit J of π(x) set", not "is z complicated"; no self-referential Kt inequality. (O3) The per-bit family supplies J*(N) ≈ N − log₂ N strings per N, but they are FIXED, not traversal-path-dependent fresh prefixes — Brandt's Lemma 2 needs an infinite supply of growing-length Kt-random prefixes generated on demand by oracle access, not a finite stack of pre-determined strings. (O4) Uniform DTIME bound, not circuit. The "minimal weakening of O1 admitting Brandt's TRAVERSE" the C3 spec asked about does NOT exist on {π_J : J = 0..N-1}. **Closure mode E** (equivalence): per-bit decomposition is structurally orthogonal to Brandt's diagonalisation skeleton; C3 reduces to E5.8 wholesale at the structural level. **Empirical novelty** (the only new content beyond E5.8 + E1.3): bounded-Kt cut location J*(N) ≈ N − log₂ N is a quantitative sharpening of E1.3's Fourier cut, expressing the limit of what stack-VM-class programs can resolve about per-bit difficulty. Detecting the smooth/oscillatory transition at the Kt level would require a richer VM with arithmetic primitives (LOG2, LI_APPROX, DIV_LOG, GEO_SUM — the R^{-1} kernel) — proposed as successor C3.a. **Cross-domain ingredient**: Brandt 2024 ePrint 2024/687 bounded-Kt simulator (3-bit stack VM), reused from S51. **Adds new finite-N empirical fact** (not previously measured): per-bit Kt-bounded cut at J*(N) ≈ N − log₂ N, gap to E1.3's 0.5N cut grows as 0.5N − log₂ N. Cites E5.8, E1.3, E1.9, E5.3. See experiments/constructions/brandt_per_bit/. | 105 |
| §A5 ATTACK_VECTORS — Maynard 2015 multidimensional sieve weight w(n) = (Σ_{d_i\|n+h_i, gcd=1, ∏d_i≤R} μ(d_1)…μ(d_k) F(log d_i/log R))^2 as TC^0 primality witness (cross-domain: Maynard 2015 *Annals* 181 / Polymath8b 2014 / Pascadi 2025) | FAIL | E | Wild-swing attack S116 on the most refined explicit prime-detection sieve in modern analytic NT, framed as: is `w(n) > τ*` a pointwise primality witness AND is w(n) polylog-evaluable, so that PRIMES ∈ TC⁰ unconditionally? Implementation: `experiments/sieve/maynard_weight_pointwise/`. k=3, H=admissible {0,2,6} (also tested {0,4,6} and {0,2,6,8,12}); F ∈ {(1-Σx_i)^2 GPY, (1-Σx_i) linear, constant=1, Maynard-symmetric (1-Σx)^2(1+0.5Σx+2Σx_ix_j)}; θ ∈ {0.10,0.15,0.20,0.25,0.30,0.35,0.40} (R = N^θ); N ∈ {10^4, 10^4.5, 10^5, 10^5.5, 10^6}. **Pre-registered targets**: A-grade requires AUC restricted to odd n ≥ 0.95 AND mean ops/eval = polylog(N). Both fail. **Pointwise-AUC table (selberg_gpy F)**: at θ ≤ 0.20 with R<6, AUC_all ranges 0.84–0.90 — but stratification by parity reveals that 0.22 of the AUC is *parity detection* (H={0,2,6} share parity ⇒ even n window has zero primes). AUC restricted to odd n is in [0.66, 0.69] across all θ. Best F1 = 0.62 with selberg_gpy. The maynard_sym F (with 2-parameter symmetric correction) is no better — eigenvalue ratio M_3/I_3 = 1.515 controls aggregate, not pointwise. **Aggregate-positivity replicated**: max ratio Σw(n)χ_P(n+h_i)/Σw(n) = 0.094–0.212 across θ — Maynard's theorem holds at finite N. The empirical pointwise/aggregate gap is DIRECTLY the structural fact that `Σ w·χ_P > c·Σ w` does NOT propagate to single-n separation. **Op-count scaling**: mean coprime simplex tuples ∝ N^α for α ∈ {0.10, 0.11, 0.12} at θ ∈ {0.20, 0.30, 0.40} on N ∈ [10^4, 10^6] — sub-θ but POSITIVE; not polylog. Worst-case (p99) tuple count grows with same exponent. Divisor enumeration up to R = N^θ is the fundamental sub-routine and reduces to growing-dim modular powering — exactly E5.3. **Mechanism for closure mode E**: Maynard's main theorem is `Σ w·χ_P ≥ c·Σ w` (averaged); pointwise content of w(n) is bounded by aggregate-vs-pointwise loss factor that depends on the F-eigenvalue ratio, not on F-shape. Empirical AUC_oddN ≤ 0.69 across all θ confirms this loss is irreducible; maynard_sym attempt did not improve over selberg_gpy. **Refines E6.7 (sieve pebbling)** by closing the most refined Selberg-sieve descendant; **reduces to E5.3 (MPOW)** via divisor-enumeration cost; **closes the project's only open problem (PRIMES in TC⁰) along the sieve-route attack family**, completing the four-family closure {AKS-modulus E7.10, Brandt MKtP E5.8, convergence-acceleration E7.11, **Maynard sieve E7.14**}. **Cross-domain ingredient (multidim GPY-Maynard sieve) performed real work**: first project quantitative use; status in CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md upgraded PROPOSED → USED E with edge E7.14. **Adds EDGE E7.14**: Maynard 2015 multidim weight is aggregate-not-pointwise (AUC_oddN ∈ [0.66, 0.69]) AND requires Ω(N^{θ-o(1)}) divisor-enumeration cost per single n (sub-poly, not polylog) for any θ > 0 with non-trivial weight. Cites E6.7, E5.3, E7.10. Cross-domain refs: Maynard 2015 *Annals* 181:383 = arXiv:1311.4600; Polymath8b 2014 arXiv:1407.4897; Pascadi 2025 (E3.12). See experiments/sieve/maynard_weight_pointwise/maynard_weight_pointwise_results.md. | 116 |
| §D2.a (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — PH of W=210 W-tricked normalised prime gaps: does the W-trick erase the E2.17 PH deficit? | REFINEMENT | E | Composition of E2.13 (Gowers W=210 uniformity at U^2 within 0.1%) + E2.17 (PH of Takens-embedded Cramér-normalised prime gaps deviates ≥ 5σ from gap-marginal-permuted control B2 at M=2000, d=3, x≈10⁶: T0 z(B2) = -8.70, T1 z(B2) = -11.99). Filter primes to single residue class b mod W with W=210, b ∈ {1, 11, 13}, gcd(b, 210) = 1 (φ(W) = 48). Cramér-normalise as `x_n = g_n / (φ(W) log q_n)`. Run identical Takens (τ=1) + Vietoris-Rips PH (ripser, thresh=4 at d=3, thresh=5 at d=4) pipeline as S96. **Pre-registered F_a (W-trick erases all PH deviation): pooled \|z\| within ±3 for both T0 and T1 against both B1 (IID Exp(1)) AND B2 (gap-permuted).** F_b (≥50% reduction with at least one residual). F_c (within 25% of S96). **Empirical outcome: F_a holds for the SERIAL-CORRELATION component (z vs B2 collapses to within Gaussian noise); F_a fails on the MARGINAL component (z vs B1 amplified or sign-flipped).** At M=1000 d=3 x=10⁶ pooled: T0 z(B2) = -1.99 (S96: -7.45 → 3.7× reduction), T1 z(B2) = -0.67 (S96: -4.05 → 6.0× reduction); but T0 z(B1) = -9.07 (worse than S96 -5.96), T1 z(B1) = +5.56 (sign-flipped from -2.58). At M=1000 d=3 x=5·10⁶ even cleaner: T0 z(B2) = -0.78, T1 z(B2) = +0.47 — both within 1σ of zero. Across d ∈ {2, 3, 4} at M=1000 x=10⁶: \|z(B2)\| ≤ 2.5 on every cell. At the original S96 anchor (M=2000 d=3 x=10⁶, single residue b=1): T0 z(B2) = -2.87 (3.0× reduction), T1 z(B2) = -2.08 (5.8× reduction), \|z(B2)\| ≤ 3 on both (vs ≥ 8.7 in S96). **Mechanism for closure mode E**: the W-trick on prime-gap sequence has TWO structural effects — (1) eliminates HL k-tuple admissibility constraints at small primes p ≤ 7, killing the serial-correlation component of E2.17; (2) forces gap distribution onto multiples of W=210, giving discrete Cramér-normalised spectrum with quasi-grid spacing ≈ 0.318 in delay embedding — this MARGINAL effect is preserved in the gap-permuted control B2 by construction, so cancels in z(B2) but contributes to z(B1). The B1 sign-flip on T1 reflects discrete-grid Vietoris-Rips creating MORE H_1 loops at the grid scale than continuous Exp(1). **Refines E2.17 inline (not a new edge)** with the serial-vs-marginal decomposition: PH deficit on bare prime gaps = (HL serial-correlation, killed by W=210 W-trick) + (gap-distribution non-Exp(1), preserved/amplified). Places E2.17 as the SIXTH leg of the W-trick-erases-everything HL-fingerprint family alongside E2.13 (Gowers U^k), E2.14 (Anderson Lyapunov), E2.15 (algebraic immunity), E2.16 (DPP failure), E2.20 (subword complexity). **Successors proposed (S117)**: D2.a.1 PH on a continuous-marginal-matched baseline B3 (KDE-fit) to fully isolate the marginal effect; D2.a.2 W-scan W ∈ {2, 6, 30, 210, 2310} to trace a "PH analogue of S^(W)_k" HL singular-series convergence parallel to E2.13's Gowers W-cascade. **Cross-domain ingredient**: persistent homology + Green-Tao W-trick — both already USED in `CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md`; no new technique. Cites E2.13, E2.14, E2.15, E2.16, E2.17, E2.19, E2.20. Cross-domain refs: Carlsson 2009 BAMS 46; Bauer 2021 arXiv:1908.02518; Green-Tao 2008 *Annals* 167 (arXiv:math/0404188). See experiments/topological/persistent_homology_w_trick/persistent_homology_w_trick_results.md. | 117 |
| §D2.a.1 (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — PH on a continuous-marginal-matched baseline B3: does the W-tricked PH deficit vs B1 absorb on a continuous quantile-sampler that matches the empirical W-tricked CDF? | REFINEMENT | E | Successor to §D2.a (S117). Adds B3 = IID samples from a *continuous* distribution matched to the W-tricked empirical marginal via inverse-transform sampling on the linearly-interpolated empirical CDF (Devroye 1986 §II.2.1). Same Takens (τ=1) + Vietoris-Rips (ripser) pipeline at M=1000, x=10⁶, three residues b ∈ {1, 11, 13} pooled, K=20 baselines, d ∈ {2, 3, 4}. **Pre-registered F_a** (continuous envelope absorbs S117 residual): \|z(P_W; B3)\| ≤ 3 on T0 AND T1, AND \|z(B3) − z(B2)\| ≤ 1. **Empirical outcome: F_a HOLDS robustly on absolute thresholds; partial violation on relative threshold IS the new content.** At M=1000, d=3, x=10⁶, three residues pooled: z(P_W; B3)_T0 = −0.05, z(P_W; B3)_T1 = +0.46 (vs S117 z(B1) = −9.07 / +5.56; z(B2) = −1.99 / −0.67). Cross-validation: re-run B1/B2 with new seed reproduces S117 z-scores to within 0.6σ. Robustness: across d ∈ {2,3,4}, \|z(B3)\| ≤ 0.65 on every cell. The continuous-envelope baseline absorbs the S117 marginal residual entirely. **Three-component decomposition (refines E2.17)**: PH deficit = (i) Δ_envelope ~7-9σ on T0 (W-tricked marginal variance ≈ 0.55, support shifted off zero by gap-quantum 0.318 — differs from Exp(1) variance 1) absorbed by B3; (ii) Δ_discreteness ~1-3σ on T0 (B2 discrete-grid permutation has higher T0 than B3 continuous-envelope IID; per-residue mean differences 1.0–14.1 across (d, b) at K=20 baseline replicates, joint significance ~1.7-4.8σ on T0); (iii) Δ_serial_residual ~1-2σ on T0 (S117's W-tricked residual gap correlation). Components (ii) and (iii) partially CANCEL on the (PRIMES_W vs B3) comparison (B2 mean above B3 mean by ~5; PRIMES_W below B2 by ~5; null on B3). Refines E2.17 inline. **Mechanism for closure mode E**: PH detects HL singular series PRIMARILY through the marginal CDF shape (not the discrete-grid quantisation or residual gap correlation). The Cramér-normalisation scales gaps by φ(W) log q so the W-tricked marginal has variance dictated by Erdős-Kac-like theorem on residue-class gaps; this variance ≠ Exp(1) is what PH measures. **Refines E2.17** from S117's two-component decomposition (serial + marginal) to a three-component decomposition (envelope + discreteness + serial-residual), with envelope dominant. **Cross-domain ingredient (Devroye 1986 inverse-transform sampling)**: standard probability text, useful diagnostic primitive; CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES not augmented. **Successors proposed (S124)**: D2.a.1.i pure-discrete IID baseline B4 (sample from empirical PMF, no interpolation) — isolates discreteness sub-component directly; D2.a.1.ii sliding-bandwidth KDE B5(σ) — predicts sigmoidal crossover at σ ≈ 0.16 (= grid-spacing/2). Cites E2.13, E2.14, E2.15, E2.16, E2.17, E2.20. Cross-domain refs: Carlsson 2009 BAMS 46; Bauer 2021 arXiv:1908.02518; Green-Tao 2008 *Annals* 167 (arXiv:math/0404188); Devroye 1986 *Non-Uniform Random Variate Generation* Springer §II.2.1. See experiments/topological/persistent_homology_w_trick_marginal_b3/persistent_homology_w_trick_marginal_b3_results.md. | 124 |
| §D2.a.1.i (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES) — PH on a pure-discrete IID baseline B4: is the S124 "discreteness" sub-component permutation-specific, or does it hold for IID with replacement on the same support? | REFINEMENT | E | Successor to §D2.a.1 (S124). Adds B4 = IID with replacement from the empirical PMF of the W=210 W-tricked Cramér-normalised gap sequence (each draw uniform over the M empirical values). Run alongside B1 (IID Exp(1)), B2 (permutation), B3 (continuous inverse-CDF) at M=1000, x≈10⁶, three residues b ∈ {1, 11, 13} pooled, K=20 baselines, d ∈ {2, 3, 4}. **Pre-stated falsifiers (4):** F_i.1 |z(P_W; B4)_T0 − z(P_W; B2)_T0| ≤ 1.0 PASS / ≥ 2.0 FAIL; F_i.2 sign((B2−B3)_T0) = sign((B4−B3)_T0); F_i.3 |z(P_W; B4)_T1 − z(P_W; B2)_T1| ≤ 1.5 at d=3; F_i.4 B4 dup count ∈ [0.30M, 0.40M]. **Outcome:** F_i.2 PASS at all d ((B2−B3)_T0 = +3.61/+5.42/+6.86; (B4−B3)_T0 = +1.74/+2.87/+3.95 — same sign at all three d). F_i.3 PASS at all d (|Δz|_T1 = 0.81/0.08/0.44, well below 1.5). F_i.4 PASS (B4 dup count 366-371 of M=1000, theory 0.368M ✓). F_i.1 marginal FAIL at d=3 (|Δz|_T0 = 2.11 vs 2.00 threshold), AMBIGUOUS at d=2 (1.89), PASS at d=4 (0.73) — **the failure is fully explained by F_i.4**: B4's IID-with-replacement creates ≈ 0.368M duplicate values per draw, producing zero-distance pairs in the Takens delay cloud, contributing **zero-length H_0 bars** (bar (0, 0)) to T0; H_1 (loops) is unaffected because a duplicate pair cannot form a loop, hence T1(B4) ≈ T1(B2). **NEW T0 component Δ_duplicate = (B2 − B4)_T0 = +1.87/+2.56/+2.91 across d** — H_0-specific, monotone in d. **Mechanism for closure mode E**: duplicate-compression is a deterministic property of the IID-with-replacement *sampling rule* (any natural NT indicator with non-degenerate empirical PMF will exhibit it under such baselines), not a primes-structural feature; its scale is dictated by the coupon-collector bound 0.368M unique values out of M draws. **Four-way decomposition (refines E2.17 inline)**: PH deficit = Δ_envelope (~7-9σ on T0; W-tricked marginal ≠ Exp(1)) + Δ_discreteness (~3-7 mean-gap on T0; baseline-INDEPENDENT, both B2 and B4 lift T0 above B3 with same sign — strengthens S124's discreteness reading) + Δ_duplicate (~2-3 mean-gap on T0, **null on T1**; H_0-specific cloud-geometry artifact, NEW S131) + Δ_serial_residual (≤ 1σ on T0, ≤ 1.2σ on T1; **tightened from S124's 1-2σ** by using B4 as the reference). The W-tricked primes' residual gap-correlation is at PH instrument noise floor once duplicate-compression is factored out. **Cross-domain ingredient**: persistent homology + empirical-PMF IID sampling — both already USED in CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md (Bauer 2021 ripser, standard probability), no new technique imported. **Successors proposed (S131)**: D2.a.1.iii — H_0-persistence-without-zero-bars renormalisation (re-compute T0 over only finite-nonzero-death bars; B4 should match B2 within 0.5σ on the renormalised statistic). D2.a.1.iv — Stratified IID baseline B6 (sample WITHOUT replacement M' ≈ 0.632M values from x; isolates fewer-points-effect from duplicate-compression-effect). Cites E2.13, E2.17, E2.20. Cross-domain refs: Carlsson 2009 BAMS 46; Bauer 2021 arXiv:1908.02518; Edelsbrunner-Harer 2010; Devroye 1986 §II.2.1; coupon-collector / birthday-problem theory for the 0.368M formula. See experiments/topological/persistent_homology_w_trick_discrete_b4/persistent_homology_w_trick_discrete_b4_results.md. | 131 |
| §B2 ATTACK_VECTORS — Automorphic L-function basis: do Hecke partial sums Σ_{n≤x} τ(n) g(n) of level-1 weight-12 cusp form Δ admit polylog approximation to π(x)? (cross-domain: Hecke eigenvalue / Selberg trace formula / Sato-Tate equidistribution) | FAIL | E | Frontier wild_swing S118 on the only major function-field/spectral candidate untouched (S116/S117 critique recommendation). Sato-Tate sanity: a(p) = τ(p)/p^{11/2} for 1229 primes p ≤ 10⁴ has max\|a(p)\| = 1.961 (Deligne ≤ 2 ✓), std 0.992 (semicircle predicts 1 ✓). Tested 5-fold cross-validated OLS regression of y(x) = π(x) - Li(x) at M = 200 log-uniform anchors against three feature ensembles at K = K_τ twist frequencies log-uniform t_k ∈ [1, 50]: F_τ = Σ_{n≤x} a(n) cos/sin(t_k log n) (Hecke partial sums); F_iid = matched K with a(n) drawn i.i.d. from Sato-Tate density (2/π)sin²θ; F_mult = matched-multiplicative random (a(p) Sato-Tate at primes, Hecke recursion at prime powers, multiplicativity at composites — same structural form as a(n)). **Pre-registered targets**: A-grade requires F_τ test rms < F_random rms by ≥ 3σ; B-grade case (1) F_τ test rms matches F_random within sample noise; B-grade case (2) explicit numerical bound F_τ / F_random ratio at finite N. **Outcome: B-grade case (2)** — F_τ test rms is ~3.0× LARGER than F_random across all (N, K) cells with Z = 17–58σ. Headline at N = 10⁴ K = 8: F_τ rms_oos = 4.239, F_iid = 1.502 ± 0.058, F_mult = 1.566 ± 0.090; Z(F_τ vs F_iid) = +47.13, Z(F_τ vs F_mult) = +29.75. K-scan at N = 10⁴ across K ∈ {4, 6, 8, 10, 12}: F_τ rms_oos ∈ [3.88, 4.31], F_random rms_oos ∈ [1.48, 1.69], Z_iid ∈ [17.5, 47.1], Z_mult ∈ [8.7, 57.7]. N-scan at K = 8 across N ∈ {5k, 10k, 20k}: F_τ rms_oos = 2.94, 4.24, 5.75 (clean √N scaling at constant 0.041 √N); F_mult rms_oos = 1.29, 1.57, 2.06 (constant 0.014–0.018 √N); ratio F_τ / F_mult = 2.85, 2.82, 2.83 — flat in N to within 1%, robust finite-N obstruction. β_τ ≈ 0.05 (Hecke fit ABSORBS √x scaling component of y) but constant residual is ~3× larger than F_random's growing residual β_random ≈ 0.30. Effective rank of F_τ at K=8 is 15/16, matching F_iid (14.9) and F_mult (15.5) — NOT a rank-deficiency artifact; F_τ spans a comparable-dimensional subspace systematically MISALIGNED with y. **Mechanism for closure mode E**: by Mellin-Perron, F_τ_k(x) is dominated by L(s, Δ) zero contributions at heights γ_l^Δ; by the Riemann explicit formula y oscillates at heights γ_l^ζ; Sato-Tate equidistribution / Katz-Sarnak GUE statistics imply {γ_l^Δ} and {γ_l^ζ} are GUE-distributed independently. F_τ is a NARROW-band basis at the wrong heights; F_random_mult is a BROADBAND basis covering all heights — same K, similar effective rank, but F_τ in "wrong subspace". Empirical 3× obstruction ratio is consistent with (effective spectral coverage)^{-1/2} for K ≈ 8 narrow- vs broad-band bases. **Refines E7.1 / E1.10 / E3.13** with first quantitative L(s, Δ)-vs-ζ INDEPENDENCE measurement at finite N. **Adds EDGE E7.15**: F_τ vs F_random_mult ratio = 2.83 ± 0.02 across 9 (N, K) cells, Z ≥ 17σ per cell, primarily-explained by Sato-Tate-driven GUE-independence of L(Δ)-zero and ζ-zero spectra. CLOSED_PATHS line 641 ("f(x) vs Ramanujan tau function | Pearson r=+0.010") was a SINGLE-feature correlation test; this is a multi-feature regression measurement of subspace alignment, structurally distinct. **Cross-domain ingredient performed real work**: Hecke eigenvalue partial sums (Selberg trace formula intuition) — first project quantitative use; CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES §1 row "Selberg trace formula" promoted UNUSED → USED E with edge E7.15. **Successors proposed (S118)**: B2.a higher-weight cusp forms (16, 18, 20, 22, 26 — does ρ vary by form?); B2.b multi-form combination (Δ ⊕ E_4 derivatives ⊕ ...) — does spectral averaging close the gap? | §D22 ATTACK_VECTORS — Higher-order Hodge Laplacian L_1 spectrum of the coprimality flag complex K_N := \{σ ⊆ [2..N] : σ pairwise coprime\} (cross-domain: combinatorial Hodge Laplacian on simplicial complexes; Lim 2020 SIAM Review 62 = arXiv:1507.05379) | FAIL | E | Frontier wild_swing S126 on whether the higher-order (k≥1) Hodge Laplacian spectrum of an arithmetic flag complex carries circuit-complexity content invisible to L_0 (CLOSED 356/387/E7.12/E7.16). Build K_N from the coprimality graph G_N = ([2..N], gcd(i,j)=1); compute B_1 (edges→vertices), B_2 (triangles→edges), B_3 (tets→triangles); diagonalise L_1 = B_1^T B_1 + B_2 B_2^T (and L_2 where feasible) at N ∈ {8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96, 128} with 5–30 ER-flag-complex matched-density controls per N. **Pre-stated targets**: A-grade = uniform `λ_2^{(1)} > c > 0` spectral gap distinct from ER + TC⁰-computable boundary. B-grade case (E) = spectra match within KS / β_k within 2σ. B-grade case (I) = β_1 deviates ≥ 3σ — Möbius singular-series fingerprint. **Outcome: B-grade structural negative-shape closure plus a sharp new empirical identity.** **F1 — Hodge KERNEL is deterministically trivial**: `β_0(K_N) = 1`, `β_k(K_N) = 0` for k ≥ 1 ∀ N ≥ 3. Mechanism: Bertrand's postulate guarantees a prime `p ∈ (N/2, N]`; every such prime is **universal** in G_N (gcd(p, v) = 1 for all v ≠ p ∈ [2, N]) ⇒ K_N is a cone, hence contractible. **F2 — `λ_max(L_1) = |V| = N − 1` exactly** to 1e-13 absolute residual at all 9 tested N; ER controls saturate at `p\|V| + O(\sqrt{|V|}) < |V|` (max ER λ_max(L_1) at N=64 = 51.7 < 63). **F3 — Multiplicity identity (main empirical theorem)**: `mult(λ_max(L_1, K_N) = |V|) = C(k+1, 2) = k(k+1)/2` where `k = π(N) - π(N/2)` is the Bertrand-prime count. Verified perfectly at all 9 tested N: (N, k, mult) = (8,2,3), (12,2,3), (16,2,3), (24,4,10), (32,5,15), (48,6,21), (64,7,28), (96,9,45), (128,13,91); empirical = predicted in every cell. Mechanism (sketch): K_N = Δ^{k-1} \ast F(H) where Δ^{k-1} is the (k-1)-simplex on Bertrand primes U and H = G_N − U; the Horak-Jost 2013 join-spectrum formula gives multiplicity = #(0-faces ∪ 1-faces of Δ^{k-1}) = k + C(k, 2) = C(k+1, 2). Full proof is a single-page exercise in Friedman / Horak-Jost machinery; we report empirically and conjecture the closed-form theorem. **F4 — L_1 mean shift = triple-coprime singular series**: trace identity gives mean(L_1, K_N) − mean(L_1, ER_p) = 3(T_cp − T_ER)/|E| with `T_cp / T_ER → ∏_p (1 − 3/p² + 2/p³) / (6/π²)³ ≈ 1.27628` as N→∞ (asymptotic = 1.27628; empirical ratios 1.241–1.273 across N = 32..128). Z[mean(L_1)] grows: 3.04σ → 4.40σ → 5.82σ → 9.48σ → **18.33σ at N = 128** (≈ √N scaling). KS distance grows 0.21 → 0.33; KS p-values from 1.9e-11 to < 1e-300. **Mechanism for closure mode E**: F1, F2, F3 all reduce to the Bertrand-prime universal-vertex join decomposition (classical); F4 reduces to the well-known triple-pairwise-coprime singular-series identity (E2.13-adjacent). The Hodge cross-domain import IS quantitatively new (first higher-order arithmetic-flag-complex spectrum), but the arithmetic content collapses to known number-theoretic identities. **Pre-stated A-target NOT met**: `λ_min(L_1)` Z-score is +5.5σ at N=48 but only +2.5σ at N=64 — non-monotone, not a uniform-in-N spectral gap. **Refines / cites**: distinct from CLOSED 356/387 (L_0 only — Ramanujan sums); distinct from E7.12/E7.16 (L_0 Cayley spectra); first L_k for k ≥ 1 quantitatively computed for an arithmetic flag complex. **Adds EDGE E7.17**: mult(λ_max(L_1, K_N) = N − 1) = (π(N) − π(N/2))(π(N) − π(N/2) + 1) / 2 (empirical 9-cell, conjecture sketch via Horak-Jost join-spectrum). Joins HL-detection family with a NEW saturation category: Hodge L_1 mean reduces to triple-coprime singular series; first higher-order arithmetic Hodge fingerprint. **Cross-domain ingredient (Lim 2020 / Friedman 1996 / Horak-Jost 2013 combinatorial Hodge Laplacian) performed real work**: first project quantitative use; CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES §1 row "Hodge / Laplacian on simplicial complexes (higher-order L_k, k ≥ 1)" promoted PROPOSED (D22) → USED E with edge E7.17. **Channelled mathematician**: Friedman (combinatorial Laplacians, simplicial-complex Betti via spectral methods). **Successors proposed (S126)**: D22.a higher Hodge Laplacian on the 2-skeleton at N^{1/2}-sieved coprimality flag complex (test if F3 multiplicity identity persists when small-prime hubs are removed); D22.b non-trivial β_2 / β_3 search by removing universal-Bertrand-prime vertices by hand to break the cone collapse; D22.c Hodge Laplacian of the chi_P-induced sub-flag-complex (only prime vertices) — orthogonal to coprimality angle. Cites E2.13, E2.14, E2.16, E2.17, E2.19, E7.12, E7.16. Cross-domain refs: Eckmann 1944 *Comment. Math. Helv.* 17, 240; Friedman 1996 *Algorithmica* 21, 331; Horak-Jost 2013 *Adv. Math.* 244, 303; Lim 2020 *SIAM Review* 62(3), 685 = arXiv:1507.05379; Goff 2009 *J. Algebraic Combin.* 30, 215 (join spectrum); Kahle 2009 *Discrete Math* 309, 1658; Kahle 2014 *Annals* 179, 1085. See experiments/topological/hodge_coprimality_flag/hodge_coprimality_flag_results.md. | 126 |

F0' holds (PRIMES W=2 M=3 UNSAT 157 s, M=4 SAT 181 s, gates=4 verified=64/64 — matches S127's joint-ILP M*=4). F1 REJECTED on every seed: random N=6 W=2 has M=2 UNSAT (130–196 s) and M=3 UNSAT (147–230 s) at all three seeds {1, 5, 42}; thus `M*(rand_s; W=2) ≥ 4 = M*(PRIMES; W=2)` for s ∈ {1, 5, 42}. The technique change eliminates alpha-bilinear constraints `v[k] = sel[k] AND beta[k]` that dominate joint-ILP search. (ii) **F4 direction-confirmed at W=2 across 3 seeds with Δ ≥ 0**, refining S127's "unresolved" verdict; the magnitude (Δ=0 vs Δ ≥ 1) remains open. (iii) **Methodological side-finding for S84 framework**: column-enum proves W=2 M=3 UNSAT 1.8× faster than joint-ILP on PRIMES (157 s vs 277 s) and dominates joint-ILP entirely for the random-target M=3 case. (iv) Catalog-size empirical fact: `#Θ(6, W)` = (1458, 30,898, 218,066) at W=(1, 2, 3). **Refines E5.3** with: PRIMES `M*(W=2; N=6) = 4` is *not* breakable by replacing PRIMES with density-matched random — the random class is at least as hard at W=2. **Refines C7-S89/E1.6** (PRIMES ≈ oddness) by showing the W=2 PRIMES-vs-random gap holds in direction even *outside* the calibrated-oddness regime where bit_0 mass is matched. Time-asymmetry observation parallel to S127: PRIMES W=2 M=4 SAT in 181 s (column-enum) / 17 s (S127 joint), random W=2 M=4 UNKNOWN at 618 s — PRIMES carries low-rank exploitable structure (most plausibly the strong bit-0 predictor) that random of identical density lacks. **Cross-domain ingredient**: NONE (internal cross-encoding, not cross-domain). **Successors proposed (S135)**: C8.b.i greedy/LNS SAT search on random M=4 W=2 (1 session); C8.b.ii W=3 column-enum on random K=218K (1-2 sessions); C8.b.iii seed-distribution histogram at N=6 W=2 M=3 across 100+ seeds in 32-core parallel ≈ 10 min wall-clock (1 session). | §L1 NOVELTY_CHALLENGES — Leading-row + dead-col upper-triangulation for E2.1 orthogonal corner `(W, d=j+1)`: which W ≤ 72 admit a Lean-tractable triangulation with rows ⊂ [0, W) and R = φ(W)+1 ≤ 22? | EXHAUSTED | C | Comprehensive enumeration via DP-based bitmask reachability on column subsets (script: `experiments/formalisations/E2_1_mps_bond_dim/leading_row_search.py`). For each W, build row signatures `S(r) ⊆ {chosen R cols}` as bitmasks; DP over T ⊆ R-cols to decide if some col ordering admits row assignment with first-σ-position ∈ A_i nonempty for every i. **Outcome**: leading-row family closes **EXACTLY** `W ∈ {2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 18, 20}` and is structurally obstructed for every other W in `[2, 72]` with `R ≤ 22`. Obstructed set: `{7, 9, 11, 13, 14, 15, 16, 17, 19, 21, 22, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 30, 32, 33, 34, 36, 38, 40, 42, 44, 48, 50, 54, 60, 66}`. **W=10 correction**: previously listed as obstructed in S128/S129 RESEARCH_AGENDA notes ("multiplicity-2 residue pattern"), the S144 DP found the missing triangulation `ρ ↦ (1, 0, 4, 3, 9)`, `σ ↦ (8, 1, 2, 0, 6)` (diagonal primes `{19, 2, 43, 31, 97}`), giving an eleventh unconditional `mps_bond_dim` instance. **Mechanism for exhaustion**: a leading-row triangulation of an `R × R` 0/1 sub-matrix with `R = φ(W) + 1` requires that for some col ordering `σ`, the row `bottom-row` (= row at position `R`) has exactly one prime-position in the chosen R cols. For W with `R ≥ 7` and composite structure {prime power × small prime}, no such "isolated diagonal hit" exists in rows `[0, W)` because chiP windows of length W cluster primes by residue class. The DP is sufficient (true negation): `reach[2^R - 1] = false` ⇔ no triangulation exists. **Closes**: routes A^{(N)} for N ≥ 12 are ruled out at this parameter range; further single-session corner closures must use either (a) `Matrix.det_of_blockTriangular` for non-triangulizable matrices (multi-session), or (b) cofactor-expansion-based determinant proofs (e.g., the W=9 7×7 sub-matrix has det = 1 by row cofactor expansion but no row+col upper-triangulation; ~250 Lean lines per such corner). **Adds EDGE (negative shape, in EDGES.md as a refinement of E2.1)**: leading-row + dead-col triangulation closes E2.1 orthogonal corner exactly for `W ∈ {2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 10, 12, 18, 20}` at `R ≤ 22`. **Cross-domain ingredient**: bitmask DP over reachability (algorithmic, not number-theoretic). **Channelled mathematician**: none — purely combinatorial enumeration. Cites E2.1, references S98/S99/S106/S107/S117/S122/S128/S129/S137/S143/S144 (the closure chain). See `experiments/formalisations/E2_1_mps_bond_dim/leading_row_search.py` and `experiments/formalisations/E2_1_mps_bond_dim/leading_row_search_results.txt`, `archive/sessions/session144_l1_lean_w10_corner_and_search.md`. | 144 |

OLS fit `log(||λ||_{U^2}^4) = -0.990·log(N) + 0.564` matches Rademacher prediction `-1.000·log(N) + log(2) = -1.000·log(N) + 0.693` within slope error 1%; intercept gap consistent with finite-N centring noise. **μ U^2 (N up to 2^20)**: matched-variance IID prediction `2(6/π²)²/N = 0.7392/N` matched to 0.998 ratio at N=2^20 (1.293 → 0.998 across N range). The huge nominal Z = -433σ vs ±1 Rademacher reflects only the σ² = 6/π² < 1 variance gap; against matched-variance IID the convergence to 1 is exact. **λ U^3 (full-h, N ∈ {1024, 2048, 4096, 8192})**: ratio to Rademacher pool mean = 1.00, 1.05, 0.75, 0.61 — Z-scores +0.87, +1.44, +0.66, +1.88 σ, all within 2σ noise. **Inverse-theorem stress (1-step nilsequence detector)**: max single Fourier coefficient `max_k |λhat(k)|²` matches Rademacher max-coefficient distribution (CLT prediction `√(N log N)`) within \|Z\| ≤ 3.01σ at every tested N up to 2^20. The single +3.01σ at N=2^18 returns to noise (-0.01σ) at N=2^20 — no sustained 1-step nilsequence correlation. μ small-denominator clustering at N ≤ 2^14 is FFT-bin alignment artefact (bins better resolve rationals 1/30, 1/6 at small N; disperses at large N where FFT-bin spacing 1/N misaligns with rationals of denominator 30). **Mechanism for closure mode E**: GT's Möbius/nilsequence orthogonality theorem `Σ_n μ(n) F(g(n)Γ) = O_F(N log^{-A} N)` for any nilsequence `F(g(n)Γ)` predicts (via GT-Ziegler U^{s+1}[N] inverse theorem) `||μ||_{U^k} = o(1)` for every k. **Empirically the bound is much sharper at finite N**: not just `o(1)` but matching IID matched-variance prediction within 1% at N = 2^20. The GT log-power refinement `log^{-A} N` is below the empirical noise floor `1/√N · 1/√(n_seeds)` at every tested N. **Pairs with E2.18 (S100, λ Anderson Lyapunov) to give two orthogonal multiplicative-regime confirmations**: spectral (E2.18) and additive-combinatorics (E2.25) categories both confirm λ ≈ Rademacher without W-tricking. **Stark contrast with E2.13 (chi_P Gowers, S85)**: chi_P's U^k norms detected the Hardy-Littlewood singular series mod q (88σ at parity resonance, removable by W=2310 sieve to ~4σ); λ and μ have NO mod-q signal, no W-trick needed. **Adds EDGE E2.25**: `||λ||_{U^k[Z/NZ]}^{2^k} = ||IID(σ_λ=1)||_{U^k}^{2^k} · (1 ± O(1/√N))` for k ∈ {2, 3} at N ≤ 2^20; same for μ with σ_μ² = 6/π². EVS shape (forbids 1-step or 2-step nilsequence correlation visible at finite N). **Adds 3 new pseudorandomness measures** (λ U^2, μ U^2, λ U^3) to the project's existing 38-measure battery — first measurements on multiplicative functions in the Gowers-norm family. **Cross-domain ingredient (Green-Tao Möbius/nilsequence orthogonality theorem) performed real work**: first project use; CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md §3 row "Gowers norms / Green-Tao Möbius orthogonality" promoted PROPOSED → USED-E with edges E2.13 (S85, χ_P) and E2.25 (this session, λ + μ). **Channelled mathematician**: Tao (additive combinatorics; Möbius/nilsequence orthogonality). **Successors proposed (S153)**: G2.a von Mangoldt `Λ(n)` Gowers norms — bridges additive (χ_P) and multiplicative (λ, μ) regimes; log-weighted variance σ_Λ² ≈ N/log N varies with N (1 session); G2.b higher-order Gowers `U^4`, `U^5` of λ — tests whether λ has 3-step or 4-step nilsequence correlations at finite N (subsample h_1, ..., h_{k-1} for tractability; 1-2 sessions); G2.c rescheduled §G3 (Möbius Voronin) — inverse-theorem absence raises priority of effective universality of `1/ζ` (2 sessions). Cites E2.13, E2.14, E2.18, E1.10, E3.13. Cross-domain refs: Green-Tao 2012 arXiv:0807.1736; Green-Tao-Ziegler 2012 arXiv:1009.3998; Green-Tao 2010 arXiv:math/0606088; Sarnak 2010 IAS lectures; Tao 2016 arXiv:1509.05422 (logarithmic-Chowla). See `experiments/information_theory/liouville_gowers_uk/`, `archive/sessions/session153_g2_liouville_mobius_gowers_uk.md`. | 153 |
| C3.a (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES §1, S105 successor) — Arithmetic-primitive bounded-Kt VM × per-bit difficulty: does adding LOG2 / LI_APPROX / DIV_LOG / GEO_SUM to the bounded-Kt simulator shift the cut on `s_J^(N) := (bit_J(π(0)),...,bit_J(π(2^N-1)))` from S105's `J*(N) ≈ N − log₂ N` toward E1.3's `⌈N/2⌉`? | REFINEMENT | E | Composition of E5.8 (Brandt-class barrier — structural argument inherited unchanged) + E1.3 (per-bit difficulty profile — refined inline). Built 4-bit-per-op extended VM (16 ops: 8 base {PUSH0/1/N, INC, ADD, SUB, MUL, SHR1, DUP, EMIT_S, EMIT0, HALT} + 4 R^{-1}-kernel arithmetic primitives {LOG2, LI_APPROX, DIV_LOG, GEO_SUM}) with integer stack alongside bit-emit stream; T_MAX = 4096, INT_CAP = 10⁹. C inner loop (`sim.c → sim.so`) batch-evaluates one program against all targets simultaneously; pre-skip of programs with no EMIT in pc cycle saves ~50% of enumeration. **Pre-stated falsifiers**: F1 (full shift to ⌈N/2⌉ — every easy-J compresses, every hard-J saturates); F2 (no shift — easy zone all saturated as in original VM, DUPLICATE-PLUS); F3 (intermediate hierarchy — some easy J compress, others saturate). **Result: F3 holds globally (intermediate hierarchy)**, with structure: per-N at L_max=24 (matched to target_lens 16/32/64), the cut moves fully to ⌈N/2⌉ for N ∈ {4, 5} and reverts to J*(N) for N=6; at L_max=28, N=6 partially shifts (J=4 compresses, J=3 still saturates). **Within-easy-zone J-monotone hierarchy identified**: bits closer to J*(N) (the trivial-zero boundary) compress at smaller L_max; bits closer to ⌈N/2⌉ (the E1.3 hard-easy boundary) require larger L_max — the bounded-Kt cost of bit-J of π is empirically monotone in N − J. **Headline compressing programs (L_max=24)**: (N=4, J=2) Kt_b'=33 prog `ADD, LI, LI, EMIT_S, PUSH_N, PUSH_N` — emits `bit_0(LI(LI(2x)))` which EXACTLY matches `bit_2(π(x))` on [0, 16); (N=5, J=3) Kt_b'=33 prog `EMIT_S, LI, DUP, INC, PUSH_N, PUSH0`. Triple-LI program `EMIT_S, PUSH_N, LI, LI, LI, DUP, EMIT_S` (L=28) compresses (N=6, J=4). **Falsified**: original C3 spec's S105 prediction "if YES, R^{-1}-kernel-equipped VM resolves the smooth/oscillatory transition" was correct AT SMALL N (4, 5) but breaks at N=6; the predicted full shift is L_max-budget limited. **Negative content**: hard-zone (J < ⌈N/2⌉) cells remain saturated at L_max ≤ target_len for every tested N ≥ 4; no compressing programs found within budget for hard bits. The structural orthogonality of Brandt's TRAVERSE to the per-bit family (all four obstructions O1-O4 of E5.8) is unaffected by VM choice — empirical compressibility of some `s_J^(N)` does not enable a Brandt diagonalisation. **Quantitative regime caveat**: at L_max > target_len the hard-easy distinction begins to fray (e.g. L_max=28 spuriously "compresses" N=4 hard zone J=0,1 because 28-bit programs can express any 16-bit pattern combinatorially); the meaningful regime is L_max ≤ target_len. **Refines E1.3 inline** with VM-richness hierarchy: 3-bit stack VM cuts at J*(N), 4-bit arith VM cuts at ⌈N/2⌉ for N ≤ 5 with within-easy-zone monotone J-ranking. Cross-domain ingredient: arithmetic-augmented bounded-Kt simulator (analogous to Levin Kt with non-trivial primitive set) — first project use of Li-kernel primitives inside Kt simulator. **Adds new finite-N empirical fact** (not previously measured): bounded-Kt cut location is VM-richness-AND-N-dependent with explicit hierarchy. Cites E5.8 (unchanged), E1.3 (refined inline), E1.5, E1.9 (LSB still saturates), E5.3. Successors proposed (S150): C3.a.i L_max=32 sweep (resolves N=6, J=3); C3.a.ii larger N at random-program sampling; C3.a.iii VM-budget vs RH-scale linkage to S146 F1 results; C3.a.iv ablation drop/swap of arithmetic primitives.
