| C3.a.iv (NOVELTY_CHALLENGES §1, S150 successor) — Arithmetic-primitive ABLATION of the C3.a bounded-Kt VM: are any of {LOG2, LI, DIV_LOG, GEO_SUM} strictly necessary for the easy-zone cut shift documented in S150? | REFINEMENT | E | Composition of E5.8 (unchanged) + E1.3 (refined inline) + C3.a/S150 (refined). Re-ran S150's per-bit Kt_b'(s_J^(N)) scan over N ∈ {3,4,5,6}, J = 0..N−1 at L_max=28 under SIX ablation conditions: baseline, drop_LOG2, drop_LI, drop_DIV_LOG, drop_GEO_SUM, only_LI (drop other three). C inner loop (`sim_ablation.so`) extends `batch_enumerate_at_length_filtered` with a 16-bit `forbidden_ops_mask`; programs containing any forbidden op are skipped pre-simulation (exact ablation, no noise). Total 6 × 268M = 1.6B programs in 463s wall-time. **Pre-stated falsifiers (5 outcomes)**: F1 (LI solely necessary AND sufficient for easy zone); F2 (multiple primitives jointly necessary); F3 (LI alone insufficient); F4 (no single primitive strictly necessary); F5 mixed/incoherent. **Result: F4 holds for the easy zone** {(3,2), (4,2), (5,3), (6,4)} — every cell that compresses in baseline also compresses under every single-drop AND under only_LI. drop_LI matches baseline at L_max≤24 cells with alternative compressing programs: (N=3,J=2) `EMIT_S, PUSH_N, GEO_SUM, EMIT_S`; (N=4,J=2) `GEO_SUM, DIV_LOG, EMIT_S, PUSH_N, INC, PUSH_N`; (N=5,J=3) `EMIT_S, INC, LOG2, DUP, PUSH_N, PUSH0` (LOG2 alone); and is +1 bit at (N=6,J=4) (`EMIT_S, GEO_SUM, PUSH_N, ADD, DUP, LOG2, PUSH0` Kt 37 vs baseline LI-triple Kt 36). **Refutes S150's narrowest reading** that "iterated LI is the dominant compression mechanism" — LI is the cleanest realization but is one of four substitutable mechanisms. only_LI matches baseline on every easy-zone cell, confirming LI is sufficient; drop_LI also preserves compression, confirming LI is not necessary. The arithmetic-primitive class {LOG2, LI, DIV_LOG, GEO_SUM} forms an OVER-COMPLETE basis for E1.3-easy-zone bounded-Kt compression at the tested (N, L_max). **Hard-zone primitive sensitivity differs**: meaningful (L<target_len) cell (N=5, J=2) requires LI ∧ DIV_LOG (drop_LI saturates, drop_DIV_LOG saturates, drop_LOG2 and drop_GEO_SUM preserve) — the compressing program `DIV_LOG, LI, SHR1, SHR1, EMIT_S, PUSH_N, PUSH0` realizes a structurally distinct floor(out_count/log(LI(out_count)))>>2 mod 2 mechanism. The other "hard-zone" cells with apparent ablation-sensitivity (N=4, J=0,1) live in the L_max>target_len combinatorial regime (E1.3 caveat (a)) and are not load-bearing. **Closure mode E** (refinement of E1.3, no asymptotic improvement). **Refines E1.3 inline** with primitive-class-robustness statement: the S150 cut shift is driven by the FAMILY of slow-growing-integer-function primitives, not by LI specifically. **Refines C3.a/S150** by demonstrating that the optimum-program disassembly does not faithfully indicate which primitives are causally required. Cites E5.8 (unchanged), E1.3 (refined), E1.5, E5.3. Successors proposed (S158): C3.a.iv.α LI-vs-non-LI gap scaling at large N (test whether LI gains strict advantage at N≥7); C3.a.iv.β alternative primitive set {LN, INV_LI, ZETA_K} (test class-robustness across primitive sets). See `experiments/constructions/brandt_per_bit_arith_vm_ablation/`, `archive/sessions/session158_c3aiv_arith_vm_ablation.md`. | 158 |

| §D44 ATTACK_VECTORS — Arithmetic topology / Morishita arithmetic Massey products on prime triples — empirical distribution of the Rédei symbol `[p, q, r] ∈ {±1}` on Borromean-admissible prime triples up to N=1000: does the empirical mean deviate from uniform 1/2 in a way correlated with the Hardy-Littlewood ternary singular series `S_3(N)` (A-grade), or is it unbiased within the strict 5σ envelope (B-grade case I, mode E)? (cross-domain: Mazur 1966 *Ann. Sci. ENS* 6, 521 + Morishita 2002 *J. Reine Angew. Math.* 550, 141 + Morishita 2012 *Knots and Primes* Springer + Rédei 1939 *J. Reine Angew. Math.* 180, 1 + Lemmermeyer 2000 *Reciprocity Laws* CUP Ch. 9.1) | FAIL | E | Frontier wild_swing S164 on the FIRST project measurement of arithmetic-topology / Galois-cohomological triple Massey-product invariants on the prime sequence. Implemented Lemmermeyer's maximal-order Rédei-symbol formula `[p,q,r] = ((U+Vσ)·2⁻¹/r)` with `U²−pV²=4q`, `U≡V (mod 2)`, `σ²≡p (mod r)` via `sympy.solvers.diophantine.diop_DN`. Validated on canonical Borromean triple `[13, 61, 937] = -1` (Wikipedia / Morishita 2012); six independent fundamental solutions to `U²−13V²=244` all give `-1`, confirming sigma- and (U,V)-choice invariance. **Pre-stated falsifiers**: F1 (A-grade): `|f_+ - 0.5| > 5/√K` AND HL-S_3 Pearson > 0.3; F2 (B-grade case I, UNBIASED, mode E): `|f_+ - 0.5| < 5/√K` at all three N levels; F3 (B-grade case II): `|f_+ - 0.5| > 5/√K` but bias not matched by HL S_3; INC: > 10% triples lack norm rep. **Outcome: F2 HOLDS at all three N, F1 FALSIFIED**. Three-N table (admissible, well-defined K, INC, n_+, n_−, f_+, z vs ½): N=200 (122, 122, 0, 59, 63, 0.4836, -0.36); N=500 (1419, 1361, 58, 662, 699, 0.4864, -1.00); N=1000 (9100, 8577, 523, 4174, 4403, 0.4867, -2.47); 1000 clean-subset excluding p ∈ {229, 257, 401, 577, 733, 761} (7591, 7591, 0, 3684, 3907, 0.4853, -2.56). 5σ envelope `2.5/√K` at N=1000 = 0.027, observed `|Δ|` = 0.0133 < 5σ. **Constant `Δf_+ ≈ -0.013`, `z ∼ √K` pattern** consistent with finite-r effective-Chebotarev convergence of the Frobenius density to 1/2 in the Rédei field (`O(1/√(log r)) ≈ 0.01` at r ~ 1000), NOT a true asymptotic bias. INC concentrated on six primes p ∈ {229, 257, 401, 577, 733, 761} with class group `h(p) ≥ 3`; clean-subset analysis (z = -2.56) confirms bias is NOT INC artefact. **No mod-residue substructure (mod 8, 12, 24) deviates beyond Bonferroni-corrected 3σ**: max |z| at mod-8 = 2.49 (1,1,5) below threshold 2.73; max |z| at mod-24 = 2.31 (5,1,13) below threshold 3.24. **Mechanism for closure mode E**: the Rédei symbol [p, q, r] is the Frobenius image of `r` in the Rédei field `K_pq`/Q (cyclic-of-order-4, ramified only at p, q, containing `Q(√pq)`). By Chebotarev density, asymptotic Frobenius distribution in `Gal(K_pq/Q) ≅ Z/4Z` projected to Z/2Z yields uniform ±1. Finite-N effective Chebotarev gives `|Δf_+| = O(1/√(log r))` correction, observed `|Δf_+| ≈ 0.013` at r ≲ 1000 matches predicted scale. **Adds EDGE E2.29**: cohomological-Massey orthogonality — Rédei symbol [p, q, r] empirical mean is uniform within 5σ on principal-narrow-class admissible triples up to N=1000, consistent with bilinear HL singular series and Chebotarev at finite r; arithmetic-topology Massey-product invariant carries no detectable triple-prime arithmetic content beyond HL bilinear at the level of empirical mean-of-symbol. EVS M shape. **13th orthogonal pseudorandomness category** for χ_P (cohomological-Massey / arithmetic-topology category) after E2.13 / E2.14 / E2.15 / E2.16 / E2.17 / E2.19 / E2.20 / E2.22 / E2.24 / E2.26 / E2.27 / E2.28. **Distinct from CLOSED line 208** (generic knot polynomial invariants Jones/HOMFLY/Khovanov on unrelated knots) — Rédei is *arithmetic by construction* via Galois cohomology of `G_{Q, {p, q, r, ∞}}`. **Distinct from CLOSED line 202** (étale cohomology of Spec(Z) recovers Euler product) — Rédei is finite triple symbol on specific prime triples. **Distinct from class-group / Cohen-Lenstra heuristics** — Rédei is the GENUS-level invariant one step below the 4-rank density that C-L predicts. **Cross-domain ingredient (arithmetic topology / Mazur primes-as-knots) performed real work**: FIRST project use; CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md §4 row "Arithmetic topology" promoted PROPOSED → USED-E with edge E2.29. **Channelled mathematician**: Morishita (arithmetic topology) / Stevenhagen (Rédei reciprocity). **Successors proposed (S164)**: D44.a quartic Massey product `⟨p₁, p₂, p₃, p₄⟩_2` (Vogel 2004 thesis) on admissible 4-tuples — first higher-rank arithmetic-topology invariant (1-2 sessions); D44.b 4-rank distribution of `Cl(Q(√(p·q)))` (Cohen-Lenstra) on admissible (p, q) pairs (1-2 sessions); D44.c quantitative effective-Chebotarev rate test — model `|Δf_+| ∼ c / √log N` and confirm sub-5σ z-growth is finite-N effective Chebotarev for the Rédei field (1 session). Cross-domain refs: Mazur 1966 *Ann. Sci. ENS* 6, 521; Morishita 2002 *J. Reine Angew. Math.* 550, 141; Morishita 2012 *Knots and Primes* Springer; Rédei 1939 *J. Reine Angew. Math.* 180, 1; Lemmermeyer 2000 *Reciprocity Laws* CUP; Stevenhagen 1996 *Acta Arith.* 76, 89; Vogel 2004 Heidelberg PhD thesis; Wikipedia: Arithmetic topology. See `experiments/algebraic/redei_symbol_prime_triples/` and `archive/sessions/session164_d44_redei_symbol.md`. | 164 |

| §D45 ATTACK_VECTORS — Baker-Norine graph Riemann-Roch / chip-firing rank `r_G(D_P^N)` of the prime divisor on the divisibility graph `Γ_N := ([1, N], divisibility edges)` and the Hasse cover graph `H_N := ([1, N], cover edges)`: does `r_G(D_P^N)` deviate by `> 5σ` from a matched-degree random divisor, or exhibit Brill-Noether specialness `r > deg − g`? (cross-domain: Baker-Norine 2007 *Adv. Math.* 215, 766 = arXiv:math/0608360 + Dhar 1990 *Phys. Rev. Lett.* 64, 1613 burning) | FAIL | I | Frontier wild_swing S161 on the only chip-firing-theoretic invariant of arithmetic graphs. Implemented Dhar burning + q-reduction + Baker-Norine Algorithm 4.1 rank computation in `experiments/algebraic/baker_norine_chi_p/`. **Pre-stated falsifiers**: F1 (rank `r_G(D_P^N) ≥ deg − g + 2` for some N, A-grade Brill-Noether specialness witness); F2 (rank deviates from matched-density random by > 5σ — A-grade if mechanism is arithmetic; B-grade if reducible to graph topology). **Outcome (F2 holds in B-grade form, A-grade falsified globally)**: rank `r(D_P^N) = max(0, deg − g + 1) = 0` for all N ∈ {32, 64, 128} on both `Γ_N` and `H_N`. By Riemann-Roch `r(K − D_P) = g − π(N) − 1`, identical for any effective degree-π(N) divisor — **specialness in the Baker-Norine sense is the same for D_P and matched-density random divisors**. The rank invariant is determined by `(deg(D), graph G)` modulo Riemann-Roch and graph gonality — blind to deeper prime arithmetic. **Closed-form q-reduced-form identities (B-grade content, new EDGE E2.28)**: (1) on `Γ_N` with sink q=1: `D'_P^N = (π(N) − π(N/2)) · δ_1 + Σ_{p prime, p ≤ N/2} δ_p` (verified N up to 512); (2) on `H_N` with sink q=1: `D'_P^N = π(N) · δ_1` (verified N up to 512). Both proved via Dhar burn analysis. **Z-score growth on Hasse**: D'(q) = π(N) for D_P vs E[π(N)²/N] for random matched divisor → z-score `~ √π(N) ~ √(N / log N)` super-linear in N. Empirical: +8.6σ at N=32, +11.1σ at N=64, +14.6σ at N=128. **Mechanism for closure mode I**: D'(q) measures (chips on N(q) of D) for any divisor — a graph-NEIGHBORHOOD count, not arithmetic. For D_P on H_N, N(q=1) = primes (by Hasse construction), so D'(q) = π(N) tautologically. The chip-firing reformulation expresses π(N) as q-component of q-reduced D_P but is NOT algorithmically useful: building D_P^N requires knowing primes. **Generalised identity**: on H_N, q=1, for any divisor D supported on `{1} ∪ {primes}`: `D'(1) = D(1) + Σ_p D(p)`. Tested on D_P, D_sqfree (holds), D_μ_pos (holds), D_λ_pos (fails — chips on composites cascade through Dhar), D_Omega2 (fails — same cascade). **Adds EDGE E2.28** (chip-firing q-reduced identity for D_P^N on Γ_N and H_N, EVS M shape) — first chip-firing-theoretic invariant computed on any arithmetic graph in the project. **Distinct from**: CLOSED tropical lines 204, 326, 431, 432, 660 (those are min-plus compression of generating functions, fundamentally different from chip-firing on graphs); D22 CLOSED Hodge L_1 spectrum (linear spectral, vs non-linear divisor rank); A3 CLOSED Cayley graph spectrum (L_0 spectrum, vs higher-order divisor rank). **Cross-domain ingredient (Baker-Norine RR + Dhar burning) performed real work**: first project use of chip-firing graph theory; CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md §2 row "Baker-Norine graph Riemann-Roch / chip-firing" promoted PROPOSED → USED-I with edge E2.28. **Channelled mathematician**: Baker (graph divisor theory). **Successors proposed (S161)**: D45.a chip-firing on the prime-gap multigraph or prime-driven Cayley graph (where graph topology ITSELF is non-trivial arithmetic, not just `[1, N]` with divisibility) — does the rank invariant carry arithmetic content there? (1-2 sessions); D45.b "tropical Brill-Noether" — explicit dimensional study of `W^r_d(Γ_N)` Brill-Noether locus on arithmetic graphs (1 session); D45.c gonality of `Γ_N` and `H_N` exact computation (test whether arithmetic graphs have lower / higher gonality than typical sparse graphs of same edge density, 1 session). Cross-domain refs: Baker-Norine 2007 arXiv:math/0608360; Dhar 1990 PRL 64, 1613; Bjorner-Lovász-Shor 1991 Eur. J. Combin. 12, 283; Backman-Baker-Yuen 2019 Forum Math. Sigma 7. See `experiments/algebraic/baker_norine_chi_p/` and `archive/sessions/session161_d45_baker_norine_chi_p.md`. | 161 |

| S166 commit-thread step 2 — analytic theorem for V_p ⊕ V_{2p}^prim energy of chi_P MPS unfolding (replaces S148's empirical "K = 3.86" with an exact Ramanujan-sum formula) [B-grade per S167 verification, demoted from A; theorem itself confirmed but proof tools are textbook NT] | THEOREM+REFINEMENT | C | Commit-thread session 2/5 on the S82 invariant-subspace theorem. **Theorem (S166).** For W=2, `N = 2^d`, the L² energy of `chi_P` in the additive-Fourier subspace `V_p^prim ⊕ V_{2p}^prim` equals `E(p, N) = (2 (pi(N) − O(1))²) / ((p−1) N) + O(p · Var(p, N) / N)` where `Var(p, N) = sum_{a coprime to p} (pi(N; p, a) − pi(N)/(p−1))²` is the Gallagher PNT-in-AP variance. Equivalently in S148's K-form: `K(p, N) = E(p, N) · p · log²(N) / N → 2p/(p−1)` as `N → ∞`, with finite-N correction `(pi(N) log N / N)²`. **Proof sketch**: For each k coprime to q, the additive-Fourier coefficient `S_q^k = sum_{p' prime ≤ N} ω_q^{k p'}` decomposes via Dirichlet character orthogonality as a *principal-character main term* (proportional to the Ramanujan sum `c_q(k)`) plus *non-principal Dirichlet sums* `Psi(N, chi)`. Ramanujan sums: `c_p(k) = mu(p) = -1` (k coprime to p prime), `c_{2p}(k) = mu(2p) = +1` (k coprime to 2p, p odd). Both give `|S_q^k|² ≈ (pi(N) − O(1))²/(p−1)²`. Sum over `phi(q) = p-1` coprime k-values per q ∈ {p, 2p}, divide by N. The non-principal sum is bounded by Plancherel: `≤ p · Var(p, N) / N`. **Empirical verification at 25 cells `(d, p) ∈ {14, 16, 18, 20, 22} × {3, 5, 7, 11, 13}`: ratio empirical/predicted ∈ [0.992, 1.000], mean 0.998, max deviation 0.8%, shrinking as N grows**. Specifically at d=22 (N = 4M, pi(N) = 295,947): pred V_p+V_{2p} = 20881.80, empirical = 20881.27 (ratio 1.0000) for p=3; ratio 1.0000 for p=5, 7; ratio 0.9999 for p=11, 13. **Net new content**: (a) **The "K = 3.86" universal constant from S148 is corrected**: K is *p-dependent*, with `K_∞(p) = 2p/(p-1)` (3.0 / 2.5 / 7/3 / 2.2 for p = 3, 5, 7, 11). The S148 average ≈ 3.86 reflected (i) p=3 weight in the data, (ii) finite-N correction `(pi(N) log N / N)² ≈ 1.18 at d=20`, and (iii) SVD spike-block leakage outside V_p + V_{2p}^prim. (b) **S148's Gallagher-variance framing is REFUTED for the main term**: the main term is a **Ramanujan-sum / principal-character-of-Dirichlet quantity**, NOT a 2nd-moment of primes. The Gallagher variance enters only in the sub-leading remainder. These are different objects governed by different machinery (Ramanujan-sum c_q(k) = mu(q) — deterministic; Gallagher variance — Bombieri-Vinogradov 2nd-moment). (c) **SVD spike-block leakage diagnosed**: empirical `sum sigma²_top-spikes` exceeds `E(p, N)` by 4-38% at d=20 (p=3 / 5 / 7), shrinking with N (e.g., p=3: 18% at d=14 → 4% at d=20); asymptotically the SVD spike block converges to the V_p ⊕ V_{2p}^prim subspace energy. (d) **Sharper structural identification**: chi_P's MPS-unfolding spike content at conductor 2p is **EXACTLY** the principal-Dirichlet-character contribution to mod-p / mod-2p prime distribution, with all character L-function content sitting in the sub-leading remainder. This is sharper than S82's "approximate eigenvector ~ |L(1, chi)|²" claim (refuted in S148) AND sharper than S148's "Gallagher variance" claim. **Closure mode C** (circular): the theorem proves what the spike-block energy IS, but computing E(p, N) requires pi(N) and the remainder requires Gallagher 2nd-moment data. The C-circular collapse stands; what's new is the *exactness* of the analytic identification of the main term. Cites E2.1, E1.5, S74, S82, S148. Cross-domain refs: Hardy-Wright (2008) *An Intro to the Theory of Numbers* Ch. 16 (Ramanujan sums); Iwaniec-Kowalski (2004) *Analytic Number Theory* Ch. 3 (Dirichlet character orthogonality, Gauss sums); Davenport (2000) *MNT* Ch. 6, 8 (PNT-in-AP); Gallagher (1970) *Mathematika* 23 (used only for remainder bound); Montgomery-Vaughan (2007) *MNT I* Ch. 9 (Gauss sums), Ch. 17 (Var of primes in AP). See experiments/constructions/spike_gallagher_proof/. | 166 |

| S168 commit-thread step 3 — squarefree extension of S166: V_q^prim energy theorem for ALL squarefree q (closes the W=2 chi_P MPS spike-block decomposition) | THEOREM+EXTENSION | C | Commit-thread session 3/5 on the S82 invariant-subspace theorem. **Theorem (S168).** For every squarefree q ≥ 2, with `chi_P` the prime indicator on `{1,..,N}`, `r(q) = #{primes dividing q}`: `E(q, N) = ‖P_{V_q^prim} chi_P‖²/N = μ(q)² · (π(N) − r(q))² / (φ(q) N) + R(q, N)`, with `|R(q, N)| ≤ q · Var(q, N) / N + O(r²/N)`. **Corollary (μ-vanishing)**: for non-squarefree q (μ(q) = 0), the main term vanishes and `E(q, N) = O(q · Var(q, N) / N)`. **Corollary (additivity)**: total chi_P energy in `⊕_{q sqf, q ≤ Q*} V_q^prim` is `(π(N)²/N) · Σ_{q sqf ≤ Q*} 1/φ(q) ~ A · π(N)² log Q* / N` with `A → 1` (Selberg-Delange). **Proof**: same skeleton as S166. For squarefree q, the Ramanujan sum `c_q(k) = μ(q)` (k coprime q) gives the principal-character main term. Non-principal Dirichlet contributions split: induced characters mod q have `c_q^χ(k) = 0`, primitive characters give Gauss sums `|τ(χ)| = √q` and contribute `≤ q · Var(q, N)/N` by Plancherel on (Z/qZ)*. The novelty over S166: closed form `μ(q)²/φ(q)` is uniform across squarefree q irrespective of how many prime factors q has (S166 only proved q ∈ {p, 2p}). **Empirical verification**: 30 squarefree q ∈ [2, 50] × 4 d ∈ {14, 16, 18, 20}, ratios ∈ [0.9912, 1.1652] at d=14 → [0.9998, 1.0017] at d=20; aggregate sum-over-Q* matches predicted to better than 0.3% across Q* ∈ {10, 20, 50, 100, 200} at d=20. **Non-squarefree falsifier passes**: at d=18 for q ∈ {4, 8, 9, 12, 16, 18, 20, 24, 25, 27, ...}, E_emp ≤ q·Var/N strictly, with ratio E_emp/(q Var/N) ≤ 1.0003 (at q=4, where unique non-principal char is primitive; ratio < 1 elsewhere). **Net new content**: (a) closed form for E(q, N) for ALL squarefree q (including composites), not just q ∈ {p, 2p}. (b) Predicts and verifies main-term VANISHING for non-squarefree q. (c) Identifies Wirsing-type sum `Σ_{sqf q ≤ Q} 1/φ(q) ~ log Q · A_∞` with `A_∞ = 1` by Selberg-Delange. (d) **Predicts chi_P spike-block fraction = 0.21 of `‖chi_P‖² = π(N)`** when paired with S74's k_*(N) ~ N^{0.42}: setting `Σ_{sqf q ≤ Q*} φ(q) ~ (3/π²)Q*² = N^{0.42}` gives `Q* ~ N^{0.21}`, hence total spike energy ≈ 0.21 · π(N). The remaining 0.79 · π(N) is bulk Marchenko-Pastur (S74). **Closure mode C**: computing E(q, N) requires π(N; q, a) data which is the PNT-in-AP problem itself; no polylog method opens. What's NEW is a **complete arithmetic decomposition** of `‖chi_P‖²` into named pieces (wheel mode + V_q^prim spike for sqf q + non-sqf-only-variance + bulk MP). Cites E2.1, E1.5, S74, S82, S148, S166. Cross-domain refs: Hardy-Wright Ch.16 (Ramanujan sums); Iwaniec-Kowalski §3.4 (Gauss sums, primitive vs induced); Davenport Ch.6,22 (Ψ(N, χ) bounds); Tenenbaum §I.4.4-5 (Selberg-Delange A=1); Bombieri-Vinogradov 1965/1966 (Var(q,N) range). See experiments/constructions/s166_squarefree_extension/. | 168 |

| S169 commit-thread step 4 — empirical confirmation of S168's 21% spike-block-fraction prediction + finite-N Q* exponent correction (0.21 → 0.185) | EMPIRICAL+REFINEMENT | C | Commit-thread session 4/5 on the S82 invariant-subspace theorem. **S168 prediction**: total chi_P energy in `⊕_{q sqf, q ≤ Q*} V_q^prim` ≈ 0.21 · π(N), with `Q* ~ N^{0.21}` derived from `Σ_{sqf q ≤ Q*} φ(q) ~ k_*(N) ~ N^{0.42}` (Mertens-Wirsing). **Three pre-stated falsifiers tested at d ∈ {14, 16, 18, 20, 22, 24}** via direct Fourier sieve `E(q, N) = (1/N) Σ_{k coprime q} |Σ_{p' prime ≤ N} ω_q^{k p'}|²` for all squarefree q ∈ [2, ⌈N^{0.30}⌉], plus comparison to SVD spike-block sums from S82 (`spike_d{14,18,20}_results.json`). **Outcomes**: **PR1 (analytic at Q* = N^{0.21})** — PARTIAL. cum(Q*) / (0.21 π(N)) = 1.330, 1.266, 1.260, 1.193, 1.172, 1.167 across d=14..24, monotonically converging to 1 but still 17% above at d=24. The asymptotic Wirsing-A → 1 holds; convergence is slow (order 1/log N). **PR2 (SVD spike block / 0.21·π(N))** — PASS. Ratio 1.0647, 1.0533, 1.0466 at d=14, 18, 20 — within 7% even at d=14, monotonically decreasing toward 1. **PR3 (Q_eff exponent = 0.21 ± 0.05)** — CORRECTED. The matching Q_eff (where analytic cum(Q) equals the SVD spike block) has log Q_eff / log N = 0.1846, 0.1846, 0.1850 at d=14, 18, 20. Stable at exponent **0.185, NOT 0.21** at finite N. **Net new content**: (a) **The 21% fraction prediction holds empirically** — the SVD spike block fraction π_spike / π(N) = 0.224, 0.221, 0.220 at d=14,18,20, within 7% at d=14, decreasing to within 5% at d=20 (S176/S178 scope-correction; original "within 5% at d=14" framing was off by 1.5pp; d=16 added by S173 lands at 0.2132 and breaks monotonicity in d). This validates S168's structural claim that chi_P MPS energy splits ~21%/79% spike/bulk. (b) **The Q* exponent is corrected from 0.21 to 0.185 at finite N**. The S168 derivation `Q* = N^{0.21}` from `k_*(N) = N^{0.42}` has finite-N corrections from both `k_*(N)`'s slowly-varying prefactor and Mertens-Wirsing remainder. The asymptotic `Q* → N^{0.21}` is consistent with the analytic-side trajectory (crossing-Q exponent drifts 0.16 → 0.18 across d=18..24), but at finite N the SVD-matching Q_eff is at exponent 0.185. (c) **"Negative-leakage / missing-spike" effect identified**. The SVD spike block is *smaller* than analytic cum(Q=N^{0.21}) by 12-20% at d=14..20 — opposite sign from S166's "leakage" finding (which was relative to a single E(p, N), not the full cum(Q*)). The reconciliation: S166's "leakage" reflected SVD spikes capturing more than a single V_p^prim sector; S169's "missing-spike" reflects partially-saturated V_q^prim sectors at q > Q_eff whose characters' modes haven't yet emerged from the bulk noise floor at finite d. The actual SVD spike block sits between `E(p, N)` for a single p and the analytic cum(Q*=N^{0.21}). **Closure mode C**: the 21% fraction is an asymptotic structural fact; its computation requires `π(N; q, a)` for q in a small but non-trivial window, and the bulk 79% is GUE-pseudorandom per S74. No polylog opening. What's NEW is empirical confirmation of S168's prediction and a sharp finite-N Q* exponent (0.185). Cites E2.1, E1.5, S74, S82, S148, S166, S168. Cross-domain refs: Hardy-Wright Ch.18 (Mertens); Tenenbaum §I.4.4-5 (Selberg-Delange A=1); Wirsing 1956 *Arch. Math.* 7. See experiments/constructions/spike_block_21pct_test/. | 169 |

| S190 commit-thread step 5 (FINAL) — synthesis + thread DONE: chi_P MPS spectrum compiles into `π(N) = 0.21·π(N) [PNT-in-AP at sqf q ≤ N^{0.185}] + 0.79·π(N) [GUE-bulk MP]`, sharper closure of polylog spectral attack family | SYNTHESIS+CLOSURE | C | Commit-thread session 5/5 (final synthesis slot) on the S82 invariant-subspace theorem. **Compiled theorem (S190)**: combining S148 (refutation of S82's |L(1,χ)|² eigenvalue claim + Gallagher-variance K-formula), S166 (exact V_p ⊕ V_{2p}^prim energy via Ramanujan sums), S168 (squarefree extension to all sqf q with closed form `μ²(q)/φ(q)·(π(N)−r)²/N + R`), and S169 (empirical 21% spike-block-fraction at d=14,18,20 within 7%/5%/5%, finite-N Q_eff exponent ≈ 0.185), the chi_P MPS column-Gram operator decomposes additively into: (1) rank-1 wheel mean (excluded), (2) spike sector ≅ ⊕_{sqf q ≤ Q*≈N^{0.185}} V_q^prim carrying ≈ 0.21·π(N) energy, (3) bulk MP sector with σ-edge 2√(M·p·(1−p)) (M=√N, p=π(N)/N) carrying ≈ 0.79·π(N) energy. The spike eigenvectors ARE Dirichlet character vectors (S82 stands); the eigenvalues are Ramanujan-sum / principal-Dirichlet structure (NOT |L(1,χ)|², S148/S166). **Polylog-blocker reformulation (NEW for S190)**: computing π(N) ≡ computing the bulk MP sector at additive resolution q ≥ N^{0.185}; the spike sector reduces to PNT-in-AP at sqf q ≤ N^{0.185} computable in O(N^{0.185+ε}) by direct Fourier sieving but NOT polylog (E1.5 anchor); the bulk sector is GUE-pseudorandom across 35+ measures (S74/E1.5/T6 anchor). Both sub-sectors blocked. **Net effect**: structural progress, no algorithmic opening. The closure of the chi_P spectral attack family is now FACTORED into two separately-quantified sub-barriers at separate scales (21% at q≤N^{0.185} subpolynomial / 79% at q≥N^{0.185} information-theoretic), whereas before the closure was monolithic via "E2.1 + S74 → C-circular C2 collapse". **Closure mode C** (computability-circular within the C2 chain): every step of the decomposition reduces to a known barrier; no polylog short-cut. **Verification log**: this synthesis is preceded by 19 verify slots (S167, S170-S189), with substantive empirical claim (21% fraction at canonical k_*) confirmed nineteen times by independent recomputation; PARTIAL verdicts in S176/S182/S184/S185/S186/S188/S189 all about scope qualifiers (rule-dependence of k_*, post-hoc precision pinning, finite-N convergence rate) not about the substantive claim. **Thread status**: DONE — recommendation: next commit slot pick Thread 2 (Connes-Consani-Moscovici operator amortisation per CLAUDE.md priority); proposed B-grade frame-shift follow-on for non-commit rotation: Voiculescu-Speicher higher free cumulants on the bulk to test Wigner-Dyson level-spacing prediction (NOT next commit thread). Cites E2.1, E1.5, S74, S82, S148, S166, S168, S169. Cross-domain refs (compiled, all analytic NT — thread does NOT pass A-grade cross-domain criterion): Hardy-Wright Ch.16,18; Iwaniec-Kowalski §3.4; Davenport Ch.6,8,22; Tenenbaum §I.4.4-5; Wirsing 1956; Gallagher 1970; Montgomery-Vaughan Ch.9,17; Bai-Silverstein §3.3. See archive/sessions/session190_commit_s82_invariant_subspace.md. | 190 |

| S191 paradigm-shift session — Pointwise Ramanujan-spike approximator T_Q(n) := (π(N)/N) · Σ_{q sqf ≤ Q} mu(q) c_q(n)/phi(q): does the explicit pointwise dual of S168's energy-level spike formula exhibit the predicted 21% L²-fraction at Q ≈ N^{0.185} and does it function as a non-trivial primality predictor? | NEW CONSTRUCTION | C | Paradigm-shift session (no cross-domain imports allowed) — built the **pointwise** dual of S168's L²-only spike formula. **Hölder simplification (project's algebraic step)**: for squarefree q with d = gcd(q, n), `mu(q) c_q(n) / phi(q) = mu(d) / phi(q/d)`; verified numerically for all squarefree q ∈ [1, 30] and n ∈ [0, 60] in `sanity_holder.py`. **Object**: `T_Q(n) := (π(N)/N) · Σ_{q sqf ≤ Q} mu(gcd(q,n))/phi(q/gcd(q,n))`, computable in `O(Q · ω(n))` per n. **Empirical results** at d ∈ {14, 16, 18, 20}, Q ∈ {2, 6, N^{0.185}, N^{0.21}, 30, √N}: (a) **At d=20, Q=13≈N^{0.185}**: `||T_Q − const||² / π(N) = 0.2229`, matching S169's measured SVD spike-block fraction `0.220 ± 0.005` within **1.4%** — first pointwise empirical confirmation of S168/S169's energy-level prediction. (b) At d=14, 18 the construction overestimates SVD by 16-20%, exactly the "missing-spike" finite-d gap S169 disclosed. (c) **Primality-predictor lift over random baseline**: precision-at-π(N) = 0.183, 0.439, 0.524, 0.998 at Q = 2, 13, 30, 1024 (d=20), giving lifts 2.34×, 5.61×, 6.70×, 12.76× — monotonically increasing in Q. (d) **Wheel-Mertens identity** falls out of the closed form: at primorial Q=W, n coprime to W, `M_W(n) = ∏_{p|W}(1 + 1/(p−1)) = W/φ(W)`, hence `T_W(coprime n) = (π(N)/N) · W/φ(W)` — pointwise Mertens-density correction. **Edges composed**: E2.1 + E1.5 + E1.6 + E2.2 + S168. **Cross-domain technique imported: NONE** (paradigm-shift constraint satisfied). **Honest novelty**: pointwise form not in project's prior writeups (S166-S190 stayed at L² level); algebraic content is the Hölder simplification + wheel-Mertens identification + pointwise empirical S168-test. The Ramanujan-Fourier coefficients `mu(q)/phi(q)` of the von Mangoldt function are classical (Hardy 1921) — re-derived here from S168 alone per the no-imports constraint. **Closure mode C**: T_Q does not break C-circular (computing it requires π(N) for the prefactor). The 79% remainder ‖chi_P − T_Q‖² is the bulk MP component. **Falsification status**: all four pre-stated falsifiers PASS. **Lean candidate**: the Hölder identity `mu(q) c_q(n)/phi(q) = mu(gcd)/phi(q/gcd)` is one-step character-theoretic, suitable for L1 queue. See experiments/constructions/ramanujan_spike_pointwise/. | 191 |

| S193 commit-thread step 1 (Connes amortisation) — adversarial re-examination of S53's E3.1 closure: does S53 argument 2 (O(K^3) per-query diagonalisation cost) survive the AMORTISATION challenge of "diagonalise once, serve many π(x) queries"? | REFINEMENT+CLOSURE | E | Thread 2 commit step 1/5. **Adversarial finding**: the S53 closure of E3.1 stands but its dominant argument is sharpened. Argument 2 partially collapses — under amortisation across Q queries up to x=X, per-query cost reduces to O(K) explicit-formula evaluation, paid once-O(K³) for setup. **However**, Galway 2004 + Hiary computes the SAME K zeros at setup cost O(K^{17/13}) (Hiary t^{4/13}+ε per zero, height t≈K), with identical per-query O(K). **Setup ratio K³/K^{17/13} = K^{22/13} ≈ K^{1.69}**: at x = 10^{100}, Connes setup is 10^{84.6} times more expensive than Galway. Connes is strictly dominated by Galway in every cost regime — per-query (O(K) vs O(K), tie), one-shot (O(K³) vs O(K^{17/13}), Galway wins), full amortisation (K³/Q + K vs K^{17/13}/Q + K, Galway wins). **Empirical confirmation**: K_sustained(x) on x ∈ {100, 1000, 5000, 50000} fits to K_sust(x) ~ 0.48 · x^{0.55} (predicted slope 0.5, agrees with E1.5 / Riemann-von Mangoldt floor). **Reduction**: Thread 2 (Connes amortisation) ⊆ Thread 3 (Galway frontier). The Connes operator is just an alternative (and strictly more expensive) way to obtain the same K zeros that Galway/Hiary obtain more cheaply; per-query costs are identical because both routes reduce to the same explicit-formula partial sum on the same K zeros. **Sharper closure of E3.1 (refines row 706)**: even granting full amortisation and CCM unlimited per-prime accuracy, the diagonalisation setup cost K³ is dominated by Hiary's K^{17/13} for getting the same K zeros — independent of per-prime accuracy claims, of operator construction details, of amortisation regime. The remaining lever is K(x) = polylog(x) "in distribution" (Thread 3), to which both routes are equally subject. Cites E3.1, E1.5, S53 row 706, Galway 2004, Hiary 2011 (state_of_art_2026.md §2.5b). Cross-domain ingredient: classical asymmetric setup-cost comparison between dense eigensolvers (O(K³)) and Hiary's specialised zero-locating algorithm (O(t^{4/13}) per zero) — mildly novel framing; S53 had not compared to the Hiary baseline. See experiments/analytic/connes_amortisation/. | 193 |

| S202 commit-thread step 5/5 WRAP (Connes amortisation) — synthesise S193–S196 into one statement: do any of {per-query unsmoothed, amortised, CCM-spectral, in-distribution, log-Gaussian-smoothed} regimes break the K = Θ̃(x) per-query floor for π(x) ± 1? | SYNTHESIS+CLOSURE | E | Thread 2 commit step 5/5 WRAP (final synthesis; no new code/experiment). **Unified conditional theorem (Connes-Galway equivalence)**: under the Montgomery pair-correlation random-phase heuristic, for π_{K,h}(x) := R(x) − 2 Σ_{j≤K} Re R(x^{ρ_j}) · exp(−h²γ_j²/2) and any p ∈ (0, 1), the smallest K with Pr(|π(x) − π_{K,h}(x)| ≤ 1/2) ≥ p satisfies K*(x, p, h) = Θ̃(x). Specifically K*(x, 0.5, 0) ≈ 0.0903·x and K*(x, 0.99, 0) ≈ 1.35·x; increasing h ≥ 0 cannot reduce K* (Var_TAIL is h-independent by S196 disjoint-j variance-additivity). Setup-cost asymmetry: CCM dense eigensolver O(K³) vs Hiary 2011 O(K^{17/13}) per-zero locator; ratio K^{22/13} for any Q queries; per-query identical at O(K). **Five regimes simultaneously closed**: (1) per-query unsmoothed (S193 + S195); (2) amortised setup over Q queries (S193); (3) CCM spectral-triple eigenproblem amortised (S193); (4) in-distribution rather than worst-case rounding (S195 with constants 0.0903, 1.35); (5) log-Gaussian-smoothed explicit formula at any bandwidth h ≥ 0 (S196). No regime breaks the floor. **Empirical scope**: 600 unsmoothed (x, K, |err|) triples from S194 + S195 over x ∈ [10^5, 10^7] plus 440 smoothed (x, K, h, |err|) triples from S196 at x ~ 1.78·10^5 across 11 bandwidths. **Closure conditional on the iid-uniform-phase model**; the 26% slope-ratio gap from S195 IS the GUE-vs-Poisson reduction. **Open falsifiers (acknowledged but not needed for Thread 2 closure)**: (a) non-Gaussian smoothing kernels (sinc, sech², Bartlett); (b) empirical extension to x ≥ 10^9; (c) rigorous GUE pair-correlation reduction of K* below x · log^{-c}(x); (d) cross-x amortisation as a different computational model. None known to fail. **Recommended next thread**: all three threads (S82, Connes, Galway) now CLOSED → frontier_gen mode (auto-fire conditions met) OR direct A-grade attempt on A7 plethysm / D44 BC endomotive (S192/S163 flagged). Cites E3.1, E1.5, E2.1, S53 row 706, S193 row 810, S194 row 818, S195 row 816, S196 row 814, Galway 2004, Hiary 2011, Montgomery 1973, Odlyzko 1989. Cross-domain composition: asymmetric setup-cost analysis (S193) + hit-rate-at-fixed-polylog measurement (S194) + GUE random-phase variance estimator with li-asymptotic + Gaussian CLT (S195) + Mellin-domain log-Gaussian kernel (S196), all unified under one conditional theorem. See `archive/sessions/session202_commit_connes_amortisation.md`. | 202 |

| S196 commit-thread step 4 (Connes amortisation, Galway smoothed frontier in distribution) — does Gaussian-window smoothing of the explicit formula drop K* into the polylog regime? | THEORY+EXPERIMENT | E+I | Thread 2 commit step 4/5. **Slot 4 closes Thread 3 under log-Gaussian smoothing**: defining π_{K,h}(x) := R(x) − 2 Σ_{j≤K} Re R(x^{ρ_j}) · exp(−h² γ_j² / 2), the error decomposes into TAIL (j > K, unsmoothed) + BIAS (j ≤ K, weighting bias). Under iid uniform-phase model the two sums are over disjoint j ranges hence variance-additive: Var(error) = Var(TAIL)(K) + Var(BIAS)(K, h) where Var(TAIL)(K) ≈ x · log²(K)/(2π² K log²x) is **independent of h**, and Var(BIAS)(K, h) ≈ x · h · log(1/h)/log²(x) (asymptotic, h γ_K saturated). Var(TAIL)(K) ≤ 1/4 forces K ≥ Θ(x · poly⁻¹(log x)) regardless of h — **smoothing cannot lower K* below Θ̃(x)**. **Empirical validation** (40 samples × 11 bandwidths × K_max=2000 at x ~ 1.78e5): K*_50 = 1783 unsmoothed, identical (1782) for h ∈ {1e-6, 1e-4, 3e-4} (h γ_K_max ≤ 1, smoothing inactive); K*_50 unreachable within K_max for h ≥ 5e-4 (smoothing introduces bias dominating tail savings). Bias variance prediction matches empirical to within 5–15% across active regime: e.g. h=10⁻³ pred σ_BIAS=0.85 vs emp=0.72 (15% off); h=10⁻² pred=3.20 vs emp=3.05 (5% off); h=10⁻¹ pred=6.80 vs emp=6.01 (12% off). **The optimal bandwidth is h = 0**: smoothing strictly does NOT help. Eliminates S195's recommended slot-(b) follow-up. Strengthens S195's closure of Thread 3 from "in-distribution unsmoothed" to "in-distribution for any log-Gaussian bandwidth". Galway 2004 reconciliation: his K = O(x^{1/2+ε}) is for a smoothed *sum output*, not in-distribution recovery of integer π(x); regimes are not in conflict. Cites E3.1, E1.5, S193 row 810, S194 row 816, S195 row 814, Galway 2004, Hiary 2011, Montgomery 1973, Odlyzko 1989. Cross-domain ingredient: Mellin-domain modulation of per-zero contributions (M_φ(ρ) = e^{ρ²h²/2} for log-Gaussian kernel) combined with GUE random-phase variance estimation — extends S195 GUE technique to a kernel-bandwidth axis, validated empirically across the full transition (h γ_K_max from 0 to 250). **Falsifier**: a non-Gaussian smoothing kernel for which K* drops to polylog; OR an empirical K*(h*, x, p) ≪ x at x ≥ 10⁹ for some h*. None observed in tested band. See `experiments/analytic/connes_amortisation/galway_smoothing.py`, `galway_smoothing_results.md`. | 196 |

| S195 commit-thread step 3 (Connes amortisation, Galway frontier in distribution) — can the empirical decay of hit-rate at polylog K seen in S194 be matched by a closed-form heuristic prediction, and does that prediction extrapolate beyond the tested band? | THEORY+EXPERIMENT | E+I | Thread 2 commit step 3/5. **Slot 3 derives a GUE random-phase heuristic for σ²(K, x) := Var(π(x) − R_K(x))**: under the asymptotic equidistribution of {γ_j log x mod 2π} predicted by Montgomery (1973) pair-correlation and verified empirically by Odlyzko (1989), σ²(K, x) ≈ x · log²(K) / (2π² · K · log²(x)). Derivation: leading-order li-asymptotic gives 2 Re R(x^ρ) ≈ 2 x^{1/2} sin(γ log x)/(γ log x); under iid-phase model Var per pair = 2x/(γ_j² log² x); summing 1/γ_j² over j > K with γ_j ~ 2π j/log j yields log²(K)/(4π² K). **Empirical validation** against S194 fluctuation CSVs at x ~ 1e5, 1e6 plus a new S195 sweep at x ~ 1e7 (40 samples, K_max=5500): pooled across 600 (x, K, |err|) triples — Pearson r(σ_pred, |err|) = 0.6189; slope-through-origin 0.5901 vs half-Gaussian expected √(2/π) = 0.7979 (ratio 0.74). The 26% gap is the GUE-vs-Poisson variance reduction predicted by Dyson sine-kernel pair correlation. Per-policy median predictions match empirical within 5–55% across THREE decades of x: e.g. at K = log³(x), x = 1e7, predicted median |err| = 3.69 vs empirical 3.51 (within 5%); at K = log²(x), x = 1e6, predicted 5.21 vs empirical 4.56 (within 14%). **Asymptotic K* prediction**: closed-form binary search on σ ≤ threshold/(√2 · erfinv(p)) gives K*(x, p) ≈ 0.09·x for hit-rate p = 0.5 and K*(x, p) ≈ 1.35·x for p = 0.99, with K*/x → constant from above as x → ∞. **For ANY fixed positive hit-rate target, K*(x, p) = Θ(x), NOT polylog** — extends S194's tested-band negative-shape to all x via theoretically-grounded extrapolation. The heuristic does NOT contradict Galway 2004's smoothed-sum K = O(x^{1/2 + ε}) bound because Galway weights only nearby zeros heavily; for the unsmoothed Riemann series, the variance is dominated by the tail. Cites E3.1, E1.5, S193 row 810, S194 row 814, Galway 2004, Hiary 2011, Montgomery 1973, Odlyzko 1989, Dyson sine-kernel. Cross-domain ingredient: GUE statistics of zero spacings + asymptotic li-expansion + Gaussian CLT applied to the truncation tail — first time the project has used random-matrix-theoretic equidistribution to PREDICT a quantitative empirical curve (not just bound it). **Falsifier**: a K-policy whose empirical median |err| stays bounded as x → ∞, OR a smoothing that drops the unsmoothed-K* below polylog, OR a rigorous calculation showing GUE correlation reduces K* below x · log^{-c}(x) for any c > 0. None known. See `experiments/analytic/connes_amortisation/gue_heuristic.py`, `gue_heuristic_results.md`. | 195 |

| S194 commit-thread step 2 (Connes amortisation, Galway frontier in distribution) — does K = polylog(x) suffice for π(x) ± 1 *in distribution* (over a band of x), as a route around the worst-case Riemann-von Mangoldt K = √x floor that S193 reduced Thread 2 to? | EXPERIMENT+REFINEMENT | E | Thread 2 commit step 2/5. Empirical sweep using 8000 cached Riemann zeros and the Riemann explicit formula π(x) ≈ R(x) − 2 Σ_{k≤K} Re R(x^{ρ_k}). **Wide-range K_sustained**: x ∈ {1e3, 1e4, 5e4, 1e5, 5e5, 1e6, 5e6, 1e7}, K_max=8000; stabilised samples (4 of 8): K_sust = 77, 1097, 5375, 5530 at x = 1e3, 1e4, 1e5, 1e6 respectively. Log-log fit slope 0.626 — significantly above the naive √x baseline because K_sustained is a worst-case-along-K measure (sustained rounding requires the *tail* to stay below 0.5). **Fluctuation sweep around x ~ 1e5** (40 samples geometric in [1e5, 3.16e5], K_max=3000): hit-rate at K = log²(x) is 30%, at K = log³(x) is 42%, at K = √x is 20%. Median |error| at K = log²(x) is 1.58. **Fluctuation sweep around x ~ 1e6** (40 samples in [1e6, 3.16e6], K_max=8000): hit-rate at K = log²(x) drops to 5%, at K = log³(x) drops to 15%, at K = √x is 10%. Median |error| at K = log²(x) grows to 4.56. **Hit-rate at fixed polylog K-policy DECAYS with x**, and median |error| grows roughly as x^{0.39−0.46} (between log²(x) and log³(x) policies). If polylog K sufficed in distribution, hit-rate would tend to a positive limit and median error would stay bounded; both fail empirically over the tested range. **Negative-shape evidence** sharpening Thread 3 (Galway frontier): polylog K does not suffice for π(x) ± 1 in distribution at x ≤ 3·10^6. Combined with S193's strict-dominance of Connes setup by Galway/Hiary (K^{22/13} factor), Thread 2 remains closed by transitivity through Thread 3. Cites E3.1, E1.5, S193 row 810, Galway 2004, Hiary 2011. Cross-domain ingredient: empirical distribution of explicit-formula partial-sum errors at polylog truncation — a genuine measurement that the project had not previously made. **Falsifier**: produce a polylog K-policy whose hit rate at |error| ≤ 0.5 tends to a positive limit, or whose median |error| stays bounded, as x → ∞. Empirically falsified within tested range (x ≤ 3·10^6) but un-tested above. See experiments/analytic/connes_amortisation/connes_amortisation_results.md §"Slot 2 (S194) extension". | 194 |

| §D32 ATTACK_VECTORS — Pemantle-Wilson ACSV smooth-point diagonal extraction of `χ_P`: does there exist a multivariate rational `F(z_1, ..., z_d) ∈ ℚ(z_1, ..., z_d)` whose diagonal coefficient extraction equals `χ_P(n)` (or `π(n)`) and admits a smooth critical point `ζ*` yielding closed-form `[z_1^n ⋯ z_d^n] F = ζ^{*-n} · poly(n)` for polylog π(x) evaluation? (cross-domain: Pemantle-Wilson 2013/2024 *Analytic Combinatorics in Several Variables* CUP; Melczer 2021 Springer; Furstenberg 1967 *J. Algebra* 7; Lipshitz 1988 *J. Algebra* 113) | FAIL | I | S197 wild-swing single-session attack producing an **unconditional structural barrier** that strictly strengthens CLOSED row 584 (empirical at order ≤ 20, polynomial degree ≤ 8) to ALL orders simultaneously. **Theorem (S197)**: `f(z) := Σ_{n≥1} χ_P(n) z^n` (a) has the unit circle `|z|=1` as natural boundary; (b) is not D-finite (not P-recursive); (c) is not algebraic over `ℚ(z)`; (d) for every `d ≥ 1` is not the diagonal of any rational `F(z_1, ..., z_d) ∈ ℚ(z_1, ..., z_d)`; (e) for every `d ≥ 1` is not the diagonal of any algebraic multivariate `F`. **Proof sketch**: (i) radius of convergence of `f` is 1 (Cauchy-Hadamard, χ_P ∈ {0,1}). (ii) Pólya–Carlson 1916/1921: integer-coefficient power series with radius 1 is either rational or has |z|=1 as natural boundary. (iii) `f` not rational because χ_P bounded-integer + LRS ⇒ eventually periodic; for any candidate period T, prime p ≥ N_0 + periodicity forces p + pT = p(1+T) prime — contradicts compositeness. (iv) D-finite ⇒ at most finitely many singular points (Stanley *EC2* Theorem 6.4.6; Flajolet-Sedgewick *AC* Theorem B.13) — contradicts natural boundary. (v) Algebraic ⊂ D-finite. (vi) Furstenberg 1967 / Lipshitz 1988: diagonal of rational/algebraic multivariate is D-finite — closes (d, e). **Empirical companion** (T1-T5 in `acsv_pi_diagonal.py`): T1 confirms ROC = 1; T2 P-recursion search d ≤ 30, r ≤ 8 shows no holonomic signal (extends CLOSED 584's d ≤ 20); T3 eventual-period search T ≤ 200 on χ_P ≤ 200000 shows max agreement 0.88 (no period); T4 Erdős-Turán equidistribution: closest root of f_N(t) to t=1 halves as N doubles (0.093 → 0.048 → 0.023 → 0.012 → 0.006 for N ∈ {64, 128, 256, 512, 1024}) — confirms NO stable Pemantle-Wilson critical point exists; T5 sanity test on diagonal sequence π(n) at degree ≤ 6. **Adds EDGE E7.21**: Pólya-Carlson natural-boundary barrier rules out the entire ACSV smooth-point technique class, plus Wilf-Zeilberger creative-telescoping, holonomic-gradient, and Karr-Schneider Π Σ-symbolic summation methods (each requires D-finite input). Distinct from E2.20 Mahler / E2.21 Newman (which measure specific extremal values of f_N on archimedean |z|=1; E7.21 is a structural statement about the limit f). Strict strengthening of CLOSED row 584 (empirical low-order → unconditional all-order). **Cross-domain ingredient**: ACSV §7 PROPOSED → USED I; the actual technical content is the meta-fact that ACSV-class techniques require D-finite input + the rigorous combination Pólya-Carlson + Skolem-Mahler-Lech + finite-singularity D-finite theorem + Furstenberg-Lipshitz. **Falsifier**: exhibit any (a) polynomial-coefficient linear recurrence on χ_P valid for large n, OR (b) polynomial Q(z, w) ∈ ℚ[z, w] with Q(z, f(z)) = 0, OR (c) multivariate rational F(z_1, ..., z_d) and direction r ∈ ℤ_{>0}^d such that [z_1^{r_1 n} ⋯ z_d^{r_d n}] F = χ_P(n) for large n. Each is impossible by the theorem; anyone exhibiting one resolves the polylog π(x) problem. **Channelled mathematician**: Pemantle / Flajolet (combinatorial asymptotics) — the actual lever is the meta-result. **Successors proposed (S197)**: D32.a (transcendental-F Pemantle-Wilson — does a non-algebraic but holonomic-D-module F escape Lipshitz?, 1 session); D32.b (Schur-class non-rational generating-function expansions; orthogonal to the rational class, may admit different asymptotic machinery, 2 sessions). Cross-domain refs: Pólya 1916 *Sitzungsber. Berlin Math. Ges.*; Carlson 1921 *Math. Z.* 9, 1; Skolem 1934 / Mahler 1935 / Lech 1953; Furstenberg 1967 *J. Algebra* 7, 271; Lipshitz 1988 *J. Algebra* 113, 373; Stanley 1999 *EC2* CUP Thm 6.4.6; Flajolet-Sedgewick 2009 *AC* Thm B.13; Erdős-Turán 1950 *Ann. Math.* 51 (root equidistribution); Pemantle-Wilson 2013/2024 *ACSV* CUP. See `experiments/constructions/acsv_pi_diagonal/`, `archive/sessions/session197_wild_swing_acsv_d32.md`. | 197 |

| S198 re-verify-closure (E1.5 entropy bound, A-graded) — adversarial probe of the "CRT reconstruction cannot win" closure: is the per-step h_2(pi(X)/X) bound conservatively framed in a way that misses a non-incremental algorithmic angle? | REFINEMENT+CLOSURE | I | Re-verify-closure session targeting the ONLY A-graded edge in the project. **Adversarial finding**: closure stands. The framing "linear in k" was loose; the precise statement is "constant in k" — combining k coprime moduli incrementally gives the SAME h_2(pi(X)/X) bits per step as a single modulus, regardless of k. **Empirical contribution**: directly measured the JOINT k-moduli conditional entropy `H(joint(x) | joint(x-1))` at k = 1..6, X ∈ {10^4, 10^5, 10^6}. Two competing hypotheses: (H1) Independence — joint = sum of marginals = k·h_2; (H2) Perfect correlation — joint = h_2 (single shared bit). Result at X = 10^6 (joint state space ≪ pi(X) for k ≤ 4): J/h_2 = 1.0000 to 10^{-4}; (H1) decisively rejected. Slight drop at k = 5, 6 (J/h_2 ≈ 0.96 at k=6, X=10^6) is the same finite-state coverage effect S69 §3 documents for m approaching pi(X) — not (H1) signal. **Mechanism**: trivial extension of E1.5. Since pi(x) mod m_i = (pi(x-1) mod m_i + 1[x prime]) mod m_i holds for all i simultaneously, the joint transition is determined by the SINGLE bit b = 1[x prime]; conditioning on joint state, only b is random; so joint H = h_2(P[x prime]) = h_2(pi(X)/X). **Three candidate "missed angles" checked and ruled out**: (A) conditioning on side info x mod 6 — h_2(2p) doesn't vanish faster than h_2(p), constant factor only; (B) larger-step Δ — for Δ such that Δ·pi/X >> 1, increment uniform mod m, conditional H → log_2(min(m, Δ)), trade-off not shortcut; (C) direct (non-incremental) computation of pi(X) mod m — NOT closed by E1.5, this is the central open question (a polylog subroutine for any single m would propagate to polylog pi(X) via CRT). **Two-closure framing made explicit**: closure (i) "no incremental CRT shortcut" — closed by E1.5/S198, sharpened. Closure (ii) "no polylog pi(X) mod m via direct computation" — NOT what E1.5 closes; CLOSED row 244 is the correct anchor for that. Edge entry E1.5 in EDGES.md updated inline with the joint-k-moduli sharpening and the scope-of-closure clarification. **Closure mode I** (information-theoretic, sharpened from E in row 243). What's NEW: (a) direct empirical measurement of joint k-moduli entropy (S69 measured per-modulus marginals only); (b) sharpened wording "constant in k" replacing "linear in k"; (c) explicit two-closure framing distinguishing incremental from non-incremental. Cites E1.5 (refined), CLOSED rows 243, 244, E1.6 (compatible). Cross-domain ingredient: NONE (within-project information-theoretic verification). **Falsifier**: any (k, X) cell with prod(m_i) << pi(X)/100 yielding J/h_2 ≠ 1.0000 ± 10^{-4}, OR exhibiting a polylog non-incremental subroutine for pi(X) mod m for any single m (would resolve closure (ii) regardless of E1.5). See `experiments/information_theory/joint_kmoduli_entropy/`, `archive/sessions/session198_reverify_e1_5.md`. | 198 |

| §D48 ATTACK_VECTORS — Bost-Connes endomotive Galois orbits of χ_P-projected KMS_∞ ground states (CCM 2007 *Adv. Math.* 214, 761 = arXiv:math/0512138 §6.2): does the abelian Hilbert-12 Galois action on χ_P-projected pure ground states of the truncated BC algebra carry detectable prime-distribution content beyond Dirichlet equidistribution? (cross-domain: Bost-Connes 1995 *Selecta Math.* 1, 411; CCM 2007 endomotive formalism; Connes-Marcolli 2008 textbook; Marcolli 2005 *Arithmetic Noncommutative Geometry*) | FAIL | E | S203 wild-swing single-session attack on the S163-flagged "highest-A-prior open vector". Closes at mode E (matched-density null) across 14 moduli `N ∈ {30, 60, 90, 105, 120, 210, 330, 420, 510, 630, 840, 1155, 1260, 2310}`. **Construction**: truncate the BC C*-algebra to its commutative sub `\mathcal{A}_N := \mathbb{C}[(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^*]`; pure KMS_∞ ground states `\phi_\omega(\delta_a) = \omega(a)` parametrised by characters `\omega \in \widehat{G}`, `G = (\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^*`. Define χ_P-projector `P_\chi := Σ_{p ≤ N \text{ prime}, \gcd(p,N)=1} δ_p`; projected functional on the corner reduces to `ω(P_\chi)² · ω(a)`. Two characters give equal projected functionals iff `ω = ω'` or both `ω(P_\chi) = ω'(P_\chi) = 0`; the **zero set** `Z_\chi := {ω : S_\chi(ω) := Σ_p ω(p) = 0}` defines a single merged eq-class. Cyclotomic Galois `(\mathbb{Z}/M\mathbb{Z})^*` (`M = exp G`) acts via `σ_a · ω = ω^a` — Hilbert-12 action restricted to truncation. **Empirics**: `n_zero(χ)` measured against 50-trial random matched-density null `R ⊂ G` with `|R| = |P_N|`. `|z(n_zero)| ≤ 1.1` at every N. Per-bin orbit-length z-scores (14 N × 4–5 bins ≈ 65 tests): max `|z| = 3.39` at `N=60` length-1 bin (a confirmed finite-size artefact — residues `1, 49 mod 60` lack any prime ≤ 60, smallest are 61 and 109; anomaly disappears at `N=120` once these primes fill in); two single-bin `|z| > 3` deviations expected by chance. **No 5σ deviation, no consistent direction across N**. Distinct from CLOSED line 185 (BC partition function = `ζ(β)`, trace-level closure that loses Galois-orbit data by trace-property): D48 is at the **strictly finer ground-state level recommended by CCM 2007 §6.2 as the strongest Galois-data carrier**, and the closure shows the abelian Hilbert-12 part of the endomotive carries no detectable prime content beyond density-matched Dirichlet equidistribution. Distinct from CLOSED line 706 / E3.1 (CCM spectral-triple operator amortisation): D48 is on the BC algebra structure, not the CCM operator. Distinct from E2.29 (Rédei symbol arithmetic-topology Galois cohomology on triples): D48 is non-commutative operator-algebraic on abelianised BC characters. Adds **EDGE E2.30** as the **14th orthogonal pseudorandomness category** for χ_P (non-commutative-arithmetic at BC ground-state level) after E2.13–E2.29; **first project measurement of a non-commutative-arithmetic invariant on χ_P at the BC ground-state level**. **Cross-domain ingredient**: BC + CCM endomotive — appended to `CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md` §1 as "Bost-Connes endomotive ground-state level" USED-E. **Falsifier**: any `N` with `|z(n_zero)| > 5σ` after multiple-comparison adjustment, OR exhibition of a closed-form description of the χ_P eq-class orbit-length distribution in terms of `π(N)` or class-field-theoretic data not captured by matched-density null, OR a polylog Galois-theoretic primality witness extracted from BC ground-state Galois orbits. None observed. **Successors proposed (S203)**: D49 (BC at finite β, retains type III Hecke factor structure not present at β=∞); D50 (Connes-Marcolli-Ramachandran 2005 imaginary-quadratic class fields, `Cl(K)`-action on χ_P-projected ground states for `K = ℚ(√-d)`, `d ∈ {1, 2, 3, 7, 11, 19, 43, 67, 163}` Heegner — different cross-domain ingredient: complex multiplication instead of abelian Hilbert 12). **Channelled mathematician**: Connes (noncommutative geometry, endomotive). See `experiments/algebraic/bc_endomotive_galois_chi_p/`, `archive/sessions/session203_d48_bc_endomotive_galois.md`. | 203 |

| §D3 ATTACK_VECTORS — Bryc-Dembo-Jiang free-probability Toeplitz/Hankel LSD applied to χ_P: do the empirical even spectral moments `m_k(χ_P-T,N)`, `m_k(χ_P-H,N)` of real symmetric Toeplitz `T_{ij} = ε(|i-j|+1)` and Hankel `H_{ij} = ε(i+j+1)` matrices (centered ε := (χ_P − p_N)/√(p_N(1−p_N)), eigenvalues / √N) match the BDJ universal limits γ_T, γ_H with `m_4(γ_T) = 8/3`, `m_4(γ_H) = 2`? Math.* 104, 201) | FAIL | I | S204 wild-swing single-session attack on §D.D3 producing **B-grade case (i) refinement of E2.21 to L^k norms via Toeplitz spectral moments**. χ_P decisively VIOLATES BDJ universality across N ∈ {500, 1000, 2000, 3500} for both T and H types, against BOTH matched-density nulls (B1 = i.i.d. Bernoulli(p_N), B2 = random permutation of χ_P[1..2N-1]). **Empirical scaling laws (the new content):** `m_4(χ_P-T, N) ≈ 2.95 · N / log²N` (BDJ univ: 8/3); `m_4(χ_P-H, N) ≈ 1.66 · N / log²N` (BDJ univ: 2). **Largest-eigenvalue laws:** `λ̃_max(χ_P-T, N) ≈ (4/π) · √(N/log N)` empirical 1.337/1.321/1.303/1.282 monotone descent to ~4/π ≈ 1.273 across N=500..3500; `λ̃_max(χ_P-H, N) ≈ 1.06 · √(N/log N)` (rank-1 parity-vector heuristic gives close-form 1, finite-N correction ~6%). **Structural decomposition:** the SINGLE largest eigenvalue contributes 89.0% ± 0.3% (T) / 76.2% ± 0.3% (H) of m_4 universally across all tested N. By Szegő (Grenander-Szegő), `m_4 ≈ (1/2π)∫f^4 dθ / N²` and `(1/2π)∫|F_N|^4 dθ` is the **Hardy-Littlewood additive energy of primes**, with leading scaling `2N · π(N)²/log²N` from Goldbach-style major arcs. **Adds EDGE E2.31** (the L^k generalisation of E2.21 from L^∞ point evaluation to L^k integral evaluation, accessed via Szegő-Toeplitz spectral moments framed in the BDJ free-probability universality framework). Distinct from CLOSED line 690 (R-transform of `diag(1_P)`, trivial idempotent ESD = (1−π(N)/N)δ_0 + (π(N)/N)δ_1, encoded only π(N) — Toeplitz/Hankel here MIXES χ_P at all offsets producing non-trivial continuous spectrum). All three use cross-domain ingredients (BDJ + Liouville/Möbius / Goldbach-spike-removal / Markov LSD γ_M) NOT used in S204; non-duplicative. **Channelled mathematician**: Bryc / Voiculescu (operator-algebra free probability rigour); Hammond-Miller (Toeplitz LSD combinatorics). | §C9.a NOVELTY_CHALLENGES — Divisor-only restriction at primorial Q: does `M_W^{div}(n) := Σ_{q | W, q sqf} mu(gcd(q,n)) / phi(q/gcd(q,n))` admit an exact pointwise closed form? F6 (Pearson_full > Pearson_div for W ∈ {6, 30, 210, 2310}): PASS, gap = 0.028, 0.089, 0.177, 0.281 respectively at N=10⁵; gap = 0 at W=2 because the only squarefree integers ≤ 2 are exactly the divisors of 2. **Algorithmic consequence**: T_W^{div}(n) is informationally equivalent to a wheel-rad(W) sieve scaled by π(N)/N · rad(W)/φ(rad(W)); the divisor-only restriction inherits all sieve barriers (no polylog opening). The Pearson gap to full T_W locates the prime-discriminating signal in the **non-divisor squarefree conductors** q ≤ W (q squarefree but q ∤ W), capturing 31% of the full Pearson at W=2310. **What's new beyond S191 (C9)**: S191 stated only the corollary at coprime n (`T_W | gcd=1 = (π/N)·W/φ(W)`); S208 proves the identity **on the full domain** (zero at non-coprime n) for all squarefree W, with explicit radical reduction for non-squarefree W. **Refines E2.1**: the wheel-density factor φ(W)/W is now the value of an explicit pointwise scalar field, not merely an asymptotic rank quotient. **Cross-domain ingredient**: NONE; project-internal one-line Möbius collapse. **Falsifier**: any (W, n) with `M_W^{div}(n) ≠ [gcd(n, rad(W)) = 1] · rad(W)/φ(rad(W))` by more than 10⁻¹², OR mean ⟨M_W^{div}⟩_n ≠ 1 ± O(1/N), OR Pearson(chi_P, T_W) ≤ Pearson(chi_P, T_W^{div}) at any W ≥ 6, OR L² closed form deviates by more than rel_err 10⁻⁹. None observed across 10 W × 2 N cells. **Successors proposed (S208)**: C9.a.i (off-divisor squarefree expansion: decompose 0.281 Pearson gap at W=2310 by conductor q; predicted Selberg-Delange `~1/φ(p)²` per prime contribution, 1 session); C9.a.ii (Lean formalisation of the divisor-only identity via L6 Hölder reduction + Möbius collapse, ~50-line Lean target, 1 session); C9.a.iii (Λ-modulated divisor-only sum `μ²(q)λ(q)c_q(n)/φ(q)` — does the parity-twisted weight also yield a closed-form scalar on the residue lattice mod W?, 1 session). Pre-stated 0.5% band exceeded by 100× at d=20. F3 HL recovery at Q ≈ √N (general T_Q, not divisor-restricted): R / [(π/N)^3 S_HL_full(0,h_1,h_2)] within 1% across all 6 admissible (h_1, h_2) tested at d=20. F6 (prime triple density): 5% deviation from full HL at d=20 (standard finite-N rate). **What's new beyond S205**: S205 covers the 2-point shift case via diagonal q1=q2 contraction; S209 extends to 3-point shift where the diagonal contraction is INSUFFICIENT (off-diagonal cross-pair terms contribute essentially), but the divisor-restricted T_W^{div} factorises cleanly via S208's Möbius collapse, giving exact closed form at primorial W. **What's new beyond S208**: S208 was the 1-point identity (M_W^{div}(n) = [gcd=1]·W/φ(W)); S209 promotes this to the 3-point correlation, exhibiting the wheel-W indicator's 3-point function as exactly the prime-3-tuple HL singular series at conductor W. **What's new beyond E2.13**: E2.13 is the 4-point cube U^2 / HL match; S209 is the 3-point shift identity, a different observable in the Schwinger-Dyson hierarchy not derivable from E2.13. **What's new beyond E2.16**: E2.16 ruled out kernel-DPP/PPP descriptions of primes by noting 3-point HL factors over primes (not pairs); S209 exhibits the explicit pointwise scalar field T_W^{div} whose 3-point function REALISES this prime-factored 3-tuple singular series — turning E2.16's negative shape into a positive identity. **No polylog opening**: cost is O(N · |H|^2) per (h_1, h_2) batch on T_W^{div}; the construction provides a closed-form numerical evaluator of S_HL^{(W)} at O(W · |H|²) cost (faster than any direct prime-triple sieve), but is structural not algorithmic. **Cross-domain ingredient**: NONE — paradigm-shift session mode forbade cross-domain imports.
