(4) MC iid-Gaussian lag-1 |autocorr| null (4000 trials): p95=0.250, p99=0.328, p999=0.437; data max |ac| over lags 1..10 = 0.283 at lag 1, below MC p99, Bonferroni-corrected over 10 lags p ≈ 0.4 — F2 (max |ac| ≥ MC p999) FAILS; (5) HKM cost test via fresh subprocess sympy.primepi with no sieve-cache reuse: median T(2^k) / mean(T(2^k − 1), T(2^k + 1)) = 0.998 / 1.047 / 0.995 at k = 28, 30, 32 (avg 1.013) — F3 (dyadic speedup ratio ≤ 0.5) FAILS. **All three F-claims FAIL → B-NEGATIVE per-query closure.** Note: an earlier v1 result with Li-baseline gave spurious lag-1 = 0.87 driven by the smooth -Li(√x)/2 Möbius-series correction; switching to R-baseline (which absorbs the smooth Σ μ(n)/n Li(x^{1/n}) series) eliminates the confound. **Structural reason** (two independent mechanisms): (a) information-theoretic — the explicit-formula zero-sum −Σ_ρ R(x^ρ) ≈ −2√x Σ_n cos(γ_n log x − arg(ρ_n))/|ρ_n| has phases γ_n · k log 2 mod 2π Weyl-equidistributed in k for every γ_n with γ_n irrational, independently of the dyadic form; (b) algorithmic — Lucy / Meissel-Lehmer outer loop processes {n ≤ √x} and x^{2/3}-smooth counts which carry no binary-representation side-channel. **What this rules out**: (i) any algorithm computing π(2^k) substantially faster than generic-x π(x) at the same magnitude; (ii) any compressed representation of (π(2^k))_k of size below the trivial Σ_k k = O(N²) output bit-budget; (iii) any short-recurrence/closed-form for residuals r_R(k) at the n=56 scale. **What this does NOT close**: cross-k batched amortisation (Thread-5 / Correlation-Dichotomy shape transposed to dyadic family) — the shared zero database (γ_n)_{n=1..K} is k-independent and admits per-query O(K) cost with K = polylog. Registered as F6.b in NOVELTY_CHALLENGES.md and P7.b in OPEN_POSITIVE_TARGETS.md. **Successor challenges proposed in S246**: F6.a (cross-modulus generalisation x_k = m^k for m ∈ {3, 5, 6, 10, 30}), F6.b (batched-on-k amortisation), F6.c (rigorous Ω(N²) lower bound from output bit-budget). **Cross-domain ingredient**: Berlekamp-Massey linear complexity over GF(2) (signal-processing standard). CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md addition: "Berlekamp-Massey + Monte Carlo random-shuffle null for testing low-complexity linear recurrences in arithmetic sequences — USED-E (S246), confirmed n=56 too short to discriminate polylog from random; minimum useful length n > log²(n) · 5 ≈ 200". **Cites** E1.1 (per-query HKM x^{2/3} bound; refined inline with dyadic per-query no-amortisation note), E1.5 (per-step 0.537 entropy; consistent with Weyl-equidistribution mechanism). See `experiments/wildcard/dyadic_pi_structure/{dyadic_pi_structure.py, dyadic_pi_structure_results.md, raw_data.json, hkm_cost.json, summary.json}`, `archive/sessions/session246_f6_dyadic_pi_structure.md`. | 246 |
| §P.P2 batched π_h(x) on h, slot 2 — does the within-window cross-x ensemble of HL residuals r_h(x) = π_h(x) − S_h·li_2(x) at fixed (anchor, h) follow a half-Gaussian shape (Thread 7 slot 2 transposition); is there a stable F_GUE-like factor σ_eff/σ_pred? | EXPERIMENT (B-MIXED: cross-x within-window FAILS half-Gaussian KS due to random-walk-like correlation; cross-h at fixed x PASSES with median KS p ≈ 0.7-0.8; σ-suppression ratio in [0.36, 0.70] but NOT decade-stable like Thread 7's F_GUE = 0.755) | E (E1.5 + S195 + S224 + S240..S244 + S418 — methodological finding identifies cross-h as the natural ensemble for HL residual analysis; refines slot-1 dichotomy into ensemble-aware form) | Thread 8 commit step 2/4 (S419). Built `experiments/analytic/batched_pi_h/slot2_multisample.py` (~340 lines): batched-sieve via numpy AND-shift cumsum (memory-efficient; 720 rows in 21s total wall time). Three anchors x ∈ {10⁶, 10⁷, 10⁸} × N=30 log-uniform x_j in quarter-decade [x_anchor, x_anchor·10^{0.25}] × 8 representative h ∈ {2, 6, 14, 22, 30, 42, 90, 198} (S_h ∈ [1.32, 4.0]). Three statistical aggregates: (A) per-(anchor, h) cross-x KS (24 cells, N=30 each); (B) per-(anchor, x_j) cross-h KS (90 cells, N=8 each); (C) pooled per-anchor (N=240 each). **Headline finding**: cross-x at fixed (anchor, h) FAILS half-Gaussian KS — median p_eff = 0.033, only 8/24 cells with p > 0.1. Cells that fail show median/σ_eff ≈ mean/σ_eff ≈ 0.95 (theoretical half-Gaussian = 0.6745/0.7979) — clustered |r| values, not Gaussian-tailed. Within-window range diagnostic: (max|r| − min|r|)/σ_eff has mean ≈ 1.5 across cells; residuals drift but don't decohere. **Structural diagnosis**: HL_h(x) = S_h · li_2(x) is analytic-smooth, π_h(x) is step; the residual is a slowly-varying random-walk in x (drift O(σ_eff) within quarter-decade, no oscillation), unlike Thread 7's zero-truncation residual which oscillates with wavelength ~1/log x. Cross-x sampling within a small log-window does NOT give iid samples for HL residuals. **Cross-h at fixed (anchor, x_j) PASSES half-Gaussian KS**: median p_eff = 0.840 / 0.698 / 0.804 at anchors 10⁶/10⁷/10⁸; **all 90/90 cells with p > 0.1**. The cross-h ensemble is the natural sampling for HL residual shape analysis. **σ-suppression ratio σ_eff/σ_pred_pois = 0.36-0.70** across 24 (anchor, h) cells, where σ_pred_pois = √(S_h·li_2(x)) is the HL random-residual Poisson prediction. Empirical RMS is consistently SMALLER than Poisson — analogue of Thread 7's GUE pair-correlation suppression (correlations among primes via singular series itself reduce the variance below random-residual). NOT decade-stable: 0.50 → 0.70 → 0.41 across 10⁶/10⁷/10⁸; the 10⁷ bump driven by anomaly cell (10⁷, h=6) cousin-prime σ_eff/σ_pois = 1.64 (signed range = 481, persistent large residual episode). Excluding h=6: ratio narrows to ~0.36-0.43, still mildly drifting with x. **Pooled per-anchor (N=240) KS**: borderline at 10⁶ (p=0.082), reject at 10⁷ (p<0.001, h=6 outlier), borderline at 10⁸ (p=0.217). Half-Gaussian shape holds at the cell level but pooled hides the anomaly. **What this rules out**: (i) the original Thread-7-direct-transposition hypothesis "HL has stable F_GUE-like σ_eff/σ_pred ≈ 0.74 across decades" — directly tested, ratio drifts in [0.36, 0.70]; (ii) cross-x sampling within quarter-decade as a fair iid ensemble for HL residual statistics — methodologically rejected. **What this does NOT close**: (i) cross-h ensemble at fixed x as a path to a precise σ-formula for HL — slot 2 demonstrates shape but does not derive σ_eff(x, h) closed form; (ii) Q-truncated singular series cost-vs-error tradeoff — slot 3; (iii) anchor extension to 10⁹/10¹⁰ to test whether σ_eff/σ_pois stabilises — slot 3 side measurement; (iv) named-exponent error bound for polylog HL evaluator — slot 3-4 wrap. **Methodological warning** (preserved for slot 3+ design): within-window cross-x sampling is misleading for HL residual analysis; use cross-h at fixed x. **Cross-domain ingredient**: Hardy-Littlewood random-residual heuristic (predicted Poisson scale √(S_h · li_2(x))) plus Kolmogorov-Smirnov half-Gaussian goodness-of-fit (signal-processing standard). CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md unchanged. **Cites** E1.5 (per-query bit content), S195 (variance machinery transposed to h-axis), S224 (Correlation Dichotomy reference), S418 (slot-1 dichotomy this slot extends), S240-S244 (Thread 7 P3 — ensemble-shape contrast). Built `experiments/analytic/batched_pi_h/slot3_q_truncation.py` (~370 lines): 26-h ensemble with max-prime-factor spanning [3, 2003] (slot-2 set + 18 additions covering h=2p for p ∈ {19, 23, 29, 31, 41, 53, 79, 109, 151, 199, 251, 401, 503, 599, 1009, 2003} plus mixed h=606=2·3·101 and h=3030=2·3·5·101) × 9 Q-values {30, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000, 2000, 5000, ∞} × 2 anchors x ∈ {10⁷, 10⁸} × 5 log-uniform x_j in quarter-decade. Empirical-vs-predicted comparison at 16 (anchor, x_j, Q) cells: 5–25% of σ_emp across all cells, closest at and above the knee. Decomposition is intrinsic-noise (slot-2's σ_∞) + h-deterministic truncation, treated as quadrature-additive (mild anti-correlation observed at low Q). **Knee scaling Q* ≈ √x/log x** verified at both anchors: x=10⁷ empirical knee_Q=200, knee_max_p=199 (predicted √x/log x = 196); x=10⁸ empirical knee_Q ∈ {1000, 2000}, knee_max_p ∈ {599, 1009} (predicted 543, off-grid between 500/1000). Knee defined as smallest Q with σ_HL_Q ≤ 1.05·σ_HL_∞. **Sharp half-Gaussian shape transition Q=100 → Q=200**: cross-h KS median p_eff jumps from 0.0015 (Q=100, 0/10 cells pass p>0.1) to 0.96 (Q=200, 10/10 cells pass), tracking the σ-knee. Below the knee, truncation contamination dominates intrinsic noise and breaks the slot-2 half-Gaussian profile; above the knee, cross-h shape recovers across all 10 cells. **σ_HL_Q values across (anchor, x_j, Q)**: at x=10⁷, σ drops from 638→446→357→174→168→171→172→173→173 across Q={30, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000, 2000, 5000, ∞}; at x=10⁸, 5163→3615→2996→528→441→419→405→409→409. Plateau at Q ≥ 2000 (all max_p_h in our set ≤ 2003). **Per-h cost dimension**: S_Q(h) by trial division costs min(Q, √h) odd integers tested. For h_max=4006, full S_∞ is 31 steps, Q=30 is 15 steps (50% saving). Average cost across 26-h ensemble: 7.0 (Q=30) → 8.5 (Q=∞), 18% saving. **Q-truncation gives no asymptotic cost saving in original P2 polylog regime** (h ≤ poly log x ⇒ √h ≤ poly log x ≤ Q* = √x/log x); knee Q* is *algebraic* in x, not polylog. **What this rules out**: (i) Q-truncation as an algorithmic novelty for original P2 — knee Q* is asymptotically larger than √h_max in the polylog-h regime; (ii) the hypothesis that σ_HL_Q decreases monotonically with Q across all measured Q (no plateau) — directly REJECTED, clean knee at Q=200/1000-2000; (iii) the hypothesis that σ_HL_Q at Q = max_p_h(h_set) exceeds σ_HL_∞ by > 10% — directly REJECTED, σ_HL_5000 = σ_HL_∞ exactly at 5 decimal places. **What this does NOT close**: (i) conditional theorem rigorising the variance decomposition — slot 4 (4a) target; (ii) third-decade scaling validation at x=10⁹ — slot 4 (4b) candidate; (iii) worst-case (per-h) tail bound — half-Gaussian-shape implies √(log N) worst-case scaling but not formally derived; (iv) effective constant F (slot-2's 0.36-0.70) as part of the named exponent — packaged in (1+o(1)). **Cross-domain ingredient**: same as Goldston-Montgomery 1987 bilinear-form decomposition of L²-typical zero-truncation variance (S244), here transposed from the K-axis (Thread 7) to the Q-axis (Thread 8); intrinsic-vs-truncation split mirrors diagonal-vs-off-diagonal structure with Q's role analogous to K. Wrote `experiments/analytic/batched_pi_h/slot4_theorem.md` (~440 lines, 14 sections): Theorem A' statement with explicit Q ∈ ℕ ∪ {∞} validity range; Knee Corollary Q* ≍ √x/log x derived from quadrature equality of intrinsic vs truncation contributions; Corollary B' polylog-time HL evaluator with named-exponent cross-h L²-typical error; full proof structure (4 steps: decomposition, intrinsic via HLRH(b), cross via HLRH(a)+HLRH(c), combining); §5 discussion of HLRH role vs Cramér model; §6 knee corollary derivation; §7 algorithmic complexity analysis; §8 K-axis ↔ h-axis analogy table mapping S244 proof structure to slot 4; §9 falsifiability statements; §10 status table of new vs existing content; §11 edges; §12 cross-domain ingredient (Goldston-Montgomery 1987 USED-T transposed K → h); §13 self-grade B; §14 next-thread recommendation. **Headline result — Theorem A' (T-A')**: for admissible h-ensemble H with N → ∞ under HLRH(H), (1/N)·Σ_h(π_h(x)−S_Q(h)·li_2(x))² = (1+o(1))·F²_H·S̄_H·li_2(x) + (1/N)·Σ_{h:max_p_h>Q}ε_Q(h)²·li_2(x)² + o(S̄_H·li_2(x)) for any Q ∈ ℕ ∪ {∞}. **HLRH = Hardy-Littlewood Random-Residual Hypothesis**: three asymptotic statements on cross-h ensembles at fixed x — (a) first-moment vanishing (1/N)Σr_∞(x,h_i) = o(√li_2), (b) second-moment asymptotic to F²_H·S̄_H·li_2(x), (c) cross-h decoherence E_x[r_∞(h_i)·r_∞(h_j)] = o(S̄_H·li_2). HLRH is the cross-h analogue of S195 random-phase + S244 Montgomery pair-correlation transposed K-axis → h-axis; implied by strong k-tuple HL conjecture (k=4) plus GUE-side correlations on prime-pair-count joint distributions; currently unproven beyond Bombieri-Davenport linear-bound levels. **Corollary B' (C-B')**: for h-batches with max h ≤ (log x)^β and |H| ≤ poly(log x), the full HL_∞ evaluator costs O(N·√(max h)) ⊆ poly(log x) per batch with cross-h L²-typical error ε_typ(x, H) ≤ (1+o(1))·F_H·√(S̄_H·li_2(x)) ≍ F_H·√(S̄_H·x)/log x. **Knee Corollary**: under truncation profile ⟨ε_Q²⟩_H ≍ C(H)/Q² (slot-3 ensemble: C(H) ≈ S̄²_H·h_max/Q_max), Q* = √(C(H)·li_2(x)/(F²_H·S̄_H)) ≍ √x/log x. Empirical validation: 10⁷ predicted 196, knee 199-200; 10⁸ predicted 543, knee 599-1009. **Direct K↔h analogy (slot 4 §8)**: bilinear form Σ_{j,k>K}y^{ρ_j+ρ̄_k}/(ρ_j ρ̄_k) ↔ (1/N)Σ_{i,j∈[N]}r_∞(x,h_i)r_∞(x,h_j); diagonal "j=k → 1/|ρ_j|² sum" ↔ "i=j → r²_∞ average"; off-diagonal close-pair (Montgomery suppression) ↔ HLRH(c) cross-h decoherence; far-pair (RH-only Riemann-von Mangoldt) ↔ k-tuple HL for k=4. **Two structural weakenings** vs Thread 7: (i) F_H ensemble-dependent in [0.36, 0.70] vs Thread 7's universal F_GUE = 0.755 ± 0.06 across decades; (ii) HLRH (k-tuple HL + prime-pair-count joint distribution correlations) is less-studied than RH + Montgomery (the canonical analytic-NT conditional setting). **What this rules out**: (i) a Cramér-model F_H = 1 prediction for cross-h variance — empirically rejected at 0.36-0.70 across slot 2/3 cells; (ii) the hypothesis that cross-h decoherence is automatic from unbiasedness alone (HLRH(a) suffices without HLRH(c)) — the proof requires both for Q ≪ Q*. **What this does NOT close**: (i) HLRH itself (named hypothesis); (ii) pointwise (worst-case in h ∈ H) bound (half-Gaussian tail expected, √log N factor); (iii) decade-stability of F_H (slot-2 evidence shows ensemble-dependence); (iv) effective F_H constant from first principles (named ensemble-dependent constant); (v) k-tuple HL conjecture for k = 4. **Aggregate Thread 8 contribution**: polylog-time HL_∞(h, x) evaluator for h-batches, cross-h L²-typical error ε ≤ F_H·√(S̄_H·x)/log x conditional on HLRH(H); empirically verified across two decades (S419+S420); structurally parallel to Thread 7 / S244 wrap. **Cross-domain ingredient**: Goldston-Montgomery 1987 bilinear-form analysis transposed from K-axis to h-axis; technique already USED-T at S244, now USED-T on second axis (no new technique imported). | §RE-VERIFY E2.28 — Baker-Norine q-reduced form of D_P^N: was the S161 closure conservative? Specifically, does the divisor `D_Ω₁(n) = 1[Ω(n)=1]` (chips at primes AND prime powers `p^k`, `k ≥ 2`) — NOT tested in S161 — admit a q-reduction identity beyond S161's `{1} ∪ {primes}` support hypothesis? | REFINEMENT (B-grade case (i): two new identities S425.1 and S425.2 extend E2.28's scope; sharpened closure mechanism articulated as graph-topological depth-1 / leaf collection) | E (refines E2.28 inline) | S425 re-verify-closure adversarial probe of E2.28 (S161). **Probe**: `s425_inverse_chipfiring_probe.py` runs q-reduction on six divisors {D_P, D_uniform, D_sink_K=N, D_τ, D_μ², D_Ω₁} × {Γ_N, H_N} × N ∈ {16, 32, 64, 128} (48 runs). Companion `s425_verify_omega1_identity.py` extends to N=256 and prints full chip distribution. **Identity S425.1 (new)**: on H_N with sink q=1, q-reduce(D_Ω₁)·(1) = π(N) exactly. Σ_{v≠1} D'_Ω₁(v) = π(√N) + π(N^{1/3}) + π(N^{1/4}) + ⋯ = #{prime powers p^k : k ≥ 2, p^k ≤ N}. Verified at N ∈ {16, 32, 64, 128, 256} (5/5). Off-sink chips are scattered (NOT confined to prime powers themselves) but their TOTAL is the prime-power-k≥2 count by chip conservation. **Identity S425.2 (new)**: on Γ_N with sink q=1, q-reduce(D_Ω₁)·(1) = π(N) − π(N/2), identical to D_P. Verified at N ∈ {16, 32, 64, 128} (4/4). **Sharpened closure mechanism**: S161 said "building D_P^N requires knowing primes" (input-divisor-level closure). S425 sharpens to extraction-mechanism-level: on H_N, D'(1) reads off the *prime-restricted chip total* via depth-1 collection (q's depth-1 neighbours in H_N are exactly primes ≤ N; chips at depth ≥ 2 are graph-topologically blocked from single-step lending to q and contribute 0 to sink). On Γ_N, sink reads #{leaves of Γ_N with chips} = #{primes p : N/2 < p ≤ N}. **Decomposition lemma**: D_Ω₁ = D_P + D_higher (higher prime powers). q-reduce(D_higher)(1) = 0 because chips at p^k, k ≥ 2, are not adjacent to q in H_N. So q-reduce(D_Ω₁)(1) = q-reduce(D_P)(1) + q-reduce(D_higher)(1) = π(N) + 0 = π(N). **Why closure persists**: identifying H_N's depth-1 neighbours of q is polylog-equivalent to enumerating primes ≤ N; identifying Γ_N's leaves is polylog-equivalent to enumerating primes in (N/2, N]. Graph construction itself costs Ω(N) and includes prime detection. **What this rules out**: (i) D_Ω₁ as an algorithmic-novelty input that bypasses S161's "input requires primes" — BUILDING D_Ω₁ across [1, N] still costs Ω(N polylog) for prime-power testing; (ii) the hypothesis that S161's identity-class is restricted to {1} ∪ {primes} support — refuted by D_Ω₁; (iii) the hypothesis that chips at higher prime powers contribute non-zero to sink under q-reduction on H_N — directly tested, contributes exactly 0. **What this does NOT close**: (i) alternative reduction mechanisms (multi-sink reduction, weighted chip-firing) — outside slot scope; (ii) divisors supported on graph-topologically distinguished sets that DON'T coincide with primes — none currently known. **Verdict**: B-grade case (i) — refinement of E2.28 with precise new statements (S425.1 and S425.2) extending the scope of S161's generalised identity, plus sharpened closure mechanism (depth-1 / leaf collection). Closure stands. **Cites** E2.28 (refined inline), E2.29 (graph-topological closure analogue cross-reference), S161 (parent closure). Cross-domain technique: chip-firing on arithmetic graphs (Baker-Norine 2007 + Dhar 1990) — already USED-I via E2.28; this slot REFINES the scope without importing new technique. See `experiments/algebraic/baker_norine_chi_p/{s425_inverse_chipfiring_probe.py, s425_verify_omega1_identity.py, s425_inverse_chipfiring_results.md, s425_inverse_chipfiring_results.json}`, `archive/sessions/session425_reverify_e2_28.md`. | 425 |
| §P.P5 Galway worst-case K-constant tightening, slot 1 — empirical baseline. Build worst-case K-frontier evaluator at x ∈ {10⁴, 10⁴·⁵, 10⁵} with N=30 random samples per anchor in `[10^a, 1.5·10^a]`, K up to 8000 (project's pre-computed zero database); measure K_emp(x, ε, N=30) := min K such that worst-case |π_K(x_i) - π(x_i)| ≤ ε for ε ∈ {1, 3, 10, 30, 100}; compare to Galway-style explicit bound K = c·√x·log²x and to half-Gaussian-tail prediction K_pred(x, ε/√(2 log N)) under the S195 random-phase σ formula. | EMPIRICAL (B-PARTIAL-POSITIVE: first empirical worst-case K-constant for the explicit-formula evaluator; c_emp = 0.177 (10⁴), 0.210 (10⁴·⁵), >0.191 LB (10⁵); slowly drifting upward, slot 2 will resolve Galway-shape vs Thread-7-shape with a single decade-distinguishing measurement at x=10⁵·⁵; σ_eff/σ_pred = 0.796 ± 0.092 across 12 cells extends Thread 7 GUE factor 0.755 ± 0.06 to worst-case statistic) | E (E1.5 + Thread 5 / S224 + Thread 7 / S195+S240+S244 + Galway 2004 / Buthe 2018 — first empirical worst-case K-frontier measurement on the Galway-shape bound at finite x) | Thread 10 commit step 1/(≥3) (S434). Built `experiments/analytic/galway_constant/slot1_worst_case_K.py` (~280 lines): cumulative_err_trace (single-pass per-x R_at_rho accumulator at K-milestones [1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200, 300, 500, 700, 1000, 1500, 2000, 3000, 4000, 5000, 6000, 7000, 8000]) + sieve_pi (Eratosthenes ≤ 1.5×10⁵) + find_K_emp (smallest K with WORST/MEDIAN error ≤ ε across N samples) + sigma_predicted (S195 random-phase formula) + K_typical (binary search inversion) + K_galway_grh_bound (c·√x·log²x reference) + main driver. Re-uses Thread 7's polylog_approx_pi.py infrastructure (R_at_rho, riemann_R, get_zeros) at M=4 / dps=18 (3× speedup over Thread 7's M=8 / dps=25; accuracy verified via worst |err| ≤ 1.5 across 90 cells, well within 18-digit float precision). **Headline c_emp result**: at ε=1 worst-case across N=30 samples in [10^a, 1.5·10^a]: log10x=4.00 K_emp=1500 c_emp=0.177; log10x=4.50 K_emp=4000 c_emp=0.210; log10x=5.00 K_emp>8000 c_emp_LB>0.191. **σ_eff/σ_pred extension to worst-case statistic**: 12 (anchor, K) cells: 0.720 / 0.728 / 0.606 / 0.816 (10⁴ at K=100/1000/4000/8000); 0.685 / 0.780 / 0.878 / 0.818 (10⁴·⁵); 0.836 / 0.906 / 0.840 / 0.931 (10⁵). Mean 0.796 ± 0.092, consistent with Thread 7 S241/S244 0.755 ± 0.06. The half-Gaussian-tail prediction K_pred OVERSHOOTS empirical K_emp by factor 1.7-3.4× at the two reachable points; ratio K_emp/K_pred ≈ 0.49-0.60 matches the GUE-variance correction (σ_eff/σ_pred)² ≈ 0.61 — the half-Gaussian tail uses Gaussian variance but the actual variance is reduced by the GUE pair-correlation factor. **Hypothesis test (slot 2 falsifier)**: if Galway-shape `c_emp = const`, K_emp at x=10⁵·⁵ ≈ 0.21·562·160 ≈ 18900; if Thread-7-shape `c_emp ~ √x/log²x`, K_emp ≈ 10⁵·⁵·log N / (π² · log² x) · const ≈ 700. **The two predictions differ by 28×** — slot 2's measurement at x=10⁵·⁵ (requiring K up to ~25000 zeros) decisively distinguishes them. **What this rules out**: nothing structural — slot 1 is a baseline empirical measurement. The c_emp values (0.18-0.21) are 5-50× tighter than typical Galway-style published constants but the empirical-vs-published gap is uninformative without literature audit (slot 3 target). **What this does NOT close**: (i) Galway-shape vs Thread-7-shape resolution (slot 2 target); (ii) HKM/primecount benchmark comparison — at x=10⁴ K_emp=1500 with R_at_rho dps=18 evaluator gives 0.65 sec/π(x), HKM at same x gives <1ms; the explicit-formula evaluator is a theoretical object not currently competitive with primecount; (iii) literature audit of Galway 2004 / Buthe 2018 published constants for proper c_published reference (slot 3 target); (iv) tighter c_emp via larger N or different K-milestone density (slot 2/3 follow-on). **Six falsifiers** (F1: N-dependence not captured at N=100; F2: sample-window dependence at 10% vs 100%; F3: slot-2 reveals neither Galway-shape nor Thread-7-shape; F4: M=4/dps=18 truncation effect vs M=8/dps=25; F5: K_galway(c=1) reference normalisation choice; F6: K_emp non-monotonicity in K-milestone choice). **Cites** E1.5 (per-query bit-content; the explicit-formula partial sum approximates the ~50% random portion), Thread 5 / S224 (zeros-shared-database amortisation framing), Thread 7 / S195+S240+S241+S244 (σ_pred formula, GUE pair-correlation factor 0.755 ± 0.06, named-exponent corollary), E6.1 (Galway 2004 K = O(x^{1/2+ε}) — slot 1 measures the empirical worst-case constant for this bound at finite x). **Cross-domain ingredient**: no new technique; reuses Thread 7's σ_pred formula and R_at_rho evaluator. CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md unchanged. **Closure mode**: NOT a closure — slot 1 is positive-direction baseline measurement. **Next-action (slot 2)**: extend zeros database from K=8000 to K=25000-30000 via incremental mpmath.zetazero (~3h background compute); re-run slot1_worst_case_K.py at --anchors 5.0,5.5 --K_max 25000; update c_emp scaling table from 2 to 4 anchors; the K_emp(x=10^5.5) measurement decides Galway-shape vs Thread-7-shape. | §P.P4 batched-x k-tuple narrow-window count, slot 1 — empirical baseline. Build sieve-shared batched-x evaluator pi_H(x_i; w) = #{n in [x_i, x_i+w] : n+h prime for all h in H}; measure per-x amortised cost vs M for k in {2 (twin H=(0,2)), 3 (admissible H=(0,2,6)), 4 (admissible H=(0,2,6,8))} at x in {10^6, 10^7}; check whether the Thread-5 / S224 Correlation Dichotomy 33x speedup transposes to k-tuple counts. | EMPIRICAL (B-PARTIAL-POSITIVE: dichotomy direction transposes, magnitude smaller — 5x at 10^6 / 8x at 10^7 vs Thread-5's 33x — k-independent within +/-10% across k=2,3,4) | E (E1.5 + S224 + S418..S421 — first cross-x-axis experiment for narrow-window k-tuple counts in the project; positive direction) | Thread 9 commit step 1/(>=3) (S429). Built `experiments/analytic/k_tuple_batched/slot1_baseline.py`: naive_ktuples (per-x segmented sieve) + batched_ktuples (single sieve over [min(x), max(x)+w+h_max], walk M offsets) + singular_series (HL constant) + hl_estimate_window (linear-w HL approximation) + verify_small (sympy cross-check) + harness over (x_max in {10^6, 10^7}) x (k in {2, 3, 4}) x (distribution in {uncorrelated Uniform[x_max/2, x_max], corr_w=M consecutive integers, corr_w=polylog [x_anchor, x_anchor+log^2 x]}) x (M in {1, 4, 16, 64}). 72/72 cells counts_match=1. **Headline ratio T_batched/T_naive at M=64**: corr_w=M / corr_w=polylog rows are 0.188-0.213 at 10^6 (5x speedup) and 0.119-0.135 at 10^7 (8x speedup), k-independent within +/-10% across k=2,3,4. Uncorrelated rows are 9-49x (worse than naive — anti-amortising shared-sieve over Theta(x_max/2) range; structural finding, not bug; same as Thread 5's uncorrelated finding after fixing algorithm-side reduction to naive). **Why magnitude smaller than Thread 5's 33x**: per-query naive cost is O(sqrt(x) * log log x * w) segmented-sieve, NOT O(x^{2/3}) Lucy DP — amortisation ceiling is lower since baseline is already polylog-in-w. Speedup grows with x as predicted-and-confirmed sqrt(x) * log log x scaling across the 10^6 -> 10^7 measurement (extrapolation: ~25x at 10^9). **HL approximation polylog by construction, matches within 0.34 sigma_Pois at 6 cells**: HL_H(x;w) = C(H) * w / log^k(x) at (x_max, k) cells, mean |err/sigma_Pois| = 0.137 across 6 cells. Positive-direction polylog primitive on the k-tuple axis, analogous to Thread 7's R_K(x) and Thread 8's HL_inf(h, x). **What this rules out**: (i) strong H1 hypothesis "Thread-5-shape FAILS to transpose" — refuted, dichotomy transposes (REFINED to H1' bounded-by-sieve-baseline); (ii) hypothesis that speedup is x-independent within polylog regime — refuted, speedup grows with sqrt(x) / log scaling. **What this does NOT close**: (i) worst-case (pointwise) HL error bound — slot 2 target via cross-x ensemble; (ii) decade scaling beyond 10^7 — slot 2 target at 10^8 / 10^9; (iii) named-exponent error bound for HL_H(x; w) — slot 3+ target (cross-x analogue of Thread 8 HLRH); (iv) non-narrow windows w = x^delta. **Cites** E1.5 (cross-x analogue on k-tuple narrow-window axis), S195 (random-phase frame), S224 (Correlation Dichotomy template), S418 / S421 (Thread 8 EXACT/APPROX dichotomy + HL evaluator template transposed h-axis -> x-axis), Hardy-Littlewood 1923 k-tuple conjecture (singular-series formula), Tarjan 1985 amortised-analysis decomposition (T_per_x_amort = T_anchor/M + per-query floor — USED final at S224). **Cross-domain ingredient**: no new technique; reuses Thread-5 amortised-analysis decomposition. **Slot 2 recommendation** (highest yield): cross-x HL residual distribution analysis at fixed admissible H, varying x in narrow window — analogue of Thread 8 slot 2's cross-h ensemble at fixed x. Targets: half-Gaussian KS shape, sigma_eff/sigma_pois suppression factor, decade-stability check; building toward Thread-8-shape conditional named-exponent theorem on cross-x k-tuple axis at slot 4/5. See `experiments/analytic/k_tuple_batched/{slot1_baseline.py, slot1_baseline.csv (72 rows), slot1_hl_compare.csv (6 rows), slot1_run.log, slot1_baseline_results.md}`, `archive/sessions/session429_commit_p4_baseline.md`. | 429 |

Captures only 32%. **Cross-prime + boundary primes account for 68%** (analytic derivation incomplete; slot 5 GY 2007 target). **Hypothesis status**: H11 (slot 3 bug) CONFIRMED via HL prime quadruplet constant; H12 (slot 3 α=5.72 closed-form) REFUTED by extension; H13 (Δ ~ -12 C₂² w log w log log w) CONFIRMED to 2.4% via 3-param fit, slow asymptotic convergence; H14 (single-prime captures full magnitude) REFUTED, only 32%. **What this closes (slot 4)**: the slot 3 narrative "α ≈ 5.72 closed-form, slot 4 will rigorise α=24C₂²/log(6)=5.838" is closed AS REFUTED — the linear-in-log-w model is incompatible with the asymptotic. **What this does NOT close**: (i) rigorous derivation of -12 C₂² coefficient (slot 5 GY target); (ii) the remaining 68% from cross-prime/boundary contributions; (iii) Goldston-Montgomery zero residual (slot 5 alternative); (iv) cross-H universality. **Cites** E1.5 (cross-x analogue), Hardy-Littlewood 1923 (k-tuple), Hardy-Littlewood prime quadruplet constant (Brent 1975, Forbes 1998 = 4.15118 — slot 4 bug detection), Mertens 1874 (single-prime heuristic), Gallagher 1976 (slot 3 Theorem 1, refined to log·log log rate of approach), Goldston-Yıldırım 2007 (slot 5 derivation candidate), Goldston-Montgomery 1987 (slot 5 alternative), S429/S430/S431 (Thread 9 prior slots). **Cross-domain ingredient**: Selberg-Delange partial-sum machinery for multiplicative arithmetic functions — added to CROSS_DOMAIN_TECHNIQUES.md as PROPOSED for slot 5 use. **Closure mode**: REFINEMENT (B-grade ambitious-failure: slot 3's narrative refuted; new structural candidate identified; single-prime mechanism explains 32% of magnitude with cross-prime accounting for the rest, derivation incomplete). **Next-action (slot 5 FINAL)**: rigorous GY 2007 derivation of -12 C₂² log·log log coefficient OR Goldston-Montgomery zero residual analysis OR Thread 9 wrap synthesizing the 5-slot arc as partial-positive Correlation-Dichotomy-shape result.
